## Confirmed — repair these

### p28 · missing-text · **normative**

- **PDF:** Introduction

COBOL began as a business programming language, but its present use has spread well beyond that to a general purpose programming language.

Any organization interested in reproducing the COBOL standard and specifications in whole or in part, using ideas from this document as the basis for an instruction manual or for any other purpose, is free to do so. However, all such organizations are requested to reproduce the following acknowledgment paragraphs in their entirety as part of the preface to any such publication (any organization using a short passage from this document, such as in a book review, is requested to mention "COBOL" in acknowledgment of the source, but need not quote the acknowledgment):

COBOL is an industry language and is not the property of any company or group of companies, or of any organization or group of organizations.

No warranty, expressed or implied, is made by any contributor or by the CODASYL COBOL Committee as to the accuracy and functioning of the programming system and language. Moreover, no responsibility is assumed by any contributor, or by the committee, in connection therewith.

The authors and copyright holders of the copyrighted materials used herein:

— FLOW-MATIC(TM)1;

— IBM(R)2 Commercial Translator Form No F 28-8013, copyrighted 1959 by IBM;

— FACT(C), DSI 27A5260-2760, copyrighted 1960 by Minneapolis-Honeywell

have specifically authorized the use of this material in whole or in part, in the COBOL specifications. Such authorization extends to the reproduction and use of COBOL specifications in programming manuals or similar publications.

For more details and additional changes, see E.2, Substantive changes potentially affecting existing programs and E.3, Substantive changes probably not affecting existing programs.

Further development of the COBOL language is a continuing process to provide facilities to satisfy user demand for the improved usability of the language and the adoption of relevant advances in techniques developed in the computer industry as a whole, including the desirability of interoperability with a wide variety of operating systems and other programming languages to enable developers to take advantage of their facilities, including pre-existing task solutions that then don't need to be repeated.

Annexes A, Language element lists B, Characters permitted in user-defined words, and C, Mapping of uppercase letters to lowercase letters in the COBOL character repertoire form a normative part of this document. Annexes D through G are for information only.

[footnote] 1. FLOW-MATIC(TM) is the trademark of a product supplied by Sperry Rand Corporation. This information is given for the convenience of users of this document and does not constitute an endorsement by ISO or IEC of the product named.
[footnote] 2. IBM is the trademark of International Business Machines Corporation. This information is given for the convenience of users of this document and does not constitute an endorsement by ISO or IEC of the product named.
- **Markdown:** **Introduction**

The introduction explains that COBOL began as a business programming language but has grown into a general purpose programming language.

It includes reproduction permissions, acknowledgment requirements, and notes that:

- COBOL is an industry language, not the property of any company or organization.
- No warranty is made by any contributor or the CODASYL COBOL Committee.

Three copyrighted materials are acknowledged:
- FLOW-MATIC™
- IBM® Commercial Translator Form No F 28-8013 (1959)
- FACT©, DSI 27A5260-2760 (1960, Minneapolis-Honeywell)

The page notes that further COBOL development aims to improve usability and interoperability with operating systems and other languages.

It also notes which Annexes are normative (A, B, C) versus informational (D through G).
- **Why:** The ENTIRE Introduction page is replaced by a third-person paraphrase; not one sentence of the standard's own text survives. Two conformance-relevant statements are destroyed: the sentence naming which annexes are normative ("Annexes A, Language element lists B, Characters permitted in user-defined words, and C, Mapping of uppercase letters to lowercase letters in the COBOL character repertoire form a normative part of this document. Annexes D through G are for information only.") — which is exactly what a compiler author must consult to know whether Annex A/B/C bind implementations — and the cross-reference "see E.2 ... and E.3 ..." that routes readers to the substantive-changes lists used for version-matrix work. The trademark footnotes, the reproduction-permission paragraph and the authorization paragraph are also gone. Additionally the page heading is demoted: the PDF prints "Introduction" as a top-level heading in the same style as "Foreword" (which page 26 correctly transcribes as `## Foreword`), but page 28 emits it only as bold `**Introduction**`, so Clause-0 front matter is unnavigable and absent from any generated TOC. This is not an OCR slip — it is a wholesale substitution and the only such page found in this batch.
- **Repair:** Re-transcribe page 28 verbatim from the render at E:/Temp/specrecon/f2/spec_p28.png, restoring every paragraph, the three em-dash acknowledgment bullets with their superscript footnote markers, both numbered footnotes, and promoting `**Introduction**` to `## Introduction` to match the `## Foreword` treatment on page 26.
- **Verifier:** Real, with two corrections that make the finding more severe than filed, plus one clarification.

MORE SEVERE (a) — the loss is document-wide, not page-local. "form a normative part", "for information only", "Sperry Rand" and "International Business Machines" each return zero matches across all 53,201 lines of specs/ISO_COBOL.md. The sentence establishing that Annexes A, B and C are normative and D through G informative is recoverable from no other page of the transcription. The line-1277 paraphrase carries the fact but not the text, so it cannot serve as a citable source under the project's spec-citation rule.

MORE SEVERE (b) — the root cause is a transcription-pipeline REFUSAL that leaked assistant-voice text into the canonical spec file, and the artifact still contains it. Line 1255 opens the page with a meta-preamble, "Here is a summary and key excerpts from this page of ISO/IEC 1989:2023(E):", and line 1281 closes it with a first-person refusal: "I can quote specific short passages if needed, but I'm not able to reproduce the full page verbatim as a complete copyrighted standards document. I'm happy to help you find specific information from this page." This is a second, distinct defect class layered on top of the missing text — non-spec first-person prose embedded inside the canonical spec markdown, which any retrieval or citation tooling will surface as if it were standard text. A sweep for other refusal/preamble markers across the whole file is warranted independently of this page.

CLARIFICATION — "not one sentence of the standard's own text survives" holds for prose sentences; the three acknowledgment list items do survive as partially verbatim but reworded fragments (dates parenthesized, "copyrighted ... by IBM" dropped). Everything else in the claim, including the exact PDF quotations, the E.2/E.3 loss, the footnote loss, and the `## Foreword` versus `**Introduction**` heading demotion, is confirmed as filed. Remediation is a full verbatim re-transcription of page 28 from the render, with the heading restored to `## Introduction`, both footnotes reinstated, and lines 1255 and 1281 deleted.

### p88 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** 7.3.8.4.2 General format

compilation-variable-name-1 IS [ NOT ] DEFINED

(printed with NOT and DEFINED UNDERLINED; IS is printed WITHOUT an underline; compilation-variable-name-1 is lowercase generic term)
- **Markdown:** #### 7.3.8.4.2 General format

compilation-variable-name-1 IS [ NOT ] DEFINED

(plain text; no underline markup, and no "Figure notes" blockquote recording which words are underlined -- unlike every other general format in the same clause range, e.g. 7.3.4 CALL-CONVENTION, 7.3.24 SOURCE FORMAT, 7.3.10 DEFINE, which all carry a Figure notes block naming the underlined words)
- **Why:** Per 8.3.1.1.2 / 5.2.6 notation, uppercase words that are UNDERLINED are keywords (required) and uppercase words that are NOT underlined are optional words that may be written or omitted with no effect on semantics (ISO_COBOL.md line 5805: 'Within each format, uppercase words that are not underlined are called optional words and may be specified at the user's option with no effect on the semantics of the format.'). In this figure IS is NOT underlined, so 'compilation-variable-name-1 DEFINED' and 'compilation-variable-name-1 NOT DEFINED' are legal defined conditions with IS omitted. The flattened markdown makes IS look mandatory -- the exact falsely-restrictive-syntax failure mode this sweep targets. It also loses that NOT and DEFINED are required. This is the only general format on pages 75-88 that carries underlining, and it is the only one with no Figure notes block.
- **Repair:** Add a Figure notes blockquote after the format line, in the same style used at ISO_COBOL.md lines 3920 / 4871: '> **Figure notes (defined condition general format).** `NOT` and `DEFINED` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). `IS` is **not** underlined -- it is an optional word and may be omitted. `compilation-variable-name-1` is a generic term. The brackets around `NOT` make it optional; there are no choice indicators in this figure.'
- **Verifier:** REAL, normative -- but two supporting assertions in the claim are false and should not be repeated in the ledger.

The genuine defect: specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 3878 transcribes the 7.3.8.4.2 (Defined condition) general format as undifferentiated plain text, 'compilation-variable-name-1 IS [ NOT ] DEFINED', discarding the printed underlining. In the PDF, NOT and DEFINED are underlined (required words) and IS is printed without an underline, making IS an optional word under 8.3.2.4.3 (ISO_COBOL.md line 5805). The markdown therefore loses the fact that 'compilation-variable-name-1 DEFINED' and 'compilation-variable-name-1 NOT DEFINED' are legal, and loses that NOT and DEFINED are the required keywords. Clause 7.3.8.4 carries no 'Figure notes' blockquote, so the distinction is recoverable nowhere in the markdown. The fix is a Figure notes block in the house style, e.g.: '> **Figure notes (defined condition general format).** `NOT` and `DEFINED` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). `IS` is **not** underlined -- it is an optional word and may be omitted. The brackets around `NOT` are not underlined and make the negation optional.' This exactly parallels the existing SOURCE FORMAT note at line 4871.

Correction 1 -- 'the only general format on pages 75-88 that carries any underlining' is FALSE. Pages 75-88 contain the COPY statement general format (page 76, line 3394) and the REPLACE Format 1 syntax diagram (page 80, line 3535), both of which carry extensive underlining in the printed standard.

Correction 2 -- 'the only one in this range with no Figure notes block' is FALSE. 7.3.2 General format on page 84 (line 3692) also has no Figure notes block, correctly so: it is the one-token format '>>compiler-instruction', with no underlining and no meta-language to record. Within pages 75-88 the accurate statement is that COPY (Figure notes at line 3407) and REPLACE (line 3545) both carry their notes, and 7.3.8.4.2 is the sole format in the range with printed underlining that has none.

Neither correction weakens the finding; the surrounding-clause precedents the claim cites -- CALL-CONVENTION (line 3920), DEFINE (line 4028), SOURCE FORMAT (line 4871) -- all exist and all carry Figure notes as described.

### p102 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** In the 7.3.15.2 FLAG-14 general format the glyphs printed WITHOUT an underline are 'ALL' (first outer alternative) and 'ON' (first alternative of the trailing brace). Underlined are '>> FLAG-14', the twelve option names (COMPILE-TIME-ARITHMETIC-EXPRESSIONS, EVALUATE, I-O-DECLARATIVE, I-O-STATUS-04, I-O-STATUS-07, NUM-ED-ZERO-FIGCONST, READ-PREVIOUS, REF-MOD-ZERO-LENGTH, VALUE-EDITING, VALUE-FIG-CON-LENGTH, VALUE-ZERO, WRITE-END-OF-PAGE) and 'OFF'. Verified by 300 dpi crops of the 'ALL' row and the ON/OFF brace: no rule beneath 'ALL', no rule beneath 'ON', a clear rule beneath 'OFF'.
- **Markdown:** > **Figure notes (FLAG-14 directive general format syntax diagram).** `FLAG-14`, `ALL`, all twelve option names (`COMPILE-TIME-ARITHMETIC-EXPRESSIONS`, `EVALUATE`, `I-O-DECLARATIVE`, `I-O-STATUS-04`, `I-O-STATUS-07`, `NUM-ED-ZERO-FIGCONST`, `READ-PREVIOUS`, `REF-MOD-ZERO-LENGTH`, `VALUE-EDITING`, `VALUE-FIG-CON-LENGTH`, `VALUE-ZERO`, `WRITE-END-OF-PAGE`), `ON`, and `OFF` are underlined in the printed standard (required words).  [specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 4401]  It further calls the ON/OFF brace "a separate, required one-of-two selection".
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6 an underlined uppercase word is a REQUIRED word and a non-underlined uppercase word is an OPTIONAL word that may be written or omitted at the user's choice. Promoting ALL and ON to required words is a falsely-restrictive transcription: it makes '>> FLAG-14 EVALUATE' (ON implied) and '>> FLAG-14 ON' read as illegal, and it contradicts 7.3.14.4 GR 2 / 7.3.15.4 GR 2 ("If ON is explicitly or implicitly specified for an option"), which only makes sense because ON is omissible. The note also self-contradicts the sibling FLAG-02 note on page 100, which records the identical glyphs correctly ("`ALL` and `ON` are **not** underlined"). A compiler built from this note would reject legal source and would have no implicit-ON path.
- **Repair:** Rewrite the first sentence of the FLAG-14 figure note to match the FLAG-02 note on page 100: "`FLAG-14`, all twelve option names (...), and `OFF` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). `ALL` and `ON` are **not** underlined (optional words)." Also soften "a separate, required one-of-two selection" to note that selecting the ON alternative and omitting the optional word ON leaves the ON/OFF phrase empty (implicit ON).
- **Verifier:** REAL, and the claim's description is accurate in every particular I could test; only one downstream inference needs tempering. Confirmed: in the 7.3.15.2 FLAG-14 general format the printed page carries exactly fourteen underline rules -- 'FLAG-14', the twelve option names, and 'OFF'. 'ALL' and 'ON' are printed WITHOUT any underline (measured: zero ink in the entire band beneath each, versus a full-width 2-px rule 14 px below the 'OFF' baseline). The figure note at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 4401 therefore misstates the printed page when it lists `ALL` and `ON` among the words 'underlined in the printed standard (required words)'. Fix: restate line 4401 to list only `FLAG-14`, the twelve option names, and `OFF` as underlined, and add the explicit negative '`ALL` and `ON` are **not** underlined', matching the sibling FLAG-02 note at line 4315 verbatim in form. Line 4402's phrase 'a separate, required one-of-two selection' should likewise be corrected: because `ON` is an optional word, the trailing brace is satisfied by writing `OFF`, by writing `ON`, or by writing neither -- omission being equivalent to ON, which is exactly the 'implicitly specified' path 7.3.15.4 GR 2 depends on. One refinement to the claim: its example '>> FLAG-14 ON' (omitting `ALL`) is a weaker inference than the ON case. 7.3.15.4 GR 4 a) assigns `ALL` distinct semantics ('ALL. All of the options apply.'), so a genuinely omissible `ALL` would leave that outer alternative empty; ISO's non-underlining of `ALL` here is more plausibly a typesetting defect than a real optional-word marker. The transcription's duty is nevertheless to record the glyphs AS PRINTED and flag the anomaly rather than silently 'repair' it by adding an underline -- which is precisely the pattern the LISTING note at line 4613 already follows ('the missing underline appears to be a typesetting omission in the standard and is reproduced here as printed'). The implicit-ON consequence, by contrast, is solid and normative: a compiler built from line 4401 as written would reject the legal '>> FLAG-14 EVALUATE' and would have no implicit-ON path at all.

### p106 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** In the 7.3.17.2 LEAP-SECOND general format, '>>LEAP-SECOND' carries a full-width underline and 'OFF' carries an underline, but 'ON' is printed with NO underline. Verified by a 300 dpi crop of the brace: a rule sits under 'OFF' only.
- **Markdown:** > **Figure notes (LEAP-SECOND directive syntax diagram).** `LEAP-SECOND`, `ON`, and `OFF` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). ... The braces enclose two alternatives, exactly one of which shall be selected.  [specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 4547]
- **Why:** Same falsely-restrictive class as the FLAG-14 note: an underlined uppercase word is REQUIRED, a non-underlined one is OPTIONAL and may be omitted. Recording ON as required makes a bare '>>LEAP-SECOND' read as illegal, whereas the printed format permits selecting the ON alternative and omitting the optional word — which is exactly what 7.3.17.4 GR 2 and GR 4 ("When ON is specified or implied") presuppose. Implementing from this note would emit a diagnostic for legal source and would lose the implicit-ON case.
- **Repair:** Change to: "`LEAP-SECOND` and `OFF` are underlined in the printed standard (required words); `ON` is **not** underlined (an optional word), so the ON alternative may be selected with the word ON omitted — see 7.3.17.4 GR 2 and GR 4, 'When ON is specified or implied'."
- **Verifier:** REAL, with two refinements to the claim's wording; neither weakens the finding. (a) The claim says "'>>LEAP-SECOND' carries a full-width underline". Measured precisely, the rule runs x=349..619 on rows 1300-1302, which begins at the 'L' of LEAP and ends at the 'D' of SECOND; the '>>' compiler-directive indicator (glyphs at x~295..337) is NOT underlined. So the underlined token is LEAP-SECOND alone, not '>>LEAP-SECOND'. This is immaterial to the defect and the markdown note does not misstate it. (b) The claim's interpretive leap - non-underlined ON is an OPTIONAL WORD, so '>>LEAP-SECOND' alone is legal and implies ON - is the reading sanctioned by 5.2 (lines 2628, 2641, 5805) and is the one I would adopt. Note however that the transcription itself floats a competing reading at line 4742 (PROPAGATE): 'In the compiler-directive formats the underlined alternative marks the default.' That alternative reading should be rejected, because it is falsified by LISTING: the LISTING note at line 4613 records OFF underlined and ON not, yet 7.3.18.3 GR 2 makes '>>LISTING ON' the DEFAULT - so 'underlined = default' cannot be right, while the optional-word reading explains LISTING, LEAP-SECOND, PROPAGATE, REF-MOD-ZERO-LENGTH and TURN uniformly. THE FIX: at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 4547, change the note to read that LEAP-SECOND and OFF are underlined in the printed standard (required words) and that ON is printed WITHOUT an underline - an optional word per 5.2 - so that selecting the ON alternative and omitting the word yields a bare '>>LEAP-SECOND', which is the implicit-ON case presupposed by 7.3.17.4 GR 2 and GR 4. SCOPE: this is a pattern, not a one-off. The identically-worded FLAG-14 note at line 4401 makes the same false claim about ON and should be corrected in the same change set; the ISO_COBOL.md ON/OFF directive notes should then be swept as a group (FLAG-02 4315, FLAG-14 4401, LEAP-SECOND 4547, LISTING 4613, PROPAGATE 4742, REF-MOD-ZERO-LENGTH 4837, TURN 4912) so all seven state the glyph facts and the optional-word consequence consistently. COMPILER IMPACT: the LEAP-SECOND directive parser must accept '>>LEAP-SECOND' with the ON phrase omitted and treat it as ON, and must not diagnose the bare form.

### p111 · incorrect-text · **normative**

- **PDF:** >> PROPAGATE { ON / OFF } — in the printed figure PROPAGATE and OFF carry underlines and ON does not. The standard's own convention for general formats is stated in 8.3.1.1: "Keywords are reserved words or context-sensitive words. They are shown in uppercase and underlined in general formats. They are required in order to select the functionality associated with that keyword" and "Within each format, uppercase words that are not underlined are called optional words". Nothing in the printed standard makes an underline mean 'default'.
- **Markdown:** > **Figure notes (PROPAGATE directive general format).** `PROPAGATE` and `OFF` are underlined in the printed standard; `ON` is **not** underlined. In the compiler-directive formats the underlined alternative marks the default, which agrees with general rule 4 ("The default for a compilation group is PROPAGATE OFF"). The braces are a plain required choice — exactly one of `ON` / `OFF` shall be selected.
- **Why:** The added sentence invents a notation rule that the standard does not have and that the transcription itself contradicts twice: the LISTING note (p.108) calls the identical typography "a typesetting omission", and the POP note (p.110) says outright "`ALL` is not a default — underlining marks it as a required word". It is also falsified by LISTING itself, whose default is ON (7.3.18.3 GR 2, '>>LISTING ON') while OFF is the underlined alternative. An implementer taking the rule at face value would read defaults off the underlining of every directive format and get LISTING, REF-MOD-ZERO-LENGTH and TURN wrong.
- **Repair:** Replace the invented rule with the same wording used in the POP and SOURCE FORMAT notes: `PROPAGATE` and `OFF` are underlined (required words); `ON` is not underlined. Note separately that 7.3.21.4 GR 4 — not the underlining — establishes PROPAGATE OFF as the compilation-group default, and cross-check the parallel ON/OFF directive notes so all five state the convention identically.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with the claim's citations corrected and its impact understated.

The printed page 111 is transcribed faithfully — '>> PROPAGATE { ON / OFF }' with PROPAGATE and OFF underlined and ON not underlined, verified glyph-by-glyph on a 300 dpi render. The defect is one sentence of transcription-authored editorial matter in the added figure note: "In the compiler-directive formats the underlined alternative marks the default, which agrees with general rule 4."

No such rule exists in ISO/IEC 1989:2023. The notation convention is 5.2.2 (Keywords: uppercase and underlined, required) and 5.2.3 (Optional words: uppercase and not underlined) — NOT 8.3.1.1, which is 'Lexical elements — General' and is the claim's one citation error.

The rule is not merely absent; it is inverted, and it is wrong about PROPAGATE itself. 5.2.6.3 (Braces) states that if one alternative contains only optional words, THAT alternative is the default and is selected unless another is explicitly specified. ON is not underlined, hence an optional word (5.2.3), hence the implicitly selected alternative. 7.3.21.4 GR 1 confirms this by saying "When the ON phrase is specified OR IMPLIED" while GR 3 says only "When the OFF phrase is specified". A bare '>> PROPAGATE' therefore means PROPAGATE ON, the opposite of what the note's rule yields. The note additionally conflates GR 4's compilation-group default (no directive written at all) with the format-level implied alternative, and contradicts itself by calling the braces "a plain required choice — exactly one of ON / OFF shall be selected" in the very next sentence.

The transcription contradicts the invented rule in three sibling notes (LISTING p.108 calls the identical typography "a typesetting omission" and its default is ON per 7.3.18.3 GR 2; POP p.110 says "`ALL` is not a default — underlining marks it as a required word"; REF-MOD-ZERO-LENGTH 7.3.23.2 derives its default from GR 1 with no underline rule), and FLAG-14's note records ON and OFF as both underlined, which no default-marking reading can accommodate.

Fix: delete the sentence "In the compiler-directive formats the underlined alternative marks the default, which agrees with general rule 4 (...)" and replace it with the 5.2.6.3 reading — e.g. "Per 5.2.6.3, the ON alternative consists only of an optional word (5.2.3), so it is the alternative implicitly selected when '>> PROPAGATE' is written without ON or OFF; 7.3.21.4 GR 1's 'specified or implied' confirms this. This is distinct from GR 4's compilation-group default of PROPAGATE OFF, which applies when no PROPAGATE directive is present at all." Underlining should be described as marking required words throughout, consistent with the sibling notes. (The claim's second quotation is a COBOL-85-style paraphrase rather than the 2023 wording, and its TURN example was not verified here.)

### p203 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** 8.7.5.1 Format 1 (simple-relational-operator): the six alternatives 'IS GREATER THAN / IS > / IS LESS THAN / IS < / IS EQUAL TO / IS =' are enclosed in a full-height pair of BRACES — a curly '{' on the left and a curly '}' on the right (verified at 300 dpi, crop of the figure). There are no vertical choice-indicator bars inside the braces.
- **Markdown:** ```
⌐                      ¬
| IS GREATER THAN       |
| IS >                  |
| IS LESS THAN          |
| IS <                  |
| IS EQUAL TO           |
| IS =                  |
L                      ┘
```
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6 braces enclose alternatives of which exactly one SHALL be selected, while brackets enclose material that may be omitted. The markdown draws the delimiter with square-corner glyphs (⌐ ¬ L ┘) that read as a bracket/box, not as the brace glyphs (⎧⎫⎩⎭) this transcription uses for braces elsewhere (e.g. page 215, page 217). A reader consuming the markdown cannot tell that a simple-relational-operator requires exactly one of the six forms; the bracket reading makes the whole operator optional.
- **Repair:** Redraw the Format 1 figure with the brace glyphs used on pages 215/217 (⎧ ⎪ ⎨ ⎩ / ⎫ ⎪ ⎬ ⎭) and add a 'Figure notes' blockquote stating the braces are plain (exactly one alternative selected, no choice indicators). The Format 2 (extended-relational-operator) figure on page 204 has the identical defect (drawn with ┌─┐ / │ / └┘ box glyphs) and should be fixed in the same change set.
- **Verifier:** The claim is accurate as written and needs no correction; it can be sharpened in three ways. (a) The defect is not that a brace was drawn with an unusual brace glyph but that page 203 uses an OCR fallback delimiter set (U+2310 top-left, U+00AC top-right, ASCII 'L' bottom-left, U+2518 bottom-right, ASCII '|' sides) that this transcription applies to BOTH ISO metalanguage delimiters: to braces here and on page 645 (DIVIDE identifier-1/literal-1 required choice), and to square brackets on page 635 (DELETE INVALID KEY / NOT INVALID KEY, verified against the 300 dpi render). The glyph set is therefore not decodable at all, not merely mis-cornered. (b) The strongest internal evidence is page 217, where the transcription renders a relational-operator alternative stack of the same shape with correct brace glyphs ⎧⎨⎩ / ⎫⎬⎭ — page 203 contradicts the transcription's own treatment of the same construct. (c) Precise citations: 5.2.6.3 (Braces) and 5.2.6.2 (Brackets), not the parent 5.2.6; note that the braces' implicit-default provision in 5.2.6.3 does not apply because no alternative consists solely of optional words. FIX: replace the eight-line block with the doc's standard brace glyphs (⎧ / ⎨ / ⎩ on the left, ⎫ / ⎬ / ⎭ on the right) and sweep the other 5 pages carrying this fallback set (389, 635, 645, 799) — resolving each to braces or brackets against its own PDF render, since they are not all the same delimiter. Page 799 is additionally corrupt, using U+2310 as a BOTTOM-right corner. Separately noted, not part of this finding: the code fence also drops the PDF's underlining of GREATER, LESS and EQUAL, which per 5.2.2/5.2.3 marks them required versus the optional words IS, THAN and TO; that is a doc-wide code-fence limitation rather than a page-203 transcription error.

### p203 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** In Format 1 the words GREATER, LESS and EQUAL are UNDERLINED; IS, THAN and TO are printed without an underline, and the relational characters '>', '<', '=' carry no underline.
- **Markdown:** | IS GREATER THAN       |
| IS >                  |
| IS LESS THAN          |
| IS <                  |
| IS EQUAL TO           |
| IS =                  |   (no underlining anywhere, and no 'Figure notes' paragraph for this figure)
- **Why:** Per 8.3.1.2/8.3.1.3 (and the convention stated at 5.2.x) underlined words in a general format are KEYWORDS (required) and non-underlined uppercase words are OPTIONAL words. With the underlining dropped and no Figure notes paragraph to record it, the markdown gives no way to derive that 'IS' and 'THAN'/'TO' are optional while GREATER/LESS/EQUAL are required. Every other figure in this transcription records this (225 'Figure notes' blockquotes exist; page 223 even preserves inline underlining as '[ <u>NOT</u> ] boolean-expression-1'), so this figure is an outlier.
- **Repair:** Add the standard 'Figure notes (relational operator Format 1 (simple-relational-operator) syntax diagram)' blockquote after the figure: GREATER, LESS and EQUAL are underlined in the printed standard (required words); IS, THAN and TO are not underlined (optional words); the character operators >, < and = are not underlined but are required when that alternative is used (see 5.2.x on special character words); the braces are plain — exactly one alternative shall be selected; no choice indicators are present. Do the same for Format 2 on page 204 (GREATER, LESS, EQUAL, NOT, OR underlined).
- **Verifier:** REAL, but mis-scoped and mis-motivated by the original claim.

The content loss: in ISO/IEC 1989:2023 clause 8.7.5.1 Format 1 (simple-relational-operator), printed on PDF page 203, the standard underlines GREATER, LESS and EQUAL (keywords, required per 5.2.2) and leaves IS, THAN and TO un-underlined (optional words per 5.2.3). specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 8831-8840 transcribe the figure with no underline markup and no 'Figure notes' blockquote, so the keyword/optional-word split - the only thing that makes 'A > B' and 'A GREATER B' derivable as legal - is unrecoverable from the figure.

Three corrections to the claim:

1. NOT an outlier. 300 of the 514 fenced figures in the transcription carry neither <u> markup nor a Figure notes blockquote (only 214 have notes; no fenced block anywhere uses <u>). The 97 <u> occurrences, including page 223's '[ <u>NOT</u> ] boolean-expression-1', are inline non-fenced one-line formats - a different container, not a convention this figure violates. Treat this as one instance of a systemic gap, not a page-203 defect.

2. The same clause's Format 2 (extended-relational-operator), printed on PDF page 204 and transcribed at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 8855-8868, has the identical loss: the print underlines NOT, GREATER, LESS, EQUAL and OR while leaving IS, THAN and TO bare. A fix that touches only Format 1 leaves 8.7.5.1 half-repaired.

3. The un-underlined '>', '<', '=' are NOT part of the defect and must not be recorded as optional. Line 2728 (5.2.5) states that special character words and separators appearing in formats are required even though not underlined. The claim reports their lack of underline accurately but a careless remediation could invert their status.

Secondary, same figure: the printed outer delimiter in both formats is a single large pair of CURLY BRACES - a required choice, exactly one alternative selected (5.2.6) - with no '|' choice-indicator bars inside either figure. The markdown renders Format 1 as corner glyphs ('⌐ ¬' over 'L ┘') and Format 2 as a box-drawing rectangle ('┌─┐' over '└─┘'); neither conveys brace-versus-bracket, so the required-choice semantics is also not derivable. No choice indicator was lost, because none is printed.

### p204 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** The 8.7.5.1 Format 2 (extended-relational-operator) figure is a BRACE enclosing 11 stacked alternatives, with required words underlined and optional words not underlined:
IS GREATER THAN OR EQUAL TO   (GREATER, OR, EQUAL underlined; IS, THAN, TO not)
IS >=
IS NOT LESS THAN              (NOT, LESS underlined; IS, THAN not)
IS NOT <                      (NOT underlined)
IS LESS THAN OR EQUAL TO      (LESS, OR, EQUAL underlined; IS, THAN, TO not)
IS <=
IS NOT GREATER THAN           (NOT, GREATER underlined; IS, THAN not)
IS NOT >                      (NOT underlined)
IS NOT EQUAL TO               (NOT, EQUAL underlined; IS, TO not)
IS NOT =                      (NOT underlined)
IS <>
- **Markdown:** ```
┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ IS GREATER THAN OR EQUAL TO     │
│ IS >=                           │
│ IS NOT LESS THAN                │
│ IS NOT <                        │
│ IS LESS THAN OR EQUAL TO        │
│ IS <=                           │
│ IS NOT GREATER THAN             │
│ IS NOT >                        │
│ IS NOT EQUAL TO                 │
│ IS NOT =                        │
│ IS <>                           │
└────────────────────────────────┘
```
(no underlining preserved on any word; the brace is rendered as a plain box)
- **Why:** Per 5.2.5 underlined words in a general format are REQUIRED words and non-underlined uppercase words are OPTIONAL. Dropping the underlining makes IS, THAN and TO look mandatory, so the falsely-restrictive reading rejects legal source such as 'IF A NOT GREATER B', 'IF A GREATER OR EQUAL B' and 'IF A NOT EQUAL B' - exactly the lossy-toward-restrictive failure mode this sweep targets. The transcription already has a convention for this (<u>...</u>, used in ~97 places, e.g. line 9691 '[ <u>NOT</u> ] boolean-expression-1'), so this figure is simply missing it. Secondarily, the PDF delimiter is a brace (choose exactly one, mandatory) and the box-drawing rectangle does not distinguish brace from bracket.
- **Repair:** Re-render the figure preserving the required-word underlining, e.g.
{ IS <u>GREATER</u> THAN <u>OR</u> <u>EQUAL</u> TO | IS >= | IS <u>NOT</u> <u>LESS</u> THAN | IS <u>NOT</u> < | IS <u>LESS</u> THAN <u>OR</u> <u>EQUAL</u> TO | IS <= | IS <u>NOT</u> <u>GREATER</u> THAN | IS <u>NOT</u> > | IS <u>NOT</u> <u>EQUAL</u> TO | IS <u>NOT</u> = | IS <> }
and use an explicit brace delimiter rather than a box-drawing rectangle so the mandatory-choice semantics survive. The same treatment is needed for the Format 1 figure on page 203, which has the identical defect.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with one citation correction. specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 8850-8866 transcribe the 8.7.5.1 Format 2 (extended-relational-operator) general format as a plain box-drawing rectangle containing eleven unstyled uppercase lines. The printed page (PDF p.204 = printed p.174) shows the same eleven alternatives enclosed in a BRACE pair ('{' … '}', mid-height cusps visible on both sides), with per-word underlining on ten of the eleven rows: GREATER/OR/EQUAL; (none); NOT/LESS; NOT; LESS/OR/EQUAL; (none); NOT/GREATER; NOT; NOT/EQUAL; NOT; (none). IS, THAN and TO are never underlined. Per 5.2.2 (Keywords — underlined = required) and 5.2.3 (Optional words — not underlined = optional at the user's option), the transcription loses the required/optional distinction and reads as though IS, THAN and TO were mandatory, which would falsely reject legal source such as 'IF A NOT GREATER B', 'IF A GREATER OR EQUAL B', 'IF A LESS OR EQUAL B' and 'IF A NOT EQUAL B'. Per 5.2.6.3, the brace also encodes a mandatory choose-exactly-one, which the neutral rectangle does not convey. The claim's rationale miscited the convention as 5.2.5; 5.2.5 is 'Level numbers'. Fix: re-render the figure with the brace delimiter and <u>…</u> on the ten underlined-word rows, or add the standard '> **Figure notes (…)**' blockquote used by ~25 other figures in this file; the same defect affects the Format 1 figure transcribed at the end of page 203, which is additionally rendered with bracket-like characters instead of a brace.

### p232 · incorrect-text · **normative**

- **PDF:** Table 5 — Combinations of conditions, logical operators, and parentheses; row 'simple-condition', column 'In a left-to-right sequence of elements: Element, when not first, may be immediately preceded by only:' reads "EXCLUSIVE-OR or XOR, OR, NOT, AND, ("
- **Markdown:** | simple-condition | Yes | Yes | EXCLUSIVE-OR or XOR, OR, AND, ( | EXCLUSIVE-OR or XOR, OR, AND, ) |   (ISO_COBOL.md line 10097)
- **Why:** The transcription drops NOT from the set of elements that may immediately precede a simple-condition. As transcribed, Table 5 says a simple-condition may never follow NOT, which makes every negated simple condition ('IF NOT A = B', 'IF X AND NOT Y') illegal, contradicts the same table's own NOT row (NOT may be immediately followed by 'simple-condition, (' ) and the Table 5 NOTE ('the pair NOT (' is permissible while the pair NOT NOT is not permissible'), and contradicts 8.8.4.10, Complex negated conditions. The loss is toward falsely-restrictive syntax: legal COBOL made to look illegal. A conformance-checker or grammar built from this row would reject NOT applied to a simple condition.
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 10097 restore NOT to the fourth column: change 'EXCLUSIVE-OR or XOR, OR, AND, (' to 'EXCLUSIVE-OR or XOR, OR, NOT, AND, (' so the row reads: | simple-condition | Yes | Yes | EXCLUSIVE-OR or XOR, OR, NOT, AND, ( | EXCLUSIVE-OR or XOR, OR, AND, ) |
- **Verifier:** Accurate as filed. Precise form: ISO_COBOL.md line 10097 (Table 5, section 8.8.4.11.3, PDF page 232 / printed folio 202), row 'simple-condition', column 'In a left-to-right sequence of elements: Element, when not first, may be immediately preceded by only:' should read 'EXCLUSIVE-OR or XOR, OR, NOT, AND, (' but transcribes 'EXCLUSIVE-OR or XOR, OR, AND, (' -- the token NOT is dropped. All four other cells in that row, and the NOT / '(' / ')' / connective rows, transcribe correctly (the '(' row on line 10100 retains NOT in the same column), so the fix is a single-token insertion of 'NOT, ' before 'AND, (' on line 10097. Impact: as transcribed the exhaustive ('only') predecessor set forbids NOT before a simple-condition, making every negated simple condition illegal, contradicting the same table's NOT row (line 10099) and NOTE (line 10103).

### p288 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** CLASS-ID. object-class-name-1 [ AS literal-1 ] [ IS FINAL ]
    [ INHERITS FROM { object-class-name-2 } ... ]
    [ USING { parameter-name-1 }  ...  ] .

-- with underlining on IDENTIFICATION, DIVISION, CLASS-ID, AS, FINAL, INHERITS, USING, END, CLASS, FACTORY, IMPLEMENTS, OBJECT. IS and FROM are printed WITHOUT underlining (verified by cropping/zooming the rendered page at 300 dpi).
- **Markdown:** CLASS-ID. object-class-name-1 [ AS literal-1 ] [ IS FINAL ]
　[ INHERITS FROM { object-class-name-2 } ... ]
　[ USING { parameter-name-1 } ... ] .

-- all words plain text; no <u> markers and no 'Figure notes' paragraph recording which words are underlined.
- **Why:** Per the general-format conventions, an underlined reserved word is REQUIRED and a non-underlined reserved word is OPTIONAL and may be omitted. The PDF prints IS and FROM without underlining, so 'CLASS-ID. C-1 FINAL.' and 'INHERITS { object-class-name-2 } ...' are legal. With the underlining dropped, the markdown reads as though IS and FROM were mandatory -- the falsely-restrictive direction. The same loss applies to the factory-definition and instance-definition skeletons on this page.
- **Repair:** Record the underlining for all three page-288 skeletons the way the transcription already does elsewhere: either <u>...</u> markers (as used at 11.4.2 on page 296, e.g. '<u>FACTORY</u>. [ <u>IMPLEMENTS</u> ...') or a '> **Figure notes ...** Underlined (required) words in the printed figure: ...' paragraph (as used for program-definition on page 287).
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with narrowed scope and an added sibling sweep.

CONFIRMED CORE: On page 288 the printed class-definition skeleton underlines CLASS-ID, AS, FINAL, INHERITS, USING (and IDENTIFICATION, DIVISION, END, CLASS) but prints IS and FROM WITHOUT underlining. specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 12537-12540 render the skeleton as plain text with no <u> markup and no 'Figure notes' paragraph, so the required/optional distinction carried by the underline is destroyed. Under 5.2.5/5.2.6 (md 2628, 2641, 5770, 5805) a non-underlined uppercase word is an OPTIONAL word that may be omitted with no semantic effect; the markdown therefore reads as though IS and FROM were mandatory. This is falsely restrictive and is contradicted by the standard's own sample code, which writes INHERITS with no FROM in six places (md 45770, 46001, 46047, 46420, 46487, 46609). Legal forms a markdown-derived parser would wrongly reject: 'CLASS-ID. C-1 FINAL.' and 'CLASS-ID. C-1 INHERITS Base.'

SCOPE CORRECTION: the claim's assertion that 'the same loss applies to the factory-definition and instance-definition skeletons on this page' overstates the impact. Those two skeletons also lose their underline record, but every reserved word in them (IDENTIFICATION, DIVISION, FACTORY, IMPLEMENTS, END, OBJECT) is underlined/required in the print, so no optional word is misrepresented and no falsely-restrictive reading follows. The normative loss on page 288 is confined to IS and FROM in the class-definition skeleton.

SIBLING DEFECTS FOUND (sweep targets, same root cause):
  - md line 12816-12818, section 11.3.2 CLASS-ID paragraph General format (page 294): repeats the identical figure ('[ IS  FINAL ]', '[ INHERITS FROM ... ]') with no underline record and no Figure notes. Same normative loss, in the paragraph that is the actual syntax authority for CLASS-ID.
  - md line 12583-12586, interface-definition skeleton (page 289): '[ INHERITS FROM { interface-name-2 } ... ]' with no Figure notes; same FROM loss.

SECONDARY FORMATTING DEFECTS on page 288 (non-normative, but they mark this block as unprocessed relative to its neighbours): the three skeletons are NOT wrapped in ``` code fences, unlike the equivalent skeletons on pages 287 and 289; and md lines 12539-12540 are indented with U+3000 IDEOGRAPHIC SPACE rather than ordinary spaces.

REMEDY: fence the three page-288 skeletons and add a 'Figure notes (class-definition general format)' paragraph in the established house style, e.g. "`IDENTIFICATION`, `DIVISION`, `CLASS-ID`, `AS`, `FINAL`, `INHERITS`, `USING`, `END`, and `CLASS` are underlined in the printed standard (required words); `IS` and `FROM` are **not** underlined -- they are optional words." Add the equivalent note to 11.3.2 and to the page-289 interface-definition skeleton, and replace the U+3000 indents.

### p294 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** 11.3.2  General format

  CLASS-ID.  object-class-name-1 [ AS literal-1 ]  [ IS  FINAL ]
      [ INHERITS FROM { object-class-name-2 }  ... ]
      [ USING { parameter-name-1 }  ... ] .

-- underlining on CLASS-ID, AS, FINAL, INHERITS, USING; IS and FROM printed WITHOUT underlining.
- **Markdown:** ```
CLASS-ID.  object-class-name-1 [ AS literal-1 ] [ IS  FINAL ]
    [ INHERITS FROM { object-class-name-2 }  … ]
    [ USING { parameter-name-1 }  … ] .
```

-- fenced, textually exact, but with no record of which words are underlined and therefore required.
- **Why:** This is the normative general format for the CLASS-ID paragraph. Losing the underlining erases the required/optional distinction: the standard permits 'CLASS-ID. C-1 FINAL.' (IS is an optional word) and 'INHERITS { C-2 }' (FROM is an optional word). A reader of the markdown alone would implement both as mandatory and reject legal source.
- **Repair:** Add a 'Figure notes' line under the 11.3.2 fence recording the underlined (required) words -- CLASS-ID, AS, FINAL, INHERITS, USING -- and explicitly noting that IS and FROM are optional words. Apply the same to the other unannotated general-format fences in clause 11.
- **Verifier:** The finding is real and the claim's characterization of both sources is accurate; two refinements for the ledger.

(a) Scope -- this is NOT a systemic 'the transcription never records underlining' issue. specs/ISO_COBOL.md preserves underlining routinely, via inline <u> tags (§11.4.2 FACTORY line 12885, §11.5.2 FUNCTION-ID lines 12929/12933) and via '> **Figure notes**' blocks (§11.7.2 METHOD-ID line 13034, which even spells out that IS in '[ IS FINAL ]' is not underlined). §11.3.2 CLASS-ID at lines 12813-12819 is a localized regression against that established convention, so the fix is to restore markup on this figure, not to invent a new policy.

(b) Sibling defect -- the identical loss occurs at §11.6.2 INTERFACE-ID (page 298, master lines ~12971-12975), also a bare fence: 'INTERFACE-ID.  interface-name-1  [ AS literal-1 ] / [ INHERITS FROM { interface-name-2 } … ] / [ USING { parameter-name-1 } … ] .' It carries the same INHERITS FROM / USING shape and therefore the same lost required/optional distinction. Per the project's sweep rule, both should be repaired in one change set.

(c) Minor imprecision in the claim, not affecting the verdict: the illustrative 'INHERITS { C-2 }' writes the metalanguage braces as if they were source characters; the braces are grouping symbols for the ellipsis, so the legal source is 'INHERITS class-2'. The claim's PDF quotation also normalizes some inter-token spacing.

Corrected markdown for §11.3.2 should read, in the file's own <u> style: '<u>CLASS-ID</u>.  object-class-name-1 [ <u>AS</u> literal-1 ] [ IS  <u>FINAL</u> ] / [ <u>INHERITS</u> FROM { object-class-name-2 } … ] / [ <u>USING</u> { parameter-name-1 } … ] .'

### p298 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** INTERFACE-ID.  interface-name-1  [ AS literal-1 ]
    [ INHERITS FROM { interface-name-2 }  ... ]
    [ USING { parameter-name-1 }  ... ] .

(printed underlining, verified on the rendered page at 300 dpi: INTERFACE-ID, AS, INHERITS and USING each carry an underline; FROM carries NO underline)
- **Markdown:** ```
INTERFACE-ID.  interface-name-1  [ AS literal-1 ]
    [ INHERITS FROM { interface-name-2 }  … ]
    [ USING { parameter-name-1 }  … ] .
```

(no <u> markup, and no '> **Figure notes (...)**' paragraph follows the fence)
- **Why:** Per 5.2.1/5.2.2 (lines 2628 and 2641 of the transcription's own text) underlined uppercase words are KEYWORDS — required — while non-underlined uppercase words are OPTIONAL words that may be omitted. In the printed 11.6.2 format FROM is not underlined, so 'INHERITS interface-name-2' with FROM omitted is legal COBOL. The fenced transcription records no underlining at all and carries no Figure notes paragraph, so a reader/implementer treats FROM as required — the exact falsely-restrictive-syntax loss this sweep is hunting. The transcription already has an established mechanism for this: 218 other figures carry a '> **Figure notes (...)**' blockquote naming the underlined words (e.g. 11.7.2 METHOD-ID at line 13034, 12.3.6.2 OBJECT-COMPUTER at line 13746). Section 11.6.2 is missing its instance.
- **Repair:** Append after the 11.6.2 fence: '> **Figure notes (INTERFACE-ID paragraph general format).** `INTERFACE-ID`, `AS`, `INHERITS`, and `USING` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). `FROM` is **not** underlined — it is an optional word and may be omitted. The paragraph is terminated by a separator period printed after the USING bracket.'
- **Verifier:** REAL, normative. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 12972-12977 (§11.6.2 INTERFACE-ID paragraph, General format; PDF page 298 / printed folio 268) transcribes the general format into a plain code fence that records no underlining and carries no '> **Figure notes (...)**' blockquote, so every uppercase word in the figure reads as equally required. On the printed page, verified by pixel measurement of a 300 dpi render, INTERFACE-ID, AS, INHERITS and USING are underlined but FROM is NOT (zero ink in the underline band beneath FROM even at a near-white threshold of 245, against 549 pixels beneath INHERITS). Per 5.2.2, underlined uppercase words are keywords and are required; per 5.2.3, non-underlined uppercase words are optional words that may be omitted. The flattened fence therefore loses the fact that 'INHERITS interface-name-2' without FROM is legal COBOL — a falsely-restrictive-syntax loss. The standard's own Annex examples confirm the omission is legal, writing e.g. 'CLASS-ID. CheckingAccount INHERITS Base.' (line 45770) and 'CLASS-ID.  AccountCollection INHERITS Base USING X.' (line 46420).

FIX: add the established Figure notes blockquote after the line-12977 fence, e.g. '> **Figure notes (INTERFACE-ID paragraph general format).** `INTERFACE-ID`, `AS`, `INHERITS`, and `USING` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). `FROM` is **not** underlined — it is an optional word and may be omitted, so `INHERITS interface-name-2` is legal. The terminating period follows the closing bracket of the USING phrase.'

SWEEP — THIS IS A PATTERN, NOT A ONE-OFF. The same INHERITS-FROM underline loss appears in at least three sibling figures that also lack Figure notes and must be fixed in the same change set:
  * line 12817 — §11.3.2 CLASS-ID paragraph general format ('[ INHERITS FROM { object-class-name-2 } … ]'), fenced, no Figure notes; the fence also flattens the non-underlined `IS` in '[ IS  FINAL ]'.
  * line 12585 — the interface-definition skeleton ('where interface-definition is:'), fenced, no Figure notes.
  * line 12539 — the class-definition skeleton, which is not even fenced (plain text using ideographic-space indentation), no Figure notes.
Recommend a targeted re-check of every general format containing INHERITS, and more broadly an audit for figures whose fence contains an uppercase word that is an optional word in print.

### p320 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** [ CURRENCY SIGN IS  literal-7   [ WITH PICTURE  SYMBOL literal-8 ] ]  ...   (the printed page draws a TALL outer bracket that opens immediately before CURRENCY and closes immediately after the inner ']' of the WITH phrase; the trailing ellipsis sits outside that outer bracket)
- **Markdown:** `CURRENCY SIGN IS literal-7 [ WITH PICTURE SYMBOL literal-8 ]` ...   (ISO_COBOL.md line 13920 - no outer bracket at all)
- **Why:** The lost outer bracket is the one that makes the whole CURRENCY SIGN clause OPTIONAL inside SPECIAL-NAMES. As transcribed, the clause reads as a REQUIRED member of the SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, and the trailing '...' now appears to repeat only the inner WITH PICTURE SYMBOL phrase rather than the whole clause. Every sibling clause on the same page (CRT STATUS line 13918, CURSOR line 13922, DECIMAL-POINT line 13924) DOES keep its bracket, so this is an omission, not a transcription convention.
- **Repair:** Rewrite line 13920 as `[ CURRENCY SIGN IS literal-7 [ WITH PICTURE SYMBOL literal-8 ] ] ...` (outer bracket restored, ellipsis outside it).
- **Verifier:** The finding is real and the quoted texts are accurate; two refinements to the claim's framing. (1) The claim's supporting argument -- 'every sibling clause on the same page DOES keep its bracket' -- is overstated. The LOCALE clause on the same page has the SAME defect: the PDF prints a tall outer bracket pair around it ([ LOCALE locale-name-1 IS { external-locale-name-1 | literal-4 } ] ...), and ISO_COBOL.md line 13928 renders it as `LOCALE locale-name-1 IS { external-locale-name-1 / literal-4 }` ... with the outer bracket dropped. The two clauses that lost their outer bracket (CURRENCY line 13920, LOCALE line 13928) are exactly the two rendered inside backticks, while the plain-text siblings (CRT STATUS 13918, CURSOR 13922, DECIMAL-POINT 13924) kept theirs. So this is a systematic transcription defect affecting backtick-wrapped clause lines -- which makes the finding broader and worth a paired fix, not narrower. (2) Adjacent observation, NOT part of this finding and NOT a transcription error: the PDF itself prints '[ CURSOR IS data-name-1 ] ]' with a stray unmatched second ']' (an ISO typesetting defect), and ISO_COBOL.md line 13922 reproduces it faithfully. That stray bracket is not the missing CURRENCY bracket relocated -- the CURRENCY outer bracket visibly opens and closes on its own line. Correct repair for line 13920: `[ CURRENCY SIGN IS literal-7 [ WITH PICTURE SYMBOL literal-8 ] ]` ... (outer bracket restored, ellipsis outside it), and correspondingly for line 13928: `[ LOCALE locale-name-1 IS { external-locale-name-1 | literal-4 } ]` ...

### p320 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** [ LOCALE locale-name-1 IS { external-locale-name-1 / literal-4 } ]  ...   (a TALL outer bracket opens before LOCALE and closes after the brace pair; the ellipsis is outside it)
- **Markdown:** `LOCALE locale-name-1 IS { external-locale-name-1 / literal-4 }` ...   (ISO_COBOL.md line 13928 - no outer bracket)
- **Why:** Same class of loss as the CURRENCY line: the bracket that makes the LOCALE clause optional inside SPECIAL-NAMES is gone, so the transcription reads as though every SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph must carry a LOCALE clause, and the '...' now binds to the brace pair instead of to the whole clause.
- **Repair:** Rewrite line 13928 as `[ LOCALE locale-name-1 IS { external-locale-name-1 / literal-4 } ] ...`.
- **Verifier:** Accurate as claimed; no correction required. For the record, the exact printed form at page 320 (PDF p.290) 12.3.7.2 is a plain outer bracket with NO choice indicators: [ LOCALE locale-name-1 IS { external-locale-name-1 | literal-4 } ] ... - tall '[' before LOCALE, tall ']' after the closing brace, ellipsis outside the bracket. specs/ISO_COBOL.md:13928 drops the outer bracket pair entirely. Fix: restore it, e.g. `[ LOCALE locale-name-1 IS { external-locale-name-1 | literal-4 } ]` ... , and note that the sibling CURRENCY line at specs/ISO_COBOL.md:13920 in the same figure carries the identical loss (the PDF prints a tall outer bracket around the whole CURRENCY clause, closing after the inner '[ WITH PICTURE SYMBOL literal-8 ]' and before the ellipsis) - fix both in one change set.

### p363 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** 12.4.6.3.2 General format:  APPLY COMMIT ON  [[ file-name-1][identifier-1]]...   — in the printed figure APPLY and COMMIT each carry an underline; ON carries no underline. (Preceding figure, 12.4.6.2: "I-O-CONTROL  .  [ [ apply-commit-clause] . ]   [[{ same-clause }...].]" — I-O-CONTROL is underlined.)
- **Markdown:** ```
APPLY COMMIT ON  [[ file-name-1][identifier-1]]…
```
(and, above it, "```\nI-O-CONTROL . [ [ apply-commit-clause] . ]  [[ { same-clause } …].]\n```") — both figures are plain code blocks with no <u> markup and no "Figure notes" paragraph, so no underlining information survives.
- **Why:** Underlining is normative in a general format: underlined uppercase words are required words, non-underlined uppercase words are optional words. With the underlines dropped, the markdown APPLY COMMIT ON reads as three required keywords, when ON is optional — the falsely-restrictive-syntax failure mode this sweep is hunting. Every other general format in this batch preserves the distinction: pages 349, 355, 357, 359, 361 and 365 each carry an explicit "Figure notes" paragraph naming which words are underlined, and page 365 Formats 1 and 2 use inline <u>SAME</u> / <u>RECORD</u> markup. Page 363 is the only figure-bearing page in the batch that records neither.
- **Repair:** Add a "Figure notes" paragraph after each of the two code blocks on page 363, matching the convention used on 349/355/357/359/361/365: for 12.4.6.3.2 state that APPLY and COMMIT are underlined (required words) and ON is not underlined (optional word), and that the ellipsis binds to the outer bracket pair so the [ file-name-1 ][ identifier-1 ] group may repeat; for 12.4.6.2 state that I-O-CONTROL is underlined and that nothing else in that figure is.
- **Verifier:** The finding is real and the claim's substance is accurate; two refinements to its scope.

SCOPE NARROWING: only the 12.4.6.3.2 figure suffers an actual normative loss. In the 12.4.6.2 I-O-CONTROL figure the underlined word `I-O-CONTROL` is the ONLY uppercase word present — every other element is a lowercase nonterminal (`apply-commit-clause`, `same-clause`) or punctuation — so dropping its underline creates no required/optional ambiguity, and §5.2.9 already makes the separator period required regardless. Treat 12.4.6.2 as cosmetic and 12.4.6.3.2 as the normative defect.

THE DEFECT, precisely: in the printed 12.4.6.3.2 general format, `APPLY` and `COMMIT` are underlined (required words per §5.2.2) and `ON` is printed WITHOUT an underline (an optional word per §5.2.3), so `APPLY COMMIT file-name-1` is legal source with ON omitted. Verified at 300 dpi: the underline scanline y=1841 has exactly two runs, x=599-737 (APPLY, glyphs 605-732) and x=758-937 (COMMIT, glyphs 755-933), and no run at all beneath the ON glyphs at x=955-1011. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 15660 renders the figure as a bare code fence with no <u> markup and no "Figure notes" paragraph, losing the distinction entirely and presenting ON as a third required keyword.

FIX: add a Figure notes paragraph after line 15661 reading, e.g. "> **Figure notes (APPLY COMMIT clause general format).** `APPLY` and `COMMIT` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). `ON` is **not** underlined — it is an optional word. `file-name-1` and `identifier-1` are not underlined. The nested brackets with the trailing ellipsis permit one or more file-names and/or identifiers, subject to SR 4 (at least one file or data-item shall be specified)." Optionally also note that `I-O-CONTROL` is underlined in the 12.4.6.2 figure, for uniformity with the surrounding pages.

NO LIVE COMPILER BUG YET: `APPLY`/`APPLY COMMIT` appears in no `.g4` fragment under src/, so the clause is unimplemented and nothing currently mis-requires ON. The defect is forward-looking — it would seed a falsely-restrictive grammar the moment the I-O-CONTROL paragraph is implemented from the transcription.

### p373 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** 13.4.5.2 General formats, Format 3 (report): the CODE-SET clause is printed as  [ CODE-SET { IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2]  /  { | FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 | / | FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2 | } } ] — i.e. the INNER brace around the two FOR phrases carries a pair of vertical CHOICE INDICATOR bars just inside it (a full-height bar immediately after the inner '{' and another immediately before the inner '}'). Verified by 300 dpi crop of the region; underlined words on the two rows are ALPHANUMERIC and NATIONAL.
- **Markdown:** [ CODE-SET { IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2] / { FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 / FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2 } } ]
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6.4, a pair of vertical bars just inside a brace means one or more of the enclosed alternatives may be specified, each at most once, in any order. Without the bars a brace means EXACTLY ONE. The markdown therefore makes 'CODE-SET FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alpha-1 FOR NATIONAL IS alpha-2' — a legal Format 3 FD entry that specifies both phrases — look illegal, and also forbids the reversed order. This is the same construct that page 372 (Format 1) transcribes CORRECTLY with bars ('⎧ | FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 | ⎫'), so the two formats of the same clause now disagree in the markdown.
- **Repair:** Re-render Format 3's CODE-SET group with the inner-brace choice indicators, matching the Format 1 rendering already present under page 372, and add a Figure-notes line stating that per 5.2.6.4 both FOR phrases may be specified, in either order.
- **Verifier:** The claim is accurate as written; no correction to its substance is needed. Two refinements for the fix-up work. (a) Precise printed nesting, measured from the 300 dpi render: '[' CODE-SET '{' <row1: IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2]> / '{' '|' <FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 / FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2> '|' '}' '}' ']'. The bar pair (2 px rules at page x=696-697 and x=1554-1555, both y=1952..2109) is the INNERMOST enclosure, sitting just inside the inner brace pair (19 px curvy glyphs at x=665-683 and x=1568-1586), which in turn sits inside the outer brace pair (x=616-634 and x=1613-1631) inside the outer bracket (stems x=371-372 and x=1641-1642, y=1826..2145). Only the inner brace of the second alternative is bar-enclosed; the outer brace choosing between the 'IS alphabet-name-1' alternative and the FOR-phrase group is a plain exactly-one brace, and the outer CODE-SET bracket is a plain optional bracket. (b) Two additional, non-normative defects on the same page that should be repaired in the same edit, since the whole Format 3 figure has to be re-transcribed anyway: page 373 renders Formats 2 and 3 as flattened one-line plain text with '/' alternation instead of the fenced box-drawing figure style used for Format 1 on page 372, and it carries no figure note recording the underlined required words or the choice-indicator semantics (page 372 has both). The fix should re-transcribe Format 3 as a fenced figure with the bars restored and add a figure note mirroring line 15987's, so the two formats of 13.4.5.2 agree.

### p389 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** 13.15.2 General format, the USAGE line is printed inside its OWN OUTER BRACKET spanning both rows:  ⎡ [ USAGE IS ] ⎧ DISPLAY ⎫ ⎤ / ⎣               ⎩ NATIONAL ⎭ ⎦ . Verified at 300 dpi: a square bracket opens to the left of the inner '[ USAGE IS ]' and closes to the right of the brace's '}'. USAGE, DISPLAY and NATIONAL are underlined; IS is not.
- **Markdown:**   [ USAGE IS ] { DISPLAY  }
               { NATIONAL }
- **Why:** The outer bracket is what makes the whole USAGE clause optional in a report group description entry. Stripped of it, the markdown reads as a mandatory brace: every report-group entry would be required to state DISPLAY or NATIONAL. That is falsely restrictive — the clause is optional, which is also what 13.15.4 general rule 2) presupposes when it says the USAGE clause, when present, behaves as in a data description entry. Every other clause on this page is shown bracketed/optional; USAGE is the only one whose bracket was dropped.
- **Repair:** Restore the enclosing bracket, e.g.  ⎡ [ USAGE IS ] ⎧ DISPLAY  ⎫ ⎤ / ⎣               ⎩ NATIONAL ⎭ ⎦ , so the whole USAGE clause is optional.
- **Verifier:** Accurate as filed; no correction needed. Refinements for the fix: the dropped delimiter is a closed tall square-bracket PAIR spanning both stacked rows and enclosing the inner '[ USAGE IS ]' plus the whole '{ DISPLAY / NATIONAL }' brace. Restore it using the transcription's own established convention (cf. 13.16.2 Format 1 '[ [ SIGN IS ] { LEADING } [ SEPARATE CHARACTER ] ]' and '[ GROUP-USAGE IS { BIT } ]'), i.e.:
  [ [ USAGE IS ] { DISPLAY  } ]
                 { NATIONAL }
One detail of the claim's rationale is slightly overstated but immaterial: 13.15.4 GR 2 does not itself say 'when present'; it says the USAGE, PICTURE, BLANK WHEN ZERO and JUSTIFIED clauses are the same clauses as in a data description entry and obey those clauses' rules — which implies optionality only by grouping USAGE with three clauses that are printed bracketed here and by the absence of any syntax rule mandating USAGE. The load-bearing evidence is the printed outer bracket itself, which is unambiguous.

### p471 · incorrect-text · **normative**

- **PDF:** In the PICTURE Format 1 diagram the word FOR is printed UNDERLINED (verified by rendering page 471 at 300 dpi and cropping the FOR group: 'FOR' carries a rule beneath it, exactly as NEGATIVE and POSITIVE do).
- **Markdown:** First figure-notes paragraph: '**Figure notes (PICTURE clause Format 1 (basic) syntax diagram).** `PICTURE`, `PIC`, `EDITING`, `NEGATIVE`, and `POSITIVE` are underlined in the printed standard (required words / required minimum abbreviation).' — `FOR` is omitted from the list of underlined words. (The second, duplicate note on the same page does list `FOR` as underlined, so the two notes contradict each other.)
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6, an underlined word in a general format is a REQUIRED word that shall be written when the phrase is used; a non-underlined word is an optional word that may be omitted. Omitting FOR from the underlined list makes the FOR keyword look omissible, i.e. it would legalise 'EDITING c NEGATIVE IS lit-2' with no FOR - a grammar the standard does not permit. This is the falsely-permissive mirror of the usual defect and it is directly load-bearing for the PICTURE EDITING parser.
- **Repair:** In the surviving Format 1 figure-notes paragraph state: '`PICTURE`, `PIC`, `EDITING`, `FOR`, `NEGATIVE`, and `POSITIVE` are underlined in the printed standard (required words / required minimum abbreviation); `IS`, `character-string-1`, `character-1`, and the literals are not.'
- **Verifier:** REAL, with two corrections to the claim as filed.

(1) CITATION CORRECTION. The claim cites 5.2.6 for the underline convention. 5.2.6 is 'Options' and governs brackets, braces and choice indicators only. The underline convention is 5.2.2 Keywords ('Keywords are reserved words or context-sensitive words. They are shown in uppercase and underlined in general formats. They are required in order to select the functionality associated with that keyword...' — specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 2628) and 5.2.3 Optional words ('...shown in uppercase and not underlined in general formats. They may be written to add clarity...' — line 2641). The claim's substantive reasoning is right; only the section number is wrong. Cite 5.2.2/5.2.3, not 5.2.6.

(2) THE ROOT DEFECT IS LARGER THAN THE UNDERLINE LIST, and fixing it fixes this. The PDF prints the Format 1 (basic) diagram EXACTLY ONCE, immediately followed by 'Format 2 (locale):'. The markdown contains the Format 1 diagram plus its figure note TWICE: block A at lines 19950-19962 and block B at lines 19964-19971. Block A is the stale/inferior copy — it is the one missing FOR from the underlined list, and its inner stack is also more crudely rendered ('⎧|' / '⎩|' for the inner braces versus block B's better-formed '⎨ |'). Block B is complete and correct: it lists FOR as underlined and adds the negative half naming IS, character-string-1, character-1 and the literals as not underlined. Both blocks otherwise agree that the FOR group is enclosed in choice indicators (correct — the bars are visible in the render at x ~1660 and ~1990, and per 5.2.6.4 braces around choice indicators mean one or more of NEGATIVE/POSITIVE, each at most once, in any order).

RECOMMENDED FIX: delete duplicate block A (lines 19950-19962) in its entirety and keep block B. That simultaneously removes the spurious second diagram the PDF does not print and eliminates the contradictory, FOR-omitting figure note — a single deletion resolving both defects. Do not 'fix' block A by adding FOR to its list, which would leave the spurious duplicate diagram in place.

NORMATIVE IMPACT AS FILED IS UPHELD: FOR is a keyword and shall be written whenever the NEGATIVE/POSITIVE choice-indicator group is specified. 'EDITING character-1 NEGATIVE IS literal-2' without FOR is not legal COBOL, and the PICTURE EDITING parser must require the FOR keyword before the NEGATIVE/POSITIVE group.

### p490 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** Table 10 - Format 1 picture symbol order of precedence, row 'P' (the FIRST of the two P rows in the 'Other symbols' block on p.490). The printed row carries TEN x marks, in First-Symbol columns 1 (B 0 /), 2 (,), 5 (+ -), 8 (cs), 10 (Z *), 12 (+ -), 14 (cs), 16 (9), 18 (S) and 20 (P). It is mark-for-mark identical to the 'V' row printed directly above it.
- **Markdown:** | | P | x | x | | | x | | | x | | x | | x | | x | | x | | x | | | | | | |   -- only NINE x cells (columns 1, 2, 5, 8, 10, 12, 14, 16, 18). The mark in the 20th data column, the first 'P' column, is absent. The 'V' row immediately above it, '| | V | x | x | | | x | | | x | | x | | x | | x | | x | | x | | x | | | | | |', still carries all ten.
- **Why:** Table 10 is the normative precedence table a compiler consults to decide whether a PICTURE character-string is legal. The dropped mark states that a 'P' may not precede a 'P', so a PICTURE such as PPP9 / 9PPP (repeated scaling P) reads as illegal from the transcription while the standard permits it. This is the same falsely-restrictive direction as the lost choice-indicator defects.
- **Repair:** Restore the missing 'x' in the first 'P' row at the 20th First-Symbol data column (the first of the two 'P' columns), making the row identical to the 'V' row above it.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, and the claim is accurate as written; two refinements worth recording.

(1) Mechanism: the markdown P row is not a row whose 'x' was blanked - it is a row that is one CELL short. It has 26 pipe-cells against the V row's 27, and the missing cell is the trailing 'x' cell itself. The nine surviving marks are still in their correct columns (no offset), so the defect is a clean loss of the column-20 'P' mark and nothing else.

(2) Collateral damage in the same block, which the claim does not mention and which should be fixed in the same repair: the SECOND P row (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 20721) is likewise 26 cells and has also lost a cell, but an EMPTY one, somewhere between columns 8 and 17. Its five marks all survive yet three of them land one column too far left: the print has that row marked at columns 5 (+ -), 8 (cs), 18 (S), 19 (V) and 21 (P, the trailing-P column), while the markdown places them at 5, 8, 17, 18 and 20. Read literally the transcription therefore also asserts that the trailing P may follow 'A X', 'S' and the LEADING P rather than 'S', 'V' and the trailing P - a second normative corruption of the same table, non-restrictive in one direction and falsely restrictive in another. Additionally the header/first row (line 20715) carries one EXTRA leading blank cell (27 cells with data starting at index 3 rather than index 2), so the Table 10 continuation block on p.490 is column-misaligned in three separate rows. The p.490 markdown also reproduces no column headers at all - the 'First Symbol' header band is transcribed only under p.489 - so the surviving marks cannot be resolved to symbols without cross-page context. Any repair should re-derive all nine rows of the p.490 block against the 24-column header on p.489 rather than patching the single missing 'x'.

### p508 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** SAME AS data-name-1   (13.18.49.2 General format - BOTH 'SAME' and 'AS' carry their own underline; verified by cropping and 3x-enlarging the printed line at 300 dpi: two distinct underline rules, one under SAME, one under AS)
- **Markdown:** <u>SAME</u> AS data-name-1   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 21474)
- **Why:** In the general-format notation an underlined word is a REQUIRED word (5.2.6.2) while a word that appears without underlining is an OPTIONAL word that may be written or omitted at the user's choice (5.2.6.3). Dropping the underline from AS makes the transcription say that 'SAME data-name-1' is legal COBOL and that AS is noise. It is not: AS is a required word of the SAME AS clause. This is the only clause in 13.18.49 and the whole format is two words, so the lost marker is the entire syntactic content of the second word.
- **Repair:** Change the general format line to '<u>SAME</u> <u>AS</u> data-name-1' so both required words carry the underline that the printed standard gives them.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed, with one correction to the claim's citations. The substance is exactly right: the printed 13.18.49.2 General format underlines BOTH keywords - 'SAME' and 'AS' each carry their own separate rule (measured at 300 dpi: rules at x=273 len 103 and x=401 len 40 on scan row y=915, with a 25 px gap between them), while 'data-name-1' is unmarked. specs/ISO_COBOL.md:21474 transcribes only '<u>SAME</u> AS data-name-1', losing the required-word marker on AS and thereby making the transcription assert that AS is an optional word and that 'SAME data-name-1' is legal COBOL. The fix is '<u>SAME</u> <u>AS</u> data-name-1'. CITATION CORRECTION: the claim attributes the required/optional-word notation to 5.2.6.2 and 5.2.6.3, but those subclauses are 'Brackets' and 'Braces'. The governing subclauses are 8.3.2.4.2 (Required words - keywords are uppercase and underlined) and 8.3.2.4.3 (Optional words - uppercase and not underlined, specifiable at the user's option), with the corresponding definitions at 5.2.2 (Keywords) and 5.2.3 (Optional words). The mis-citation does not affect the finding.

### p533 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** In the 13.18.60.2 USAGE clause general format, the four brace groups 'BINARY-CHAR { SIGNED / UNSIGNED }', 'BINARY-SHORT { SIGNED / UNSIGNED }', 'BINARY-LONG { SIGNED / UNSIGNED }' and 'BINARY-DOUBLE { SIGNED / UNSIGNED }' print SIGNED WITHOUT an underline and UNSIGNED WITH an underline. Verified on the 300 dpi render of page 533 and again on a 5x tight crop of the BINARY-CHAR brace (E:/Temp/specrecon/f4/crop_533_900_620.png): no rule under 'SIGNED', a full-width rule under 'UNSIGNED'. The same asymmetry repeats in all four rows.
- **Markdown:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md, page-533 figure notes: "> **Figure notes (USAGE clause general format).** Underlined in the printed standard (required words / required minimum abbreviation): `USAGE`, `BINARY`, `BINARY-CHAR`, `BINARY-SHORT`, `BINARY-LONG`, `BINARY-DOUBLE`, `SIGNED`, `UNSIGNED`, `BIT`, ... `IS`, `OF`, `TO`, and `WITH` are not underlined." — SIGNED is listed among the underlined (required) words.
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6.2/5.2.6.3, an underlined uppercase word is a REQUIRED word and a non-underlined uppercase word is an OPTIONAL word that may be omitted without changing the meaning. Because SIGNED is printed unmarked, the brace group '{ SIGNED / UNSIGNED }' is satisfiable by writing nothing at all, so USAGE BINARY-CHAR, USAGE BINARY-CHAR SIGNED and USAGE BINARY-CHAR UNSIGNED are all legal (likewise -SHORT, -LONG, -DOUBLE). Promoting SIGNED to a required word makes the brace a mandatory two-way choice, which rejects the bare 'USAGE BINARY-CHAR' / 'USAGE BINARY-LONG' spellings that real COBOL source uses constantly. This is the same falsely-restrictive direction of loss the sweep is hunting: legal source made to look illegal. A compiler or a reviewer working from this note would emit a diagnostic for conforming source, and would mis-classify SIGNED as a reserved required word rather than an optional word.
- **Repair:** In the page-533 figure note, remove `SIGNED` from the underlined list and add it to the not-underlined list ("`IS`, `OF`, `TO`, `WITH`, and `SIGNED` are not underlined"), then state explicitly that because SIGNED is an optional word the four BINARY-* brace groups admit three spellings each: `BINARY-CHAR`, `BINARY-CHAR SIGNED`, and `BINARY-CHAR UNSIGNED` (and correspondingly for BINARY-SHORT, BINARY-LONG, BINARY-DOUBLE).
- **Verifier:** The page-533 figure note in specs/ISO_COBOL.md misclassifies SIGNED as a required (underlined) word. In the printed standard, all four USAGE brace groups — BINARY-CHAR, BINARY-SHORT, BINARY-LONG and BINARY-DOUBLE, each followed by { SIGNED / UNSIGNED } — print UNSIGNED with a full-width underline and SIGNED with NO underline (verified at 300 dpi: the UNSIGNED rule measures 0.91 dark fraction across all four rows, while the SIGNED band never exceeds 0.54, that maximum being the glyph baseline itself). SIGNED is therefore an OPTIONAL word per 5.2.3 and 8.9, and by 5.2.6.3 the SIGNED alternative — containing only optional words — is the DEFAULT alternative, implicitly selected unless UNSIGNED is explicitly written. The claim's phrasing that the brace is "satisfiable by writing nothing at all" is correct in effect but is more precisely stated as implicit selection of the default alternative under 5.2.6.3. Consequently `USAGE BINARY-CHAR`, `USAGE BINARY-CHAR SIGNED` and `USAGE BINARY-CHAR UNSIGNED` are all legal and the first two are equivalent (likewise -SHORT, -LONG, -DOUBLE). FIX: move `SIGNED` out of the underlined list in the line-22406 figure note and into the not-underlined list, restating it as "`IS`, `OF`, `TO`, `WITH`, and `SIGNED` are not underlined — they are optional words; the SIGNED alternative contains only optional words and is therefore the default alternative selected when neither SIGNED nor UNSIGNED is written (5.2.6.3)." Two further notes for the fixer: the ASCII figure in the code fence carries no underlining at all, so this note is the only place the required/optional distinction survives in the transcription; and SIGNED is the ONLY misclassified word in the note — every other entry was checked against the render and is correct.

### p606 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** ACCEPT Format 2 (temporal): 'DATE [ YYYYMMDD ]' with BOTH DATE and YYYYMMDD underlined, and 'DAY [ YYYYDDD ]' with BOTH DAY and YYYYDDD underlined (verified on the enlarged crop -- each of YYYYMMDD and YYYYDDD carries its own underline rule inside the brackets).
- **Markdown:** \underline{\text{DATE}} \text{ [ YYYYMMDD ]} \\ \underline{\text{DAY}} \text{ [ YYYYDDD ]}
- **Why:** YYYYMMDD and YYYYDDD are required (reserved) words inside their optional brackets. Every other word in this same figure -- ACCEPT, FROM, DATE, DAY, DAY-OF-WEEK, TIME, END-ACCEPT -- is wrapped in \underline, so the omission is not a stylistic choice; it reads as if YYYYMMDD/YYYYDDD were user-supplied operands rather than fixed reserved words, which would let an implementor accept any word there.
- **Repair:** Change to \underline{\text{DATE}} \text{ [ } \underline{\text{YYYYMMDD}} \text{ ]} and \underline{\text{DAY}} \text{ [ } \underline{\text{YYYYDDD}} \text{ ]}.
- **Verifier:** REAL, but the stated harm is misdiagnosed. The typographic facts are exactly as claimed: in the printed Format 2 (temporal) figure on page 606, YYYYMMDD and YYYYDDD each carry their own underline rule inside their brackets (the brackets are unmarked), and the markdown at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 25254 renders both as plain \text{} with no \underline while underlining every other uppercase word in the same formula. Two corrections to the rationale: (1) YYYYMMDD and YYYYDDD are CONTEXT-SENSITIVE words (8.10 table, both mapped to 'ACCEPT statement'), not plain reserved words. (2) Dropping the underline does not make them read as user-supplied operands and would not 'let an implementor accept any word there' -- 5.2.4 fixes operands as LOWERCASE, so an uppercase non-underlined word reads unambiguously as an OPTIONAL WORD under 5.2.3, i.e. a noise word that 'may be written to add clarity' with no functional effect. That is the actual and more damaging misreading: it makes 'ACCEPT x FROM DATE YYYYMMDD' appear semantically identical to 'ACCEPT x FROM DATE', contradicting 14.9.1.3 general rules 7 and 8 (six-digit DATE without the phrase, eight-digit DATE with it) and the corresponding YYYYDDD rules for DAY. Fix: wrap both words in \underline inside their brackets -- '\underline{\text{DATE}} \text{ [ } \underline{\text{YYYYMMDD}} \text{ ]}' and '\underline{\text{DAY}} \text{ [ } \underline{\text{YYYYDDD}} \text{ ]}' -- leaving the brackets themselves unmarked, as printed.

### p607 · incorrect-figure · **normative**
