- **PDF:** ACCEPT screen-name-1 / Format 3 AT phrase, printed as a BRACE WITH CHOICE INDICATORS: '[ AT { |   LINE NUMBER { identifier-3 / integer-1 }   ,   { COLUMN / COL } NUMBER { identifier-4 / integer-2 }   | } ]'. Verified by cropping and enlarging spec_p607.png: immediately inside the tall brace that follows AT there is a single vertical bar on the left, and a matching single vertical bar immediately inside the closing brace on the right. There are NO brackets around the individual LINE / COLUMN rows.
- **Markdown:** $$\left[\begin{array}{l} \text{AT} \left\{\begin{array}{l} \left[\begin{array}{l} \underline{\text{LINE}}\ \text{NUMBER} \left\{\begin{array}{l} \text{identifier-3} \\ \text{integer-1} \end{array}\right\} \end{array}\right] \\ \left[\begin{array}{l} \left\{\begin{array}{l} \underline{\text{COLUMN}} \\ \text{COL} \end{array}\right\}\ \text{NUMBER} \left\{\begin{array}{l} \text{identifier-4} \\ \text{integer-2} \end{array}\right\} \end{array}\right] \end{array}\right\} \end{array}\right]$$
- **Why:** The choice-indicator bars are dropped and each alternative is instead wrapped in a fabricated bracket. Per 5.2.6.4 bars inside a BRACE mean 'one or more of the enclosed alternatives, each at most once, in any order' -- so ACCEPT ... AT LINE NUMBER n COLUMN NUMBER m is legal. The markdown's brace-of-two-optional-brackets reads as 'exactly one of (optional LINE phrase) or (optional COLUMN phrase)', which forbids specifying BOTH LINE and COLUMN in one ACCEPT and also wrongly permits an empty AT with nothing after it. This is the exact falsely-restrictive loss the sweep is hunting; the identical construct on page 640 (DISPLAY Format 2) DOES preserve the bars, which proves the intended transcription and confirms 607 is the defect.
- **Repair:** Retranscribe the AT phrase with the bars preserved, matching the page-640 DISPLAY Format 2 treatment, e.g. an ASCII-art block:  ⎡    ⎧|                ⎧ identifier-3 ⎫             |⎫ ⎤ / ⎢ AT ⎨| LINE NUMBER  ... |⎩ ⎭ ... ⎦ -- i.e. bars just inside the AT brace, no per-row brackets. Add a figure note citing 5.2.6.4 stating that one or more of the LINE and COLUMN phrases shall be specified, each at most once, in any order.
- **Verifier:** The finding is real and the claim's reading of the printed glyphs is accurate. Two refinements, neither of which weakens it:

(a) The claim's ASCII paraphrase of the PDF inserts a comma between the LINE row and the COLUMN row ('{ identifier-3 / integer-1 }   ,   { COLUMN / COL }'). No comma is printed. The two rows are simply stacked vertically inside the choice-indicator bars; the comma is only the claim author's line-layout separator and should not be read as spec text.

(b) The SAME figure carries a SECOND, identical defect that the claim does not mention. The ON EXCEPTION group printed immediately below the AT phrase is also enclosed in choice indicators -- cropping orig px 330-1420 x 1090-1250 shows a tall '[' then a plain serif-less vertical bar, the two stacked EXCEPTION rows, then a plain vertical bar, then ']'. That is '[ | ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-1 / NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-2 | ]'. The markdown at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 25265 transcribes it as a plain bracket with two stacked rows, dropping both bars. Per 5.2.6.4, bars inside BRACKETS mean 'zero or more of the enclosed alternatives, each at most once, in any order', so the PDF permits ON EXCEPTION alone, NOT ON EXCEPTION alone, BOTH in either order, or neither. The markdown's plain bracket reads as 'optional, choose exactly one', which wrongly forbids writing both handlers on one ACCEPT -- a form every COBOL statement admits. Page 640's DISPLAY Format 2 again preserves these bars ('⎡|  ON EXCEPTION ... |⎤'), confirming 607 is the defective page.

Correct transcription for specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 25263 (AT phrase): outer BRACKET, then AT, then a BRACE whose contents are wrapped in a pair of choice-indicator bars, the bars enclosing two stacked alternatives -- 'LINE NUMBER { identifier-3 / integer-1 }' and '{ COLUMN / COL } NUMBER { identifier-4 / integer-2 }' -- with NO brackets around either alternative. Line 25265 (ON EXCEPTION) needs the same repair: a BRACKET enclosing a pair of choice-indicator bars around the two stacked EXCEPTION rows.

Compiler impact: the ACCEPT screen Format 3 AT phrase must accept LINE alone, COLUMN alone, and LINE followed by COLUMN (and, per 'in any order', COLUMN followed by LINE), with the missing phrase defaulting to 1 per general rules 16 and 17.

### p607 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** ACCEPT Format 3 exception phrase, printed as a BRACKET WITH CHOICE INDICATORS: '⎡|  ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-1  |⎤' over '⎣|  NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-2  |⎦'. Verified by cropping and enlarging spec_p607.png: a vertical bar sits immediately inside the opening bracket and a matching bar immediately inside the closing bracket, spanning both stacked rows.
- **Markdown:** $$\left[\begin{array}{l} \text{ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-1} \\ \text{NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-2} \end{array}\right]$$
- **Why:** Without the bars a bracket around stacked alternatives means AT MOST ONE of them. Per 5.2.6.4, bars inside a bracket mean zero or more, each at most once, in any order -- so a single ACCEPT screen statement may carry BOTH ON EXCEPTION and NOT ON EXCEPTION, in either order. The markdown makes legal source look illegal. Every sibling figure in this batch that has the same phrase (613 ADD, 618 CALL Fmt 1, 619 CALL Fmt 2, 640 DISPLAY, 644 DIVIDE) preserves the bars, so 607 is the sole holdout.
- **Repair:** Replace the bare \left[ ... \right] with the bar-preserving form used on pages 618/640/644, e.g. a code block: '⎡|  ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-1      |⎤' / '⎣|  NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-2  |⎦', plus a figure note citing 5.2.6.4.
- **Verifier:** The finding is real and the core of the description is accurate; two refinements.

(1) Sibling wording is loose. Pages 613 (ADD) and 644 (DIVIDE) do not carry the 'same phrase' -- they carry ON SIZE ERROR / NOT ON SIZE ERROR, not ON EXCEPTION. They are the same STRUCTURAL idiom (an exception phrase pair wrapped in a bracket with choice indicators) and they do preserve the bars, so they still corroborate; the phrase-level claim is just imprecise. The true same-phrase siblings are 618 CALL Fmt 1, 619 CALL Fmt 2, 640 DISPLAY, and the occurrences at ISO_COBOL.md lines 29816, 30635, 30652.

(2) The defect on page 607 is BROADER than the claim states, and the fix should cover both figures. The AT phrase directly above it (page-607.md line 8 / ISO_COBOL.md line 25264) has the identical defect with BRACES instead of brackets. Cropping the AT delimiters at 5x (crop_at_left.png = 450,580-580,1070; crop_at_right.png = 1350,580-1480,1070) shows an opening brace '{' followed by a detached, serif-less vertical bar, and on the right a detached vertical bar followed by '}' and then the outer ']'. The printed form is '[ AT {| [LINE NUMBER ...] / [{COLUMN|COL} NUMBER ...] |} ]'. The markdown renders it as \left\{ ... \right\} with no bars. Per 5.2.6.4, braces WITH choice indicators mean one or more of the enclosed alternatives, each at most once, in any order; braces WITHOUT them mean exactly one. The transcription therefore also makes the everyday 'AT LINE NUMBER 5 COLUMN NUMBER 10' form look illegal, permitting only LINE or only COLUMN. Both figures on page 607 lost their choice indicators and both should be repaired in the same edit, ideally with a figure-notes block matching the style used at ISO_COBOL.md line 25738.

### p607 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** In the ACCEPT Format 3 AT phrase both alternatives of the inner brace are underlined: 'COLUMN' (underlined) over 'COL' (underlined).
- **Markdown:** \left\{\begin{array}{l} \underline{\text{COLUMN}} \\ \text{COL} \end{array}\right\}
- **Why:** COL is a required (reserved) word abbreviation of COLUMN -- 14.9.1 prints it underlined, and 14.9.11 (page 640) records it in its figure note as underlined. The markdown underlines COLUMN but not COL in the very same brace, so a reader/implementor derives that COL is a user-defined word rather than a reserved alternative. Loss of underlining changes the grammar.
- **Repair:** Change \text{COL} to \underline{\text{COL}} in the 14.9.1.2 Format 3 figure.
- **Verifier:** Real, with one correction to the stated rationale. The lost underline on COL does NOT make it read as a user-defined word -- user-defined words are printed lowercase and hyphenated in general formats (identifier-4, integer-2). Under ISO 5.2.2 / 5.2.3 the actual misreading is required keyword vs OPTIONAL WORD: an uppercase word that is not underlined is by definition an optional word that may be omitted, so the markdown states that the second alternative of the COLUMN/COL brace can be written as nothing, and that COL is not required to select the functionality. COL is in fact the required minimum abbreviation of COLUMN (a keyword), as the transcription itself records at lines 17096, 17868 and 26514. Everything else in the claim -- the PDF underlines both, the markdown underlines only COLUMN, and the loss is normative -- is confirmed. Separately noted while verifying, and OUT OF SCOPE for this claim: the immediately following display block at specs/ISO_COBOL.md:25265 drops the underlines on EXCEPTION (both occurrences) and NOT, which the PDF prints underlined.

### p632 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** COMPUTE Format 1 (arithmetic-compute), 14.9.8.2. The SIZE ERROR block is printed as ONE tall square bracket spanning BOTH alternative lines, with a full-height vertical choice-indicator bar immediately inside each bracket:  [ |  ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1 / NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2  | ]  followed on the next line by  [ END-COMPUTE ] .
- **Markdown:** ```
⌈⌊ ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1        ⌉⌋
⌈⌊ NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2    ⌉⌋
[ END-COMPUTE ]
```
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6.4 a pair of vertical bars just inside a bracket means zero or more of the enclosed alternatives may be specified, each at most once, in any order. The transcription drops both bars and additionally repeats the SAME corner glyph pair (⌈⌊ / ⌉⌋) on BOTH rows, so instead of one bracket-with-choice-bars spanning two alternatives the markdown depicts two independent malformed brackets. The choice-indicator semantics are unrecoverable, and a reader following the bracket-without-bars convention would conclude at most ONE of ON SIZE ERROR / NOT ON SIZE ERROR may be written — falsely restrictive; COBOL permits both on one COMPUTE. Every sibling arithmetic statement in this same markdown file (ADD lines 25474-25475, DIVIDE 26635-26636, MULTIPLY 28741-28742, SUBTRACT 32084-32085) transcribes the identical PDF construct correctly as ⎡| ... |⎤ / ⎣| ... |⎦, which proves the convention exists and that COMPUTE is the outlier.
- **Repair:** Replace the two lines with the file's established choice-indicator rendering and pull the whole Format 1 into a single fenced block:
```
COMPUTE { identifier-1 [ rounded-phrase ] } ... = arithmetic-expression-1
  ⎡|  ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1      |⎤
  ⎣|  NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2  |⎦
  [ END-COMPUTE ]
```
- **Verifier:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 26210-26211 (page 632, 14.9.8.2 COMPUTE Format 1, arithmetic-compute) lose the choice indicators from the ON SIZE ERROR group. The PDF prints ONE tall square bracket spanning both stacked alternatives with a continuous full-height vertical bar immediately inside each bracket edge — the 5.2.6.4 choice-indicator notation meaning zero or more of the enclosed alternatives, each at most once, in any order. The markdown instead emits '⌈⌊ ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1 ⌉⌋' and '⌈⌊ NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2 ⌉⌋' — identical delimiters on both rows (U+2308 U+230A ... U+2309 U+230B), containing no vertical-bar character at all. Because the delimiters are identical on both rows, this is not a top/bottom corner split of one tall bracket but a doubled-corner OCR artifact that depicts two malformed per-row brackets. A reader applying the plain-bracket convention concludes at most ONE of ON SIZE ERROR / NOT ON SIZE ERROR may be written on a COMPUTE, which is falsely restrictive. No figure note compensates: unlike ADD (which carries an explicit 5.2.6.4 figure note at line 25482), the COMPUTE block has none. Fix by adopting the convention already used for the sibling arithmetic statements — ADD 25477-25478, DIVIDE 25493-25494 / 25507-25508 / 26638-26639, MULTIPLY 28744-28745, SUBTRACT 32087-32088 — namely '⎡|  ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1      |⎤' / '⎣|  NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2  |⎦', and add the matching figure note. Two corrections to the claim as filed: (a) its sibling line citations are off by about three lines (25474-25475, 26635-26636, 28741-28742, 32084-32085 are the identifier/literal brace rows, not the SIZE ERROR rows); (b) this COMPUTE block is not the only instance of the defect — DIVIDE Format 4 and Format 5 at lines 26705-26706 and 26719-26720 exhibit the same dropped-bar corruption with a different glyph set ('⌐⌐ ... ¬¬' / '⌊⌊ ... ┘┘'), so the fix should be swept across all of them. Separately and more minorly, the fenced block also drops the PDF's underlining of the required words SIZE, ERROR, NOT and END-COMPUTE, which sibling entries such as SUBTRACT record in a figure note (line 32092).

### p635 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** DELETE Format 1 (record), 14.9.10.2. The INVALID KEY block is printed as ONE tall square bracket spanning both alternatives with a full-height vertical choice-indicator bar immediately inside each bracket:  [ |  INVALID KEY imperative-statement-1 / NOT INVALID KEY imperative-statement-2  | ] .
- **Markdown:** ```
DELETE file-name-1  RECORD
  [ retry-phrase ]
  ⌐ INVALID KEY imperative-statement-1     ¬
  | NOT INVALID KEY imperative-statement-2 |
  [ END-DELETE ]
```
- **Why:** The two inner choice-indicator bars (5.2.6.4) are gone: the markdown has exactly one glyph per side per row, so the four printed vertical strokes per row collapse to one. Without the bars the bracket reads as 'at most one of the enclosed alternatives', which would forbid writing INVALID KEY and NOT INVALID KEY on the same DELETE statement. The standard permits both (14.9.10.4 GR 11 explicitly discusses 'the presence or absence of the optional INVALID KEY and NOT INVALID KEY phrases'), so the loss is toward falsely-restrictive syntax.
- **Repair:** Render as the file does elsewhere:
```
DELETE file-name-1 RECORD
  [ retry-phrase ]
  ⎡|  INVALID KEY imperative-statement-1      |⎤
  ⎣|  NOT INVALID KEY imperative-statement-2  |⎦
  [ END-DELETE ]
```
- **Verifier:** Real, and the claim UNDER-states it: the defect hits BOTH figures on page 635, not just Format 1. Format 2 (file) has the identical loss -- the PDF prints four strokes there too (measured at x=370 h=145 with 18px arms, x=391 h=157 bare, x=1314 h=157 bare, x=1337 h=134 with 17px arms), i.e. '[ | ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-3 / NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-4 | ]', while specs/ISO_COBOL.md:32-33 of the page slice (file lines 26332-26333) again gives only '⌐ ... ¬' / '| ... |'. So DELETE FILE likewise reads as forbidding ON EXCEPTION and NOT ON EXCEPTION together, when 5.2.6.4 permits both. Secondary precision fix: the markdown's row-2 '|' characters are the bracket sides, so the correct statement is not that 'four strokes collapse to one' in the sense of the bars surviving -- the two choice-indicator bars are deleted outright and the surviving glyph is the bracket. The fix for both figures is the transcription's own already-correct convention used at REWRITE/START/WRITE: '⎡ | ... | ⎤' over '⎣ | ... | ⎦'. Note also a related but distinct defect on another page, out of scope here but worth ledgering: READ (line 29483-29484) renders the same construct as '[ [ INVALID KEY ... ] ]', turning the choice bars into a second pair of brackets, which is also falsely restrictive.

### p635 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** DELETE Format 2 (file), 14.9.10.2. The EXCEPTION block is printed as ONE tall square bracket spanning both alternatives with a full-height vertical choice-indicator bar immediately inside each bracket:  [ |  ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-3 / NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-4  | ] .
- **Markdown:** ```
DELETE FILE [ OVERRIDE ] { file-name-1 } ...
  [ retry-phrase ]
  ⌐ ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-3     ¬
  | NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-4 |
  [ END-DELETE ]
```
- **Why:** Same lost choice indicator as Format 1. Without the bars the bracket reads as 'at most one of ON EXCEPTION / NOT ON EXCEPTION', but 14.9.10.4 GR 20 c)/d) and GR 21 b) describe behaviour in which both phrases may be present on the same DELETE FILE statement. Legal source is made to look illegal.
- **Repair:** Render as:
```
DELETE FILE [ OVERRIDE ] { file-name-1 } ...
  [ retry-phrase ]
  ⎡|  ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-3      |⎤
  ⎣|  NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-4  |⎦
  [ END-DELETE ]
```
- **Verifier:** Claim confirmed as stated; two refinements for precision. (1) The PDF glyphs are exactly as described - a full-height square bracket with serifs at top and bottom, plus a separate unserifed full-height vertical bar immediately inside it, on BOTH sides, spanning both stacked alternatives: '[| ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-3 / NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-4 |]'. The transcription drops both bars, and additionally degrades the bracket itself (it draws only the top corners '⌐'/'¬' with bare verticals below, never closing the bottom); the bracket degradation is cosmetic, the lost bars are the normative defect. (2) The strongest supporting citation is 14.9.10.4 GR 21 b), whose clause 'The ON EXCEPTION phrase is ignored, if it is specified' is unreachable unless both phrases may coexist; GR 20 c)/d) are merely consistent with that reading rather than decisive, since each speaks only to whether ON EXCEPTION is or is not specified. Governing notation rules are ISO 5.2.6.2 (brackets = at most one) and 5.2.6.4 (bars within brackets = zero or more, any order, each at most once). FIX: render the block using the convention the file already uses at ADD Format 1 (lines 25477-25478) - '⎡| ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-3 |⎤' / '⎣| NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-4 |⎦' - and attach a 'Figure notes (ISO 5.2.6.4)' annotation. The sibling defect in DELETE Format 1 (INVALID KEY / NOT INVALID KEY) on the same page is identical and must be fixed in the same change set.

### p644 · incorrect-text · **normative**

- **PDF:** In all three DIVIDE figures the exception rows print as 'ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1' / 'NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2' where SIZE and ERROR carry underline rules and NOT carries an underline rule, but ON is NOT underlined in either row (verified on the enlarged crop of the Format 1 block).
- **Markdown:** > **Figure notes (DIVIDE Format 1 (into) syntax diagram).** `DIVIDE`, `INTO`, `ON`, `SIZE`, `ERROR`, `NOT`, and `END-DIVIDE` are underlined in the printed standard (required words).  -- and identically in the Format 2 note ('`DIVIDE`, `INTO`, `GIVING`, `ON`, `SIZE`, `ERROR`, `NOT`, and `END-DIVIDE`') and the Format 3 note ('`DIVIDE`, `BY`, `GIVING`, `ON`, `SIZE`, `ERROR`, `NOT`, and `END-DIVIDE`')
- **Why:** ON is an optional word in the ON SIZE ERROR phrase -- 'SIZE ERROR ...' and 'NOT SIZE ERROR ...' are legal COBOL. Three figure notes on this page assert ON is a required word, which is the transcription's own annotation contradicting the printed glyphs and would drive an implementation to reject legal source. The correctly-written sibling notes on pages 618, 619 and 640 list only the genuinely underlined words and omit ON.
- **Repair:** Delete '`ON`, ' from the underlined-word list in all three DIVIDE figure notes on page 644, leaving DIVIDE / INTO / GIVING / BY / SIZE / ERROR / NOT / END-DIVIDE as appropriate per format.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed and accurately described; scope is slightly broader than the claim's stated verification. The claim verified Format 1 by eye; I verified ALL SIX exception rows (both rows in each of Formats 1, 2 and 3) both visually at 3x and by pixel-coverage measurement of the underline band. In every row ON has exactly 0.00 underline coverage while NOT, SIZE and ERROR score 0.94-0.98. Per the standard's own conventions -- §5.2.2 (keywords are underlined and required) and §5.2.3 (optional words are uppercase and not underlined) -- ON is an OPTIONAL word of the SIZE ERROR phrase, so `SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1` and `NOT SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2` are conforming. The defect is confined to the transcription's own editorial 'Figure notes' blockquotes at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 26640-26641 (Format 1), 26661-26662 (Format 2) and 26681-26682 (Format 3); the fenced general-format blocks themselves are correct. Fix: delete `ON` from the required-word enumeration in all three notes, leaving Format 1 as DIVIDE, INTO, SIZE, ERROR, NOT, END-DIVIDE; Format 2 as DIVIDE, INTO, GIVING, SIZE, ERROR, NOT, END-DIVIDE; Format 3 as DIVIDE, BY, GIVING, SIZE, ERROR, NOT, END-DIVIDE -- matching the correctly-written sibling notes on pages 618, 619 and 640, which I confirmed all omit ON for the structurally identical ON EXCEPTION / NOT ON EXCEPTION group. Worth sweeping every other figure note in the transcription that enumerates required words for an ON-introduced conditional phrase (ON SIZE ERROR, ON EXCEPTION, ON OVERFLOW, AT END, INVALID KEY), since the same annotation error is likely to recur on the ADD, SUBTRACT, MULTIPLY and COMPUTE pages.

### p645 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** DIVIDE { identifier-1 / literal-1 } INTO { identifier-2 / literal-2 }   -- both operand groups are printed with true BRACES (curly '{' ... '}'), stacked over two lines. Same in Format 5: DIVIDE { identifier-2 / literal-2 } BY { identifier-1 / literal-1 }.
- **Markdown:** ```
           ⌐ identifier-1 ¬        ⌐ identifier-2 ¬
DIVIDE     |              | INTO   |               |
           └ literal-1   ┘        └ literal-2    ┘
```  (identical corner-glyph delimiter used again in Format 5)
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6.2/5.2.6.3 a BRACE means exactly one of the stacked alternatives SHALL be chosen, while a BRACKET means at most one (the whole group may be omitted). The markdown renders the PDF's braces with the corner glyphs ⌐ ¬ └ ┘, which in this very document's own convention are the BRACKET shape — Formats 1, 2 and 3 of the same DIVIDE statement (page 644) render braces as ⎧⎨⎩ / ⎫⎬⎭ and brackets as ⎡⎣ / ⎤⎦. Read as brackets, Formats 4 and 5 would falsely permit 'DIVIDE INTO ...' with the dividend/divisor operand omitted. Formats 4 and 5 are the only DIVIDE formats in the file left in the un-normalized OCR glyph set.
- **Repair:** Re-render Format 4 and Format 5 with the file's brace convention, e.g.
```
           ⎧ identifier-1 ⎫      ⎧ identifier-2 ⎫
DIVIDE     ⎨              ⎬ INTO ⎨              ⎬
           ⎩ literal-1    ⎭      ⎩ literal-2    ⎭
```
and the mirror form for Format 5 (identifier-2/literal-2 BY identifier-1/literal-1).
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with the scope of the defect slightly wider than described. Page 645 is the only page where the two DIVIDE remainder formats survive in the un-normalized OCR glyph set, and that page loses TWO distinct normative choice indicators, not one.

(a) THE CLAIMED DEFECT, confirmed exactly as stated. The PDF prints true curly braces around the dividend/divisor stacks in Format 4 ('DIVIDE {identifier-1 / literal-1} INTO {identifier-2 / literal-2}') and Format 5 ('DIVIDE {identifier-2 / literal-2} BY {identifier-1 / literal-1}'). specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 26699-26701 and 26713-26715 render both with the corner glyphs U+2310/U+00AC over U+2514/U+2518 -- the square-cornered rectangle that is this file's BRACKET shape (cf. its normalized brackets U+23A1/23A3/23A4/23A6, and its normalized braces U+23A7/23A8/23A9 used for the very same operand groups in Formats 1-3 on page 644 at lines 26634-26636, 26653-26655, 26673-26675). Per 5.2.6.3 the brace makes the operand mandatory; read as the bracket of 5.2.6.2 it becomes omittable.

(b) AN AGGRAVATING DETAIL THE CLAIM DOES NOT MENTION. The middle row of the markdown's operand box is rendered with a pair of ASCII '|' characters ('DIVIDE     |              | INTO   |               |'). In this document '|' is itself a normative glyph -- the CHOICE INDICATOR of 5.2.6.4. So the transcribed operand group can be misread not merely as a bracket but as a bracket enclosing choice indicators, i.e. 'zero or more of identifier-1 / literal-1, in any order' -- a strictly worse reading than the plain-bracket one the claim describes.

(c) A SECOND LOST INDICATOR ON THE SAME PAGE. In the PDF, the ON SIZE ERROR group of Formats 4 and 5 is a BRACKET enclosing CHOICE INDICATOR bars ('[ | ON SIZE ERROR ... | NOT ON SIZE ERROR ... | ]'), which per 5.2.6.4 permits zero or more of the alternatives, each at most once, in any order -- so both handlers may appear on one statement. The markdown renders it as '⌐⌐ ... ¬¬' / '⌊⌊ ... ┘┘' (lines 26705-26706 and 26719-26720), collapsing bracket and bar into an undifferentiated doubled corner. Page 644 repaired exactly this for Formats 1-3 with an explicit 'Figure notes' warning citing 5.2.6.4; page 645 has no figure notes whatsoever, so Formats 4 and 5 lose both the brace marking and that compensating annotation.

Remedy: re-render both formats on page 645 using the file's normalized glyphs -- U+23A7/23A8/23A9 and U+23AB/23AC/23AD for the two operand stacks, U+23A1/23A3/23A4/23A6 plus explicit '|' bars for the ON SIZE ERROR group -- and add the same 'Figure notes' block (underlined required words plus the 5.2.6.4 choice-indicator warning) that Formats 1-3 already carry.

### p665 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** Format 1 (delimited):   IF condition-1 THEN statement-1 [ ELSE statement-2 ] END-IF   — with IF, ELSE and END-IF UNDERLINED and THEN, statement-1, statement-2 NOT underlined.
- **Markdown:** IF condition-1 THEN statement-1 [ ELSE statement-2 ] END-IF   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27465 — no <u> markup on any word)
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6.2 an underlined word in a general format is a REQUIRED word and a non-underlined uppercase word is an OPTIONAL word. This figure mixes both: IF / ELSE / END-IF are required, THEN is optional. With every underline dropped the required-vs-optional distinction is unrecoverable from the markdown, and a reader deriving the grammar from it cannot tell that THEN may be omitted while END-IF may not. The transcription DOES have a convention for this — 14.9.17.2 on page 660 renders '<u>GO</u> TO ... <u>DEPENDING</u> ON identifier-1' — so this is an omission, not a house style.
- **Repair:** Restore the underlines using the existing convention: '<u>IF</u> condition-1 THEN statement-1 [ <u>ELSE</u> statement-2 ] <u>END-IF</u>'.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with three corrections and one broadening. (a) Line reference: the markdown Format 1 line is specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27468, not 27465. (b) Citation: the underlined-equals-required rule is 5.2.2 (Keywords) together with 5.2.3 (Optional words), not 5.2.6.2 — 5.2.6 is 'Options', which governs brackets/braces/ellipses instead. (c) Scope: the defect is NOT confined to Format 1. Format 2 (historic) on the same page has the identical problem — the PDF prints 'IF' underlined, 'THEN' NOT underlined, 'ELSE' underlined, and 'NEXT' and 'SENTENCE' underlined in BOTH stacked alternatives, while markdown line 27472 ('IF condition-1 THEN { statement-1 / NEXT SENTENCE } [ ELSE { statement-2 / NEXT SENTENCE } ]') carries no <u> markup at all. So 14.9.19.2 loses the required/optional distinction in both of its general formats, dropping seven distinct underlines across the two figures (IF, ELSE, END-IF in Format 1; IF, ELSE, NEXT, SENTENCE in Format 2). Everything else in both figures — word order, bracket and brace structure, the '/' flattening of the stacked alternatives — transcribes correctly. Fix: restore <u> markup as '<u>IF</u> condition-1 THEN statement-1 [ <u>ELSE</u> statement-2 ] <u>END-IF</u>' and '<u>IF</u> condition-1 THEN { statement-1 / <u>NEXT</u> <u>SENTENCE</u> } [ <u>ELSE</u> { statement-2 / <u>NEXT</u> <u>SENTENCE</u> } ]', matching the convention already used at lines 27291/27295.

### p665 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** Format 2 (historic):   IF condition-1 THEN { statement-1 / NEXT SENTENCE } [ ELSE { statement-2 / NEXT SENTENCE } ]   — with IF, NEXT, SENTENCE (both occurrences) and ELSE UNDERLINED; THEN, statement-1, statement-2 NOT underlined.
- **Markdown:** IF condition-1 THEN { statement-1 / NEXT SENTENCE } [ ELSE { statement-2 / NEXT SENTENCE } ]   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27469 — no <u> markup on any word)
- **Why:** Same required-word loss as Format 1, and here it also erases that NEXT and SENTENCE are each individually underlined (both are required words of the NEXT SENTENCE phrase). The brace/bracket structure and the ASCII '/' rendering of the stacked alternatives are faithful; only the underlining is lost.
- **Repair:** Restore: '<u>IF</u> condition-1 THEN { statement-1 / <u>NEXT</u> <u>SENTENCE</u> } [ <u>ELSE</u> { statement-2 / <u>NEXT</u> <u>SENTENCE</u> } ]'.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with two corrections to the claim's framing. (a) Line number: the Format 2 line is specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27472, not 27469 (27469 is a blank line; 27468 is the Format 1 line, 27470 is 'Format 2 (historic):'). (b) Emphasis: the normatively load-bearing loss is the keyword/optional-word distinction mandated by 5.2.2 and 5.2.3 — that IF, ELSE, NEXT and SENTENCE are keywords (required) while THEN is an optional word (may be omitted). The claim's added stress on NEXT and SENTENCE being *individually* underlined rather than sharing one continuous rule is cosmetically accurate (the printed page does show two separate rules with a visible gap, in both occurrences) but carries no distinct normative weight: either rendering yields the same reading that both words are required. Accurate statement of the defect: specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27472 transcribes the clause 14.9.19.2 Format 2 general format as plain ASCII with no <u> markup and no compensating figure-notes annotation, dropping the underlines the printed standard sets under IF, ELSE, and both occurrences of NEXT and of SENTENCE, and thereby erasing the only signal that THEN — printed without an underline — is an optional word. The identical loss affects the Format 1 line at 27468 (IF, ELSE, END-IF underlined in print; THEN not). Suggested fix: mark up as 'IF condition-1 THEN { statement-1 / NEXT SENTENCE } [ ELSE { statement-2 / NEXT SENTENCE } ]' with <u> on IF, ELSE, and each NEXT and SENTENCE, or add a 'Figure notes' block naming the underlined words, matching the convention already used elsewhere in the transcription.

### p687 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** MERGE general format (14.9.24.2), COLLATING SEQUENCE alternative 2 is a brace carrying CHOICE INDICATOR bars just inside it: '{ | FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 | }' over '{ | FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2 | }' -- i.e. a tall '{' followed by a tall vertical bar on the left, and a tall vertical bar followed by the closing '}' on the right, enclosing the two FOR lines.
- **Markdown:** [ COLLATING SEQUENCE { IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]          } ]
                     { { FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 }          }
                     { { FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2     }          }
- **Why:** The vertical bars are the 5.2.6.4 choice indicator: with them, BOTH 'FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1' and 'FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2' may be specified, each at most once, in any order. Without them a brace around stacked alternatives means EXACTLY ONE. The transcription therefore makes 'COLLATING SEQUENCE FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alpha-1 FOR NATIONAL IS alpha-2' -- the normal case for a MERGE keyed on both alphanumeric and national items, and the case General rule 5a on page 660 explicitly describes -- look illegal, and would drive a compiler to reject legal source.
- **Repair:** Restore the choice indicator in the ASCII figure, e.g.
[ COLLATING SEQUENCE { IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]      } ]
                     { {| FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 |}   }
                     { {| FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2     |}   }
and note in the figure that the bars mean 'each at most once, in any order'.
- **Verifier:** The claim is accurate as written; no correction to its substance is needed. Two refinements for the ledger entry.

(1) Precise printed structure, for the fix: the outer delimiter is a BRACKET (whole clause optional); inside it a tall CURLY BRACE selects exactly one of two alternatives -- alternative 1 is the single line 'IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]', alternative 2 is a BRACE WITH CHOICE INDICATORS, i.e. one shorter curly '{' + bar on the left and bar + '}' on the right spanning BOTH FOR lines (it is one brace pair around the stack, not a separate brace per line as the ASCII stack currently suggests). Underlined (required) words in the printed figure: SEQUENCE, ALPHANUMERIC, NATIONAL; COLLATING, IS, and FOR are not underlined. A faithful transcription matching the already-reconciled SORT Format 1 style would be:

    [                      { IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]     } ]
    [ COLLATING SEQUENCE   { {| FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 |}  } ]
    [                      { {| FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2     |}  } ]

plus a figure note citing 5.2.6.4 and 14.9.24.4 general rule 5a.

(2) SIBLING DEFECTS found while verifying (out of scope for this claim, worth filing separately). (a) Same page 687, the OUTPUT PROCEDURE / GIVING group: the PDF encloses those two lines in a tall CURLY BRACE (exactly one shall be specified), but specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 28280-28282 render them as two independent optional BRACKETS '[ OUTPUT PROCEDURE ... ]' / '[ GIVING ... ]', which would wrongly permit neither or both. (b) SORT Format 2 (14.9.40, specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 31491, PDF page 776) has the identical missing-choice-indicator defect in its COLLATING SEQUENCE group. This is a pattern across the un-reconciled sort/merge figures, not a one-off.

### p687 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** MERGE general format (14.9.24.2), final group is a single tall BRACE spanning two lines:
{ OUTPUT PROCEDURE IS procedure-name-1 [ { THROUGH } procedure-name-2 ]
{                                        { THRU    }
{ GIVING { file-name-4 } ...
- **Markdown:** [ OUTPUT PROCEDURE IS procedure-name-1 [ { THROUGH } procedure-name-2 ] ]
[                                         { THRU    }                    ]
[ GIVING { file-name-4 } ...                                             ]
- **Why:** The PDF uses a brace, which per 5.2.6.2 means exactly one of the enclosed alternatives is REQUIRED: every MERGE statement shall specify either an OUTPUT PROCEDURE or a GIVING phrase. The markdown replaces the one brace with two independent brackets, which says both are optional and both may be omitted or both given. That both weakens a mandatory requirement (a MERGE with neither phrase would be accepted) and permits an illegal combination (OUTPUT PROCEDURE together with GIVING).
- **Repair:** Render the final group as one brace spanning both alternatives, e.g.
{ OUTPUT PROCEDURE IS procedure-name-1 [ { THROUGH } procedure-name-2 ] }
{                                        { THRU    }                    }
{ GIVING { file-name-4 } ...                                            }
- **Verifier:** REAL, with the rationale narrowed. Confirmed defect: in the MERGE general format (14.9.24.2, md lines 28280-28282), the tall CURLY BRACE that the PDF prints around the two mutually exclusive alternatives 'OUTPUT PROCEDURE IS procedure-name-1 [{THROUGH|THRU} procedure-name-2]' and 'GIVING {file-name-4}...' is transcribed as a tall SQUARE BRACKET. Per 5.2.6.3 the brace makes selection of one alternative mandatory; per 5.2.6.2 the bracket makes the whole group omissible. The transcription therefore permits a MERGE with neither an OUTPUT PROCEDURE nor a GIVING phrase, which the standard forbids, and no MERGE syntax rule (1-13) restores the requirement.

Correction to the claim's wording: the claim describes the markdown as 'two independent brackets' permitting OUTPUT PROCEDURE together with GIVING. The markdown actually repeats '[' at column 0 on all THREE lines with ']' near-aligned at columns 72/73/73 - the same per-line tall-delimiter convention used for the inner brace of the COLLATING group two lines above - so the natural reading is ONE tall bracket spanning the group, i.e. at-most-one, not both. The certain and unambiguous defect is required-choice degraded to optional. The 'both phrases permitted' consequence is only a secondary risk if a consumer reads the three '[...]' pairs as independent; it should not be asserted as the primary harm.

Suggested fix: redraw the group with the Unicode brace glyphs already used for the identical SORT Format 1 group at md lines 31467-31471 (U+23A7/23A8/23A9 and U+23AB/23AC/23AD), and add a figure note recording that per 5.2.6.3 exactly one of OUTPUT PROCEDURE / GIVING shall be specified and that the THROUGH/THRU bracket attaches only to the OUTPUT PROCEDURE alternative. Consider sweeping the whole file for other ASCII-bracket renderings of tall braces in general formats - this is a pattern, not a one-off.

### p703 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** In BOTH MULTIPLY general formats the ON SIZE ERROR block is printed as:  [| ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1 |] / [| NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2 |], where SIZE, ERROR, NOT and END-MULTIPLY carry underlines but the word ON does NOT (verified by a 900-dpi render of the clip; the underline strokes sit under SIZE and under ERROR, and under NOT on the second line, with clear white space under both occurrences of ON).
- **Markdown:** Figure note for Format 1: "`MULTIPLY`, `BY`, `ON`, `SIZE`, `ERROR`, `NOT`, and `END-MULTIPLY` are underlined in the printed standard (required words)."  Figure note for Format 2: "`MULTIPLY`, `BY`, `GIVING`, `ON`, `SIZE`, `ERROR`, `NOT`, and `END-MULTIPLY` are underlined in the printed standard (required words)."
- **Why:** The code-block figures carry no underlining, so these notes are the ONLY carrier of the required/optional word distinction on this page. Listing `ON` as underlined makes it a required word, which would reject the legal statements `MULTIPLY A BY B SIZE ERROR ...` and `MULTIPLY A BY B NOT SIZE ERROR ...`. This is exactly the falsely-restrictive direction the reconciliation is hunting: legal COBOL made to look illegal. The same defect is duplicated in both notes on the page.
- **Repair:** In both figure notes on page 703, delete `ON` from the list of underlined words and add an explicit statement that `ON` is printed WITHOUT an underline and is therefore an optional word in both `[ON] SIZE ERROR` and `NOT [ON] SIZE ERROR`. Sweep the whole transcription for the same wording: any other statement's SIZE ERROR figure note that lists `ON` as underlined has the identical defect (ADD, SUBTRACT, DIVIDE, COMPUTE, arithmetic statements generally).
- **Verifier:** Both MULTIPLY figure notes on page 703 wrongly list `ON` as underlined. Verified at 1200 dpi and by extracting the underline rectangles from the PDF vector layer: in Format 1 and Format 2 alike, the printed standard rules MULTIPLY, BY, (GIVING in Format 2), NOT, SIZE, ERROR and END-MULTIPLY, but leaves BOTH occurrences of ON unruled in each format -- four unruled ONs on the page. Each size-error line carries exactly one fewer underline stroke than it has reserved words, and the stroke widths match SIZE and ERROR (and NOT), not ON. Because the markdown code-block figures carry no underlining, the note is the sole carrier of the required/optional distinction, so this makes the optional word ON look required and would falsely reject the legal `MULTIPLY A BY B SIZE ERROR ...` and `MULTIPLY A BY B NOT SIZE ERROR ...`. Fix: drop `ON` from both lists and, matching the convention already used for SUBTRACT (specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 32089/32106/32121) and USE (line 32558), append "; `ON` is printed without an underline in both size-error lines". The identical defect is present in the three DIVIDE figure notes at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 26640, 26661 and 26681 and should be swept in the same change set.

### p722 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** READ statement, Format 1 (sequential): the AT END / NOT AT END group is printed as a tall BRACKET with a pair of vertical CHOICE-INDICATOR BARS just inside it, spanning both stacked alternatives:  [|  AT END imperative-statement-1  |]  /  [|  NOT AT END imperative-statement-2  |]
- **Markdown:** ```
  [ [ AT END imperative-statement-1         ] ]
  [ [ NOT AT END imperative-statement-2     ] ]
[ END-READ ]
```
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6.4 a pair of vertical bars just inside a BRACKET means zero or more of the enclosed alternatives may be specified, each at most once, in any order - so AT END and NOT AT END may BOTH appear on the same READ. The markdown replaced the bars with a nested bracket; under the transcription's own convention (a tall delimiter is written by repeating it on every row - cf. the { NEXT } / { PREVIOUS } and [ WITH LOCK ] / [ WITH NO LOCK ] groups in the same figure) that reads as bracket-inside-bracket, i.e. AT MOST ONE of AT END / NOT AT END. That is falsely restrictive and would make legal COBOL look illegal. It also contradicts GR 13 c on page 726, which describes the NOT AT END phrase being taken after a successful read of a statement that also carries an AT END phrase. The rest of the document uses bars for this construct (e.g. specs/ISO_COBOL.md:26324 '| NOT INVALID KEY imperative-statement-2 |', :25475 '⎣|  NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2  |⎦').
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 29461-29462 replace the nested brackets with the choice-indicator form already used elsewhere:
⎡|  AT END imperative-statement-1      |⎤
⎣|  NOT AT END imperative-statement-2  |⎦
and add the standard '> **Figure notes (ISO 5.2.6.4)**' callout stating that both AT END and NOT AT END may be specified, in any order.
- **Verifier:** Page 722, 14.9.30.2 READ statement general formats - the transcription drops the CHOICE-INDICATOR BARS from BOTH exception-phrase groups on the page, replacing each bar with a second bracket.

Format 1 (sequential), specs/ISO_COBOL.md:29464-29465. PDF prints a tall bracket with a pair of footless vertical bars just inside it, spanning both stacked alternatives:
  [|  AT END imperative-statement-1      |]
  [|  NOT AT END imperative-statement-2  |]
Markdown has '[ [ AT END imperative-statement-1 ] ]' / '[ [ NOT AT END imperative-statement-2 ] ]'.

Format 2 (random), specs/ISO_COBOL.md:29483-29484 - SAME defect, not mentioned in the original claim. PDF prints:
  [|  INVALID KEY imperative-statement-3      |]
  [|  NOT INVALID KEY imperative-statement-4  |]
Markdown has '[ [ INVALID KEY imperative-statement-3 ] ]' / '[ [ NOT INVALID KEY imperative-statement-4 ] ]'.

Evidence that the inner strokes are bars, not brackets: at 8x the inner vertical strokes are taller than the outer delimiters and have NO horizontal feet at top or bottom, whereas the outer '[' and ']' have feet - and the WITH LOCK / WITH NO LOCK group in the same figure, a genuine plain bracket, shows a single footed delimiter on each side with nothing inboard.

Normative impact: per 5.2.6.4, bars enclosed by brackets mean zero or more of the enclosed alternatives, each at most once, in any order - so one READ may carry BOTH AT END and NOT AT END (Format 1), or BOTH INVALID KEY and NOT INVALID KEY (Format 2), in either order. Per 5.2.6.2, the transcribed nested plain brackets read as at most one alternative, which is falsely restrictive and would reject legal COBOL.

Fix: render both groups in the house choice-indicator style already used throughout the file, e.g.
  ⎡|  AT END imperative-statement-1      |⎤
  ⎣|  NOT AT END imperative-statement-2  |⎦
and add a '**Figure notes (ISO 5.2.6.4)**' annotation of the kind attached to the ADD Format 1 and CALL Format 2 figures.

Caveat on the original rationale: the cited page-726 GR 13 c does not actually describe a READ carrying both an AT END and a NOT AT END phrase - it only says control passes to imperative-statement-2 when a NOT AT END / NOT INVALID KEY phrase is specified. The finding rests on the printed glyphs and 5.2.6.4, not on GR 13 c.

### p722 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** READ statement, Format 2 (random): the INVALID KEY / NOT INVALID KEY group is printed as a tall BRACKET with a pair of vertical CHOICE-INDICATOR BARS just inside it, spanning both stacked alternatives:  [|  INVALID KEY imperative-statement-3  |]  /  [|  NOT INVALID KEY imperative-statement-4  |]
- **Markdown:** ```
  [ [ INVALID KEY imperative-statement-3     ] ]
  [ [ NOT INVALID KEY imperative-statement-4 ] ]
[ END-READ ]
```
- **Why:** Same defect class as the Format 1 AT END group: the 5.2.6.4 choice indicators were replaced by a nested bracket, which reads as 'at most one of INVALID KEY / NOT INVALID KEY'. The standard permits BOTH on one READ, and GR 13 c (page 726) relies on that. The transcription renders this identical construct correctly for DELETE (specs/ISO_COBOL.md:26323-26324) and for the START/REWRITE/WRITE family (:30094, :31754, :32999).
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 29480-29481 replace the nested brackets with:
⎡|  INVALID KEY imperative-statement-3      |⎤
⎣|  NOT INVALID KEY imperative-statement-4  |⎦
and add the standard '> **Figure notes (ISO 5.2.6.4)**' callout.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed as described; no correction to the substance. Two refinements for the ledger. (1) The lost markup is a single pair of choice-indicator bars spanning the whole two-alternative stack, sitting just inside one tall outer bracket pair - i.e. one bracket group plus one bar group, not a per-line delimiter on each row; the markdown's per-line '[ ... ]' repetition is its normal way of drawing one tall delimiter, which is why it reads as a nested bracket rather than as two independent options. (2) Among the cited correct exemplars, REWRITE (:30094), START (:31754) and WRITE (:32999) are the clean ones, using '⎡ | ... | ⎤ / ⎣ | ... | ⎦'; DELETE (:26323-26324) preserves the bars but draws the outer bracket with corner glyphs ('⌐ ... ¬'), so it is a weaker precedent. The fix for READ Format 2 is to restore the bars in the REWRITE/START/WRITE form, and the same fix applies to the AT END group of READ Format 1 at :29464-29465.

### p738 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** 14.9.34.2 General format

RETURN file-name-1 RECORD [ INTO identifier-1 ]
   AT END imperative-statement-1
   [ NOT AT END imperative-statement-2 ]
   [ END-RETURN ]

(printed underlining, verified at 300 dpi: RETURN, INTO, END, NOT, END, END-RETURN are underlined; RECORD, AT and AT are NOT underlined; file-name-1 / identifier-1 / imperative-statement-1 / imperative-statement-2 are lower-case generic terms.)
- **Markdown:** ### 14.9.34.2 General format

`RETURN` file-name-1 `RECORD` [ `INTO` identifier-1 ]
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;`AT` `END` imperative-statement-1
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;[ `NOT` `AT` `END` imperative-statement-2 ]
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;[ `END-RETURN` ]

(no "Figure notes (...)" blockquote follows the figure)
- **Why:** The RETURN general format is the only general format in this page range and it is transcribed with a code-span-per-reserved-word convention that renders underlined and non-underlined words identically, then omits the compensating "Figure notes" blockquote that the rest of the transcription uses (see the REWRITE general format at 14.9.35.2, whose notes read "`RECORD`, `WITH`, and `KEY` are **not** underlined — they are optional words"). Per 5.2.6.2/5.2.6.3 a non-underlined reserved word is an OPTIONAL word that may be written or omitted with no effect on meaning. As transcribed, RECORD and both occurrences of AT read as required words, so an implementer working from the markdown would reject the legal forms `RETURN sm-file` (no RECORD), `RETURN sm-file RECORD END imp-stmt` (no AT) and `NOT END imp-stmt-2`. This is the same falsely-restrictive direction the transcription is known to drift in.
- **Repair:** Re-render the figure using the transcription's established convention: a fenced code block holding the four lines exactly as printed, followed by a blockquote — "**Figure notes (RETURN statement general format).** `RETURN`, `INTO`, `END`, `NOT`, `END`, and `END-RETURN` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). `RECORD` and both occurrences of `AT` are **not** underlined — they are optional words. No choice indicators appear in this figure; the `AT END` phrase is unbracketed and is therefore mandatory, while `NOT AT END` and `END-RETURN` are each optional and, per syntax rule 4, the AT END and NOT AT END phrases may be written in reversed order."
- **Verifier:** The finding is REAL and normative, with two corrections to the reported rationale.

CORRECTION 1 — wrong section cited. The claim cites '5.2.6.2/5.2.6.3' for the rule that a non-underlined reserved word is an optional word. Those sections are Brackets and Braces respectively and say nothing about underlining. The governing sections are 5.2.2 (Keywords — 'shown in uppercase and underlined in general formats ... They are required in order to select the functionality associated with that keyword') and 5.2.3 (Optional words — 'shown in uppercase and not underlined in general formats ... may be written to add clarity'), restated normatively at 8.3.2.4.2 (Required words) and 8.3.2.4.3 (Optional words). The substance of the claim survives; only the citation is wrong.

CORRECTION 2 — one listed 'legal form' is not legal. The claim says an implementer would wrongly reject `RETURN sm-file`. That form is genuinely illegal and its rejection is correct: the 'AT END imperative-statement-1' line carries NO brackets in the printed format (confirmed by the column profile — line 2 has exactly three glyph runs, AT / END / imperative-statement-1, with none of the bracket glyphs that appear at x~314 and x~1115 on lines 3 and 4). The AT END phrase is therefore mandatory on RETURN, unlike READ. The minimum legal RETURN is `RETURN file-name-1 END imperative-statement-1`.

The surviving, correct statement of impact: because RECORD and both AT occurrences are transcribed as though required, an implementer working from the markdown would wrongly reject at least these legal forms — `RETURN sm-file END imp-stmt-1` (RECORD omitted), `RETURN sm-file RECORD END imp-stmt-1` (AT omitted), and `NOT END imp-stmt-2` (AT omitted in the NOT AT END phrase) — plus every combination thereof.

Recommended repair, matching the convention used by the neighbouring 14.9.33.2 and 14.9.35.2: re-render 14.9.34.2 as a fenced code block and append a Figure notes blockquote, e.g. '**Figure notes (RETURN statement general format).** `RETURN`, `INTO`, `END`, `NOT`, and `END-RETURN` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). `RECORD` and both occurrences of `AT` are **not** underlined — they are optional words. The AT END phrase is not bracketed and is therefore required; the NOT AT END phrase and END-RETURN are bracketed and optional. Per syntax rule 4 the AT END and NOT AT END phrases may be written in reversed order.'

### p756 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** Format 1 (to-message-server): 'SEND TO { literal-1 / message-server-name-1 } FROM identifier-1' -- at 300 dpi (crop of the printed glyphs, and again for Format 2 'SEND TO  data-name-2  FROM identifier-1') the underline rule runs only beneath SEND and beneath FROM. There is a clear gap after the D of SEND and NO rule beneath TO in either format. (By contrast the SET formats on pp. 759-762 do print an underline beneath TO, so the omission here is deliberate typesetting, not a printing defect.)
- **Markdown:** ISO_COBOL.md line 30641 (Format 1 figure note): '... Underlined (required) words: SEND, TO, FROM, RETURNING, EXCEPTION, NOT, END-SEND.'  and line 30658 (Format 2 figure note): '... Underlined (required) words: SEND, TO, FROM, RAISING, EXCEPTION, LAST, NOT, END-SEND.'
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6.2 an underlined word is a REQUIRED word; a non-underlined uppercase word is an OPTIONAL word that may be omitted without changing meaning. Both SEND figure notes promote the optional word TO to required. A compiler implemented from this transcription would reject legal source such as SEND "SRV" FROM WS-MSG RETURNING WS-TAG ... END-SEND (TO omitted) -- the same falsely-restrictive direction as the known choice-indicator losses. The choice-indicator bars and every other claim in these two notes are correct; only the TO entry is wrong.
- **Repair:** In both figure notes on page 756 remove `TO` from the 'Underlined (required) words' list, and state explicitly that `TO` is NOT underlined (an optional word), matching the pattern already used on pp. 736/750/760/762 (e.g. "`AT` is not underlined", "`OF` is not underlined").
- **Verifier:** REAL, and the claim is accurate as written apart from one citation slip. Printed page 756 (ISO printed folio 726), 14.9.38.2 SEND general formats: in BOTH Format 1 (to-message-server) and Format 2 (message-server-response) the underline rule is present only beneath SEND and beneath FROM -- pixel-verified at 300 dpi, rules at y=1114/1115 spanning x=(306,404) and (1088,1195) in Format 1 and at y=1794/1795 spanning x=(320,417) and (791,898) in Format 2, versus TO occupying x=(424,474) and (438,487) respectively with no ink on the underline row. TO is therefore an OPTIONAL word in the SEND statement. The transcription's two figure notes at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 30641 and 30658 both list TO in 'Underlined (required) words:', promoting an optional word to a keyword. Because the fenced ASCII figure cannot render underlining, the note is the only place the required/optional distinction survives, so the error is fully load-bearing: a compiler built from the transcription would reject legal source such as SEND "SRV" FROM WS-MSG RETURNING WS-TAG END-SEND. FIX: delete 'TO, ' from both lists, and preferably add an explicit sentence to each note, e.g. 'TO and ON are printed WITHOUT an underline -- they are optional words.' CORRECTION TO THE CLAIM'S RATIONALE ONLY (does not affect the verdict): the underlining convention is not 5.2.6.2 (5.2.6 is 'Options'); it is 5.2.2 Keywords ('shown in uppercase and underlined ... They are required') together with 5.2.3 Optional words ('shown in uppercase and not underlined ... may be written to add clarity'). The claim's control observation is also confirmed: PDF page 759 (SET Formats 1 and 3) prints TO underlined, so p.756 is deliberate typesetting rather than a printing defect. Everything else asserted by the two notes matches the printed glyphs (ON not underlined; NOT, EXCEPTION, RETURNING, RAISING, LAST underlined; choice-indicator bars present inside the ON/NOT ON EXCEPTION bracket and absent from the RAISING brace). A sweep of the whole transcription finds this TO error only on these two lines.

### p776 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** SORT Format 2 (table), COLLATING SEQUENCE clause. The printed figure is an outer BRACKET containing 'COLLATING SEQUENCE' followed by a BRACE with two alternatives:  alternative 1 = 'IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]';  alternative 2 = an inner BRACE WITH CHOICE INDICATORS, i.e. a vertical bar just inside each side of the inner brace, spanning the two lines 'FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1' and 'FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2'. Rendered linearly:  [ COLLATING SEQUENCE { IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]  |  { | FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 | / | FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2 | } } ]
- **Markdown:** ```
SORT data-name-2 [ ON { ASCENDING  } KEY [ data-name-1 ] … ] …
                       { DESCENDING }

    [ WITH DUPLICATES IN ORDER ]

  [ COLLATING SEQUENCE { { IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]          } } ]
                       { { FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1             } }
                       { { FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2                 } }
```
- **Why:** Two separate losses, both toward falsely-restrictive syntax. (1) The choice-indicator bars just inside the inner brace are gone; per 5.2.6.4 those bars mean the enclosed alternatives may each be specified at most once, in any order, so 'FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1' and 'FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2' may BOTH appear. Without the bars the markdown reads as select-exactly-one, making a legal table SORT that sets both an alphanumeric and a national collating sequence look illegal. (2) The nesting is garbled: 'IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]' is shown inside the same inner brace as the two FOR lines, whereas the PDF makes it a sibling ALTERNATIVE to the whole bar-group. The identical construct in SORT Format 1 (page 775) has already been repaired in the markdown with box-drawing brackets plus a 'Figure notes (SORT Format 1)' paragraph; Format 2 was left unrepaired, so the two general formats of the same statement now contradict each other.
- **Repair:** Replace the Format 2 code fence with the same convention already used for Format 1 at page 775, e.g.

```
SORT data-name-2 ⎡ ON ⎧ ASCENDING  ⎫ KEY [ data-name-1 ] … ⎤ …
                 ⎣    ⎩ DESCENDING ⎭                       ⎦

    [ WITH DUPLICATES IN ORDER ]

⎡                      ⎧  IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]     ⎫ ⎤
⎢ COLLATING SEQUENCE   ⎨ ⎧| FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 |⎫   ⎬ ⎥
⎣                      ⎩ ⎩| FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2     |⎭   ⎭ ⎦
```

and add a 'Figure notes (SORT Format 2)' paragraph mirroring the one at page 775: the bar-group is a brace WITH choice indicators (5.2.6.4) so both FOR phrases may be specified in any order; it is one alternative of the enclosing brace, the other being the single-line 'IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]' form; the whole COLLATING SEQUENCE clause is optional; required words underlined in the printed figure are SORT, ASCENDING, DESCENDING, DUPLICATES, SEQUENCE, ALPHANUMERIC, NATIONAL.
- **Verifier:** The finding is real and the claim's prose description of the PDF is accurate; only the claim's ad-hoc linear rendering is loose and should not be copied into the repair. Two clarifications for whoever lands the fix.

(1) The claim's linear string reuses '|' both as an alternative separator between the two outer-brace alternatives and as the choice-indicator glyph, which is ambiguous. There is exactly ONE pair of tall vertical bars, one on each side, each spanning both FOR lines as a unit — not one bar per line. Use the page-775 convention instead.

(2) The inner brace in the PDF spans ONLY the two FOR lines; the markdown's inner brace wrongly spans all three. Stating the defect as 'nesting garbled' is correct, and the precise form is: the markdown promoted 'IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]' into the bar-group's brace, turning a two-way outer choice (single-line form vs. bar-group) into a three-way select-exactly-one.

RECOMMENDED REPAIR — replace the page-776 COLLATING SEQUENCE lines with the box-drawing form already used for Format 1 on page 775, so the two general formats agree:

  ⎡                      ⎧  IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]     ⎫ ⎤
  ⎢ COLLATING SEQUENCE   ⎨ ⎧| FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 |⎫   ⎬ ⎥
  ⎣                      ⎩ ⎩| FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2     |⎭   ⎭ ⎦

and append a 'Figure notes (SORT Format 2)' paragraph mirroring the Format 1 note: the second alternative is a brace with choice indicators, so per 5.2.6.4 one or more of the enclosed alternatives shall be specified, each at most once, in any order — FOR ALPHANUMERIC and FOR NATIONAL may BOTH appear, in either order; it is NOT a select-exactly-one brace; the enclosing brace chooses between that bar-group and the single-line 'IS alphabet-name-1 [ alphabet-name-2 ]' form; the whole clause is optional. Required words underlined in the printed Format 2 figure: SORT, ASCENDING, DESCENDING, DUPLICATES, SEQUENCE, ALPHANUMERIC, NATIONAL (COLLATING is not underlined). Note also that Format 2's KEY phrase is bracketed, 'KEY [ data-name-1 ] …', whereas Format 1's is braced, 'KEY { data-name-1 } …'; the markdown already has this right and it must not be 'harmonized' away while repairing the figure.

### p799 · incorrect-figure · **normative**

- **PDF:** In the 14.9.48.2 UNSTRING general format the ON OVERFLOW group is drawn as a tall BRACKET with a plain full-height VERTICAL BAR immediately inside each side (verified on a 300 dpi crop of the region: the outer glyph has square bracket serifs top and bottom, the inner glyph is a bare vertical rule with no serifs): "[| ON OVERFLOW imperative-statement-1 |]" over "[| NOT ON OVERFLOW imperative-statement-2 |]" as one two-row enclosure.
- **Markdown:** ```
⌐ ⌐                                        ¬ ¬
| |  ON OVERFLOW imperative-statement-1     | |
| | NOT ON OVERFLOW imperative-statement-2  | |
L L                                        ⌐ ⌐
```  (specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 32385-32390)
- **Why:** The inner enclosure is drawn with corner glyphs (⌐ top, L bottom) on both the outer AND the inner element, so the figure reads as two NESTED BRACKETS rather than a bracket carrying choice indicators. Per 5.2.6.4 a bracket pair enclosing stacked alternatives means AT MOST ONE of them, whereas a bracket with choice-indicator bars means zero or more, each at most once, in any order. As transcribed the figure says a UNSTRING statement may carry ON OVERFLOW or NOT ON OVERFLOW but never both - falsely restrictive, and the exact defect class already confirmed on GOBACK, CALL Format 2, STRING, START, SEND and RECEIVE. The sibling STRING statement (14.9.43.2, line 31964) renders the identical construct correctly as ⌈ | ... | ⌉ plus an explanatory figure note, so this is transcription drift, not a convention.
- **Repair:** Re-render the ON OVERFLOW group in the house style used at line 31964 (⌈ | ON OVERFLOW imperative-statement-1 | ⌉ / ⌊ | NOT ON OVERFLOW imperative-statement-2 | ⌋) and add the matching '> **Figure notes (5.2.6.4 choice indicators).**' paragraph stating that both phrases may be specified, each at most once, in any order.
- **Verifier:** REAL, and described accurately in substance. One citation correction: the fenced block is at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 32388-32393 (fence 32388, corner rows 32389 and 32392, text rows 32390-32391), not 32385-32390 as claimed - lines 32385-32386 are the '[ WITH POINTER identifier-7 ]' and '[ TALLYING IN identifier-8 ]' lines. The defect itself: page 799 prints the UNSTRING ON OVERFLOW / NOT ON OVERFLOW group as a single bracket pair enclosing a pair of choice-indicator bars ('[ | ... | ]'; outer glyph has top and bottom serifs, inner glyph is a bare serif-less vertical rule, confirmed at 6x on both sides), which per 5.2.6.4 means zero or more of the two phrases, each at most once, in any order. The markdown doubles the corner glyphs on both the top and bottom rows ('⌐ ⌐ ... ¬ ¬' / 'L L ... ⌐ ⌐'), producing two nested bracket pairs, which under 5.2.6.2/5.2.6.3 admits at most ONE of the two phrases. This falsely forbids 'UNSTRING ... ON OVERFLOW ... NOT ON OVERFLOW ... END-UNSTRING', a form the page's own General rules 16 e) and 17 presuppose is legal. FIX: render the block as '⎡ | ON OVERFLOW imperative-statement-1 | ⎤' / '⎣ | NOT ON OVERFLOW imperative-statement-2 | ⎦' and add the same 5.2.6.4 figure note the sibling STRING statement carries at line 31974, which UNSTRING currently lacks entirely. Same defect class as the already-confirmed GOBACK, CALL Format 2, STRING, START, SEND and RECEIVE findings.

### p18 · ocr-error · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.48  UNSTRING statement . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . 769
- **Markdown:** [14.9.48 UNSTRING statement](#section-14-9-48) …………………………………………………………………  [766](#page-766)
- **Why:** The TOC folio for the UNSTRING statement is transcribed as 766 instead of 769, and the markdown's cross-reference anchor was generated from the wrong number (#page-766). 766 is the folio of 14.9.46 TERMINATE, so following the link lands the reader three pages early, in the middle of the TERMINATE statement, rather than at the start of UNSTRING. The neighbouring entries confirm the digit is a transcription slip and not an ISO defect: 14.9.47 UNLOCK is 768 and 14.9.49 USE is 774, so 766 is out of the monotonically increasing sequence.
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md, on the page-18 TOC line for 14.9.48, change both the displayed folio and the anchor from 766 to 769: '[14.9.48 UNSTRING statement](#section-14-9-48) … [769](#page-769)'.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with the impact clause corrected. The transcription error itself is exactly as claimed: the printed TOC folio for 14.9.48 UNSTRING is 769, and page-18.md line 44 renders it as 766 in both the visible link text and the generated anchor (#page-766). The monotonicity argument holds (UNLOCK 768 < UNSTRING 769 < USE 774; 766 breaks the sequence), and the spec body independently confirms 769 via the <a id="page-799"> marker preceding the '## 14.9.48 UNSTRING statement' heading at specs/ISO_COBOL.md:32362. So this is a transcription slip, not an ISO defect. However, the claim's assertion that the bad link 'lands the reader three pages early, inside the TERMINATE statement' is incorrect. Body anchors in ISO_COBOL.md are numbered by PDF page, while TOC link targets are numbered by printed folio, so every TOC #page-NNN link is systematically off by the +30 front-matter offset. <a id="page-766"> is at line 31088 and heads content in the middle of the 14.9.39 SET statement (FORMAT 8, restricted data-pointer rules), i.e. printed folio ~736 -- not TERMINATE. The correct target for UNSTRING would be #page-799. Two consequences worth recording separately: (a) the folio digit error 769->766 is a real, page-local, correctable defect; (b) it produces a duplicate anchor reference, since line 42 (TERMINATE) and line 44 (UNSTRING) now both point at #page-766, which makes the error mechanically detectable. The broader TOC-anchor offset bug is pre-existing and systemic across the whole contents listing and should be tracked as its own item rather than folded into this one.

### p28 · other · **structural**

- **PDF:** (nothing — the PDF page contains only the running header, the Introduction body text, the two footnotes, and the page/copyright footer; there is no meta-commentary anywhere on the page)
- **Markdown:** Here is a summary and key excerpts from this page of ISO/IEC 1989:2023(E):

... (line 1281 of specs/ISO_COBOL.md) ...

I can quote specific short passages if needed, but I'm not able to reproduce the full page verbatim as a complete copyrighted standards document. I'm happy to help you find specific information from this page.
- **Why:** Transcription-tool meta-text and a refusal message are embedded in the document body as if they were spec content. Anything that greps specs/ISO_COBOL.md for normative language — the spec-lookup skill, a citation check, a human reading the file — will hit first-person assistant prose sitting inside ISO/IEC 1989:2023. It also marks the page as knowingly incomplete, so any future "the spec does not say X" conclusion drawn from page 28 is unsound. Verified with grep over the whole file: these two lines occur exactly once, at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 1255 and 1281, both inside the page-28 slice — the contamination is confined to this page.
- **Repair:** Delete specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 1255 and 1281 (and the surrounding `---` fences that frame the summary) as part of the page-28 re-transcription. Then re-run the grep for `not able to reproduce|Here is a summary` across the file to confirm zero remaining hits.
- **Verifier:** REAL, but the described scope is too narrow. The claim is factually correct as far as it goes — lines 1255 and 1281 of specs/ISO_COBOL.md are transcription-tool meta-text and an assistant refusal message embedded inside the page-28 slice, they occur exactly once each in the file, and nothing resembling them appears on the rendered PDF page. Severity 'structural' is right. The correction is to the extent: those two lines are not stray insertions around an otherwise-good transcription — they bracket a paraphrase that replaces the page entirely. Every one of the 26 lines between the page-28 and page-29 anchors is summary prose; not a single sentence of the printed Introduction body survives verbatim. Confirmed absent from the entire file: the reproduction-permission paragraph ('Any organization interested in reproducing the COBOL standard and specifications in whole or in part... requested to mention "COBOL" in acknowledgment of the source'), the industry-language paragraph ('COBOL is an industry language and is not the property of any company or group of companies, or of any organization or group of organizations.'), the no-warranty paragraph, the cross-reference sentence 'For more details and additional changes, see E.2, Substantive changes potentially affecting existing programs and E.3, Substantive changes probably not affecting existing programs.', the exact Annex-normativity sentence naming Annexes A/B/C by title, and both trademark footnotes (Sperry Rand, IBM). The remediation is therefore a full re-transcription of page 28 from the render, not a two-line deletion — deleting lines 1255 and 1281 alone would leave a paraphrase masquerading as a transcription and would remove the only marker that the page is incomplete.

### p34 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 3.10
alphanumeric character set
alphanumeric coded character set
coded character set that the implementor has designated for representation of data items of usage display and alphanumeric literals

(printed identically in style to 3.1 on page 33: the entry number in bold on its own line, the term in bold beneath it — both are numbered sub-clauses of Clause 3)
- **Markdown:** **3.10**
**alphanumeric character set**
**alphanumeric coded character set**
coded character set that the implementor has designated for representation of data items of usage display and alphanumeric literals

(cf. page 33, which renders the identical PDF construct as `### 3.1`, `### 3.2` ... `### 3.9`)
- **Why:** Term entries 3.10 through 3.38 are demoted from headings to plain bold text, while 3.1-3.9 on the immediately preceding page are `###` headings. The PDF prints all of them identically, so the markdown is treating one construct two ways across a page boundary. The demoted run spans pages 34, 35 and 36 (3.10-3.38, 29 definitions) and is unreachable by heading anchor or TOC, so a citation of the form "see 3.22, boolean data item" cannot be resolved by navigation — exactly the lookup pattern the spec-lookup skill depends on. Confirmed mechanically over specs/ISO_COBOL.md: lines 1412-1444 use `###`, lines 1456-1627 use `**n.nn**`.
- **Repair:** Promote `**3.10**` through `**3.38**` (specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 1456-1627) to `### 3.10` ... `### 3.38`, matching the `### 3.1`-`### 3.9` treatment on page 33, and drop the now-redundant bold markers on the number lines.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with two refinements to how it was described.

(1) The mismatch is an internal INCONSISTENCY in the transcription, not text lost from the PDF. In the PDF neither 3.1 nor 3.10 is typographically a heading: both entry numbers are body-size bold, visibly smaller than the true clause heading '3 Terms and definitions' on page 33. The transcription's `###` on 3.1-3.9 is therefore an interpretation (a defensible one - ISO terminological entries are numbered subclauses), and the page-34 bold form is the more literal one. What is indefensible is applying both to an identically-printed construct with the switch landing exactly on a page boundary the PDF does not mark. Whichever form is adopted, it must be adopted for all of Clause 3.

(2) The inconsistency is three-way and spans the whole clause, so a fix scoped to pages 34-36 would leave the defect in place. Measured over specs/ISO_COBOL.md, the 178 entries of Clause 3 use three different markers:
  - 3.1-3.9   -> `### n.nn`  (lines 1412-1444, page 33) - 9 entries
  - 3.10-3.38 -> `**n.nn**`  (lines 1456-1627, pages 34-36) - 29 entries, the run claimed
  - 3.39-3.57 -> `## n.nn`   (lines 1642-1728, page 37+) - h2, i.e. promoted to the SAME level as the parent clause '## 3 Terms and definitions'
  - 3.58-3.142  -> `**n.nn**` (lines 1739-2163)
  - 3.143-3.153 -> `## n.nn`  (lines 2174-2214) - h2 again
  - 3.154-3.178 -> `**n.nn**` (lines 2226-2364)
So 139 of 178 entries carry no heading at all and are unreachable by anchor, and a further 30 carry the wrong level (h2 nests them as siblings of Clause 3 rather than children, which corrupts any generated outline). The remediation is to normalize all 178 terminological entries to a single marker - `### n.nn` matching the existing 3.1-3.9 - not merely to promote 3.10-3.38.

Severity 'structural' is right. The consequence stated in the claim is understated rather than overstated: 'see 3.22, boolean data item' is unresolvable by navigation, and so are ~139 of the clause's 178 defined terms.

### p37 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 3.39
common program
program that, despite being directly contained within another program, can be called from any program directly or indirectly contained in that other program

(a numbered sub-clause of "3 Terms and definitions", printed in the same style as 3.1 and 3.10 — one level BELOW the clause-3 heading)
- **Markdown:** ## 3.39
**common program**
program that, despite being directly contained within another program, can be called from any program directly or indirectly contained in that other program

(cf. page 33: `## 3 Terms and definitions` — the parent clause — is also at `##`)
- **Why:** Term entries 3.39 through 3.47 are emitted at `##`, the SAME heading level as their parent clause heading `## 3 Terms and definitions` (page 33). In the document outline each definition becomes a sibling of Clause 3 rather than a child of it, so the terms-and-definitions subtree is flattened and clause 3 appears to end at 3.38. Together with the previous finding this means one PDF construct is transcribed three different ways within five pages: `###` for 3.1-3.9, bold for 3.10-3.38, `##` for 3.39-3.47. Confirmed mechanically over specs/ISO_COBOL.md: lines 1642-1678 use `## 3.39` ... `## 3.47`.
- **Repair:** Demote `## 3.39` through `## 3.47` (specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 1642-1678) to `### 3.39` ... `### 3.47` so all of 3.1-3.47 sit one level under `## 3 Terms and definitions`.
- **Verifier:** REAL, but the label and the scope both need correcting.

(a) 'missing-heading' is a mislabel. No heading is missing — '3.39' is present and its text is verbatim correct. The defect is a WRONG HEADING LEVEL: '## 3.39' collides with its parent '## 3 Terms and definitions'. The accurate kind is 'heading-level' (or 'heading-level-collision'). The claim's own prose describes the defect correctly; only the kind slug is wrong.

(b) The fix is determined by the file's own convention, which the claim does not state: top-level clauses are '##' and their one-dot subclauses are '###' (cf. '### 4.1', '### 5.1'). Entries 3.1-3.9 are already correctly at '### 3.N'. So '## 3.39'..'## 3.47' should each be '### 3.39'..'### 3.47' — matching the nine entries that were transcribed correctly at the top of the same clause.

(c) The scope is understated. '3.39 through 3.47' is exactly right FOR PAGE 37, but the '##' mis-level is not confined to that page: 30 term entries in clause 3 carry it, running from '## 3.39' (line 1642) to '## 3.153' (line 2214), interleaved with the bold form rather than forming one contiguous block. The clause-3 tally is 9 at '###' + 139 at bold '**3.N**' + 30 at '##' = 178 entries, so the three-way inconsistency the claim identifies is real but is a repeating interleaved pattern, not three clean contiguous runs.

(d) Corroborating context, in the finding's favour: the same heading-level instability recurs outside clause 3 — e.g. '## 4.2.5', '## 4.2.6', '## 4.2.7', '## 4.2.11'-'## 4.2.15' sit among siblings correctly emitted at '#### 4.2.4' / '#### 4.2.8', and '## 5.2.3'-'## 5.2.6' among '#### 5.2.2'. This is a systematic transcription defect in which heading depth was assigned per-run rather than from the clause number, which strengthens the finding: it should be remediated by deriving the level from the dotted clause number document-wide, not by patching page 37 alone.

### p52 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** Third-level clause headings in Clause 4 and Clause 5 are printed at a uniform rank throughout: '4.2.1  General', '4.2.2  Acceptance of standard language elements', '4.2.5  Implementor-defined language elements', '4.2.8  Reserved words', '4.2.11  Substitute or additional language elements', '5.2.1  General', '5.2.3  Optional words', '5.2.6  Options' are all set in the same size/weight, and second-level headings '4.3', '4.5', '5.2', '5.3', '5.4', '5.5' are likewise uniform with one another.
- **Markdown:** The transcription assigns those same-rank headings different markdown levels. In this batch: p52 '## 4.2.5 Implementor-defined language elements', '## 4.2.6 Processor-dependent language elements', '## 4.2.7 Optional language elements'; p54 '## 4.2.11', '## 4.2.12', '## 4.2.13', '## 4.2.14', '## 4.2.15'; p56 '## 4.5 Relationship of a conforming compilation group to a conforming implementation', '## 4.6 Relationship of a conforming run unit to a conforming implementation'; p58 '## 5.2.3 Optional words', '## 5.2.4 Operands', '## 5.2.5 Level numbers'; p59 '## 5.2.6 Options', '### 5.2.6.1 General', '### 5.2.6.2 Brackets', '### 5.2.6.3 Braces', '### 5.2.6.4 Choice indicators', '## 5.2.7 Ellipses', '## 5.2.8 Punctuation'; p60 '### 5.2.9 Special characters', '### 5.2.10 Meta-terms', '## 5.3 Rules', '### 5.3.1'..'### 5.3.5'; p61 '## 5.4 Arithmetic expressions', '### 5.4.1'..'### 5.4.3', '## 5.5 Integer operands'. Contrast their siblings, which the same file sets correctly: '#### 4.2.1 General', '#### 4.2.2 ...', '#### 4.2.3 ...', '#### 4.2.4 ...', '#### 4.2.8 Reserved words', '#### 4.2.9', '#### 4.2.10', '#### 4.2.16', '#### 4.2.17', '### 4.3 A conforming compilation group', '### 4.4 A conforming run unit', '### 5.1 General', '### 5.2 General formats', '#### 5.2.1 General', '#### 5.2.2 Keywords'.
- **Why:** Clause depth is the spec's addressing scheme and this file is the citation source of record ('cite the exact §'). Headings one or two levels too shallow break TOC nesting and any ancestry-based navigation: 4.2.5 through 4.2.7 and 4.2.11 through 4.2.15 detach from 4.2, 5.2.3 through 5.2.8 detach from 5.2, and 5.2.6.1-5.2.6.4 (the definitions of brackets, braces and choice indicators) end up at the same rank as 5.2 itself. At '##' these clause headings also collide with the '## Page N' scaffolding rank, so a section can be mistaken for a page marker. Verified against the live file: E:/CobolSharp/specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 2418, 2427, 2436 (4.2.5-4.2.7), 2481, 2486, 2495, 2504, 2513 (4.2.11-4.2.15), 2639, 2644, 2664, 2680, 2683-2698, 2703, 2710, 2726, 2731, 2736-2765, 2783-2803.
- **Repair:** Normalize to the convention the file already uses elsewhere — clause N = '##', N.M = '###', N.M.P = '####', N.M.P.Q = '#####'.
