Two corrections to the claim: (a) the PDF does not typographically distinguish depth-3 from depth-4 clause headings (rendering pages 319, 320 and 326 shows 12.3.7, 12.3.7.1, 12.3.7.2 and 12.3.7.4 all set in the same bold face and size), so the correct rank is established by the clause NUMBERING and by the transcription's own dominant convention (620 four-component headings at '###' vs 162 at '##'), not by any visible difference in the printed glyphs; (b) the sweep is far larger than the three headings named - '## 12.3.6.4 General rules' (line 13790) and '## 12.3.8.4 General rules' (line 14599) share the defect, and 162 four-component headings sit at h2 file-wide. The repair should be a single mechanical pass promoting every N.N.N.N heading to h3 (and re-checking the 24 at '####'), not a hand edit of 12.3.7.2/.3/.4.

### p335 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** where property-specifier is:  /  PROPERTY property-name-1 [ AS literal-4 ]   and   where user-defined-function-specifier is:  /  FUNCTION function-prototype-name-1 [ AS literal-5 ]   -- printed as general-format skeletons, with PROPERTY, AS, FUNCTION and the second AS UNDERLINED (required words); property-name-1, literal-4, function-prototype-name-1 and literal-5 are not underlined.
- **Markdown:** &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;PROPERTY property-name-1 [ AS literal-4 ]

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;FUNCTION function-prototype-name-1 [ AS literal-5 ]

(plain indented prose lines -- no code fence, no <u>...</u>, and no "Figure notes" paragraph naming the underlined words.)
- **Why:** The underline is the only marker that distinguishes a REQUIRED word from an optional/comment word that may be omitted. Because AS sits inside brackets, losing its underline turns "[ AS literal-4 ]" into a phrase whose AS could legally be dropped while literal-4 is written -- falsely permissive here, and in the general case the same loss reads as falsely restrictive. These are the last two of the six REPOSITORY specifier formats: the four printed on page 334 (class-, interface-, intrinsic-function- and program-specifier, lines 14462-14493) are each code-fenced WITH a Figure notes paragraph listing the underlined words, and pages 341 and 343 in this same batch mark inline formats with <u>FILE-CONTROL</u> and <u>SELECT</u> [ <u>OPTIONAL</u> ]. So the convention exists and only these two figures lost it.
- **Repair:** Render both as fenced general-format figures (or use <u>PROPERTY</u> / <u>AS</u> / <u>FUNCTION</u> inline as pages 341 and 343 do) and add the sibling-style note, e.g. "> **Figure notes (property-specifier syntax diagram).** `PROPERTY` and `AS` are underlined in the printed standard (required words). The `AS` phrase is optional." -- mirroring the program-specifier note at line 14493.
- **Verifier:** Accurate as filed; two refinements. (a) The loss is not only the underlines: the two formats also lost their code fence, so they are transcribed as ordinary indented prose (&nbsp; padding) rather than as general-format skeletons -- a reader or downstream tool cannot tell they are syntax diagrams at all. (b) The normative hook is the spec's own metalanguage, Sec 5.2.3 and Sec 8.3.2.4.3, which define an un-underlined uppercase word in a general format as an OPTIONAL word specifiable at the user's option. Under that rule the transcription makes three false claims per line, not one: that AS may be dropped while its literal is written, and that PROPERTY / FUNCTION -- the keywords that identify the specifier -- are themselves omittable. Fix: fence both formats and add the Figure notes paragraphs matching the four on page 334, i.e. 'PROPERTY and AS are underlined in the printed standard (required words); the AS phrase is optional' and 'FUNCTION and AS are underlined in the printed standard (required words); the AS phrase is optional'. Exact markdown locations: specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 14513 and 14517.

### p339 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** c)	the details are taken from the external repository for the function with the same name as the externalized name of the function prototype. That function is the one that will be activated.  [printed at the TOP of page 339 as a separate, indented list item c) of 12.3.8.4 general rule 11); item b) ends at the foot of page 338 with "... the same externalized name as the externalized name of the function prototype; otherwise,"]
- **Markdown:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 14648 (inside the page-338 slice): "    b) if the externalized name of the function prototype is the externalized name of a function prototype definition specified in the same compilation group, the details are taken from that function prototype definition and the details in the external repository are ignored. The function that will be activated is the one with the same externalized name as the externalized name of the function prototype; otherwise, c) the details are taken from the external repository for the function with the same name as the externalized name of the function prototype. That function is the one that will be activated."  -- and the page-339 slice contains NO item c) at all (it opens directly at "12) Within the scope of the containing environment division ...").
- **Why:** General rule 11 (how the details for activating a function through a function prototype are obtained) is an ordered a)/b)/c) fallback chain. Item c) has been glued into the body of item b) with a literal "c)" left mid-sentence, so the terminal fallback rule is no longer a list item and cannot be cited as 12.3.8.4 GR 11 c). The immediately preceding general rule 10 (the program-prototype twin, lines 14638 and 14640) keeps its b) and c) as separate items, which proves the intended structure and that this is a transcription break, not an ISO layout choice.
- **Repair:** Split line 14648 at "; otherwise, ": end item b) after "otherwise," and start a new list item on its own line, indented like line 14640 -- "    c) the details are taken from the external repository for the function with the same name as the externalized name of the function prototype. That function is the one that will be activated." Place it before the <a id="page-339"></a> anchor if it should stay with the page-338 slice, or move it under the page-339 heading to match where ISO prints it.
- **Verifier:** Accurate as filed; two refinements. (a) No text is lost -- the defect is purely the loss of the list-item boundary plus misattribution to the wrong page slice, so it is structural/citational rather than semantic: a reader can still recover the fallback chain from the run-on sentence, but 12.3.8.4 GR 11 c) is no longer an addressable item and now sits in the page-338 slice although the PDF prints it entirely on page 339. (b) The claim's supporting argument is stronger than stated: besides GR 10 retaining separate b)/c) at lines 14638/14640, line 14628 shows this transcription's normal page-break handling is to carry spilled text FORWARD into the new page's slice (GR 8's continuation onto page 338), whereas here the page-339 item was pulled BACKWARD into the page-338 slice -- an anomaly in the opposite direction from the convention. Repair: split line 14648 after 'function prototype; otherwise,' and move 'c) the details are taken from the external repository for the function with the same name as the externalized name of the function prototype. That function is the one that will be activated.' into the page-339 slice as a four-space-indented list item ahead of item 12), mirroring lines 14638/14640.

### p373 · missing-text · **structural**

- **PDF:** Formats 2 and 3 are printed as typeset general-format diagrams with required words UNDERLINED: FD, EXTERNAL, AS, GLOBAL, BLOCK, TO, RECORDS, CODE-SET, ALPHANUMERIC, NATIONAL, REPORT, REPORTS. Deliberately NOT underlined: IS, CONTAINS, CHARACTERS, FOR, ARE. (Confirmed at 300 dpi: 'CHARACTERS' has no rule beneath it while 'RECORDS' directly below it does; 'REPORT'/'REPORTS' are underlined while the following 'IS'/'ARE' are not.)
- **Markdown:** FD file-name-1

[ IS EXTERNAL [ AS literal-1 ] ]

[ IS GLOBAL ]

[ BLOCK CONTAINS [ integer-1 TO ] integer-2 { CHARACTERS / RECORDS } ]

[ record-clause ] .   ... (no code fence, and no 'Figure notes' paragraph anywhere for Format 2 or Format 3)
- **Why:** Underlining is the standard's only marking for required vs optional words, so dropping it erases the required/optional distinction for eleven words in these two formats — e.g. it no longer records that CHARACTERS is an optional word while RECORDS is required, or that IS may be omitted from 'IS EXTERNAL'. Page 372's Format 1 of the SAME entry does carry a Figure-notes paragraph listing its underlines, so the FD entry is now documented inconsistently across its three formats. The formats are also emitted as loose prose lines rather than inside a fenced block, unlike every other general format in clause 13.
- **Repair:** Wrap Format 2 and Format 3 in fenced code blocks in the same box-drawing style used for Format 1 on page 372, and add a 'Figure notes (File description entry Format 2 / Format 3)' paragraph listing the underlined words (FD, EXTERNAL, AS, GLOBAL, BLOCK, TO, RECORDS, CODE-SET, ALPHANUMERIC, NATIONAL, REPORT, REPORTS) and the non-underlined ones (IS, CONTAINS, CHARACTERS, FOR, ARE).
- **Verifier:** Page 373's Format 2 and Format 3 of the file description entry (13.4.5.2) are transcribed as loose prose with every trace of the printed diagram markup stripped, while the same entry's Format 1 on page 372 is fully documented. Two distinct losses, the second more severe than the claim reports.

LOSS 1 — required-word underlining (twelve words, not eleven). The print underlines FD, EXTERNAL, AS, GLOBAL, BLOCK, TO, RECORDS (Format 2 and 3), plus CODE-SET, ALPHANUMERIC, NATIONAL, REPORT, REPORTS (Format 3). It deliberately leaves IS, CONTAINS, CHARACTERS, FOR and ARE unruled. Verified at 300 dpi: 'CHARACTERS' has no rule while 'RECORDS' directly beneath it does; the rules under 'REPORT'/'REPORTS' stop before 'IS'/'ARE'. The markdown records none of this, so the reader cannot tell that CHARACTERS is an optional word, that RECORDS is required, or that 'IS' may be omitted from 'IS EXTERNAL' / 'IS GLOBAL'. This is a real omission and not a house style: clause 13 of the same file records underlining 41 times with inline '<u>' tags and 59 times with 'Figure notes' paragraphs, including page 372's Format 1 of this entry and the unfenced SD entry at 13.4.6.2 which still writes '<u>SD</u>'.

LOSS 2 — NORMATIVE, not merely typographic: Format 3's CODE-SET clause loses its CHOICE INDICATORS. In the print, the inner brace of the CODE-SET second alternative encloses 'FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1' and 'FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2' between visible vertical bars on both sides of the two phrases (clearly present in the 300 dpi crop). Per 5.2.6.4, braces enclosing choice indicators mean one or more of the enclosed alternatives shall be specified, each at most once, in any order — so a single CODE-SET clause may legally carry the FOR ALPHANUMERIC phrase alone, the FOR NATIONAL phrase alone, or BOTH, in either order. The markdown renders it as a plain either/or brace, '{ FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS alphabet-name-1 / FOR NATIONAL IS alphabet-name-2 }', which reads as 'specify exactly one'. Page 372's Figure notes call out this exact construct in Format 1 and state that both phrases are permitted, so the transcription now contradicts itself on the legality of 'CODE-SET FOR ALPHANUMERIC IS a1 FOR NATIONAL IS a2' in a report FD. Severity should be raised from structural to semantic on the strength of this second loss.

The missing code fence is real but incidental — many clause-13 general formats are unfenced prose and still carry their markup; the fix is to fence Formats 2 and 3 as page 372 does and attach a Figure-notes paragraph listing the twelve underlined words and the CODE-SET choice indicators.

### p388 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** RD report-name-1
  [ IS GLOBAL ]
  [code-clause]
  [control-clause]
  [page-clause]  .

(printed page 358 / PDF page 388. In the printed figure the word 'RD' carries an underline and, inside the first bracket, 'GLOBAL' carries an underline while 'IS' does not.)
- **Markdown:** RD report-name-1

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;[IS GLOBAL]

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;[code-clause]

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;[control-clause]

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;[page-clause] .
- **Why:** Per 5.2.5 (keywords are shown underlined; optional words are shown not underlined), the underline is the ONLY thing distinguishing the required keyword GLOBAL from the optional word IS, and RD from a non-keyword. With both underlines dropped, the figure no longer records which words are required, so a reader cannot tell from the transcription that 'RD report-name-1 GLOBAL.' is legal without IS. The transcription does preserve this markup elsewhere in the same batch (page 376 has '<u>SD</u>', page 402 '<u>ALIGNED</u>', page 404 '<u>AUTO</u>', page 406 '<u>BASED</u>', page 410 '<u>BLINK</u>'), so this page is an outlier, not a document-wide convention.
- **Repair:** Restore the required-word underlines in the 13.14.2 general format: '<u>RD</u> report-name-1' and '[ IS <u>GLOBAL</u> ]'. Optionally add a 'Figure notes' blockquote in the style used elsewhere in the transcription recording that RD and GLOBAL are underlined, IS is not, the four brackets are independent options with no choice indicators, and the terminating period is printed outside the last bracket.
- **Verifier:** Real, and the quoting of both sources is accurate; one refinement to the characterization. The kind label 'incorrect-figure' slightly overstates the defect: the figure's STRUCTURE is transcribed correctly -- the five lines, the four bracket pairs, the clause order, the indentation and the separator period outside the final bracket all match the printed page. The defect is confined to lost underline (keyword vs optional-word) markup on exactly two words: RD and GLOBAL. A more precise label is 'lost general-format underline markup' at structural severity, since 5.2.5 makes underlining part of the general-format meta-language alongside brackets and braces.

One addition strengthens the claim beyond what it states: the transcription preserves this data by TWO conventions, not one. Besides the inline '<u>' spans the claim cites (376, 402, 404, 406, 410), it also uses a prose 'Figure notes' block that enumerates the underlined words -- see line 15986 for the page-372 FD entry, whose printed figure underlines FD and GLOBAL just as page 388 does. Page 388 uses neither convention. Most pointedly, line 18726 (page 438, the standalone GLOBAL clause, 13.18.27.2) transcribes the identical construct as 'IS <u>GLOBAL</u>', so the transcription contradicts itself between page 388 and page 438 on the same clause.

Suggested fix, matching the page-376 inline house style: '<u>RD</u> report-name-1' and '[IS <u>GLOBAL</u>]'. Alternatively add a Figure notes block in the page-372 style recording that RD and GLOBAL are underlined in the printed standard and IS is not.

### p389 · missing-text · **structural**

- **PDF:** 13.15.2 General format is a typeset diagram with required words UNDERLINED: USAGE, DISPLAY, NATIONAL, BLANK, ZERO, PRESENT, WHEN (in PRESENT WHEN), GROUP, OCCURS, TO, DEPENDING, STEP. Deliberately NOT underlined: IS, WHEN (in BLANK WHEN ZERO), INDICATE, TIMES, ON. (Confirmed at 300 dpi: in '[ BLANK WHEN ZERO ]' only BLANK and ZERO carry rules; in '[ GROUP INDICATE ]' only GROUP does; in '[ PRESENT WHEN condition-1 ]' both PRESENT and WHEN do.)
- **Markdown:** ```
level-number [ entry-name-clause ]
  ...
  [ BLANK WHEN ZERO ]
  ...
  [ GROUP INDICATE ]
  [ OCCURS [ integer-1 TO ] integer-2 TIMES [ DEPENDING ON data-name-1 ] [ STEP integer-3 ] ]
  [ varying-clause ] .
```    (no 'Figure notes' paragraph follows the figure)
- **Why:** Underlining is the standard's sole marker of required words, so its loss erases the required/optional distinction for this format — notably that WHEN is REQUIRED in 'PRESENT WHEN' but OPTIONAL in 'BLANK WHEN ZERO', and that INDICATE and TIMES are optional words. Sibling general formats in the same clause (pages 369, 371, 377, 379, 381, 385) all carry a Figure-notes paragraph recording exactly this, so page 389 is the outlier.
- **Repair:** Add a 'Figure notes (Report group description entry general format)' paragraph listing the underlined words (USAGE, DISPLAY, NATIONAL, BLANK, ZERO, PRESENT, WHEN, GROUP, OCCURS, TO, DEPENDING, STEP) and the non-underlined ones (IS, WHEN in BLANK WHEN ZERO, INDICATE, TIMES, ON), and note that the source/sum/value group is a plain bracket with no choice indicators.
- **Verifier:** Real, but re-scope the rationale. GENUINE: page 389's 13.15.2 fence preserves every word, bracket and brace of the printed format yet records none of its underlining, and unlike every sibling general format in clause 13 it carries neither a 'Figure notes' blockquote nor <u> markup. So the required/optional word distinction is unrecoverable FROM 13.15.2 ITSELF - including the pointed asymmetry that WHEN is a required word in PRESENT WHEN but an optional word in BLANK WHEN ZERO, and that IS, INDICATE, TIMES and ON are optional. Verified underline inventory: ruled = USAGE, DISPLAY, NATIONAL, BLANK, ZERO, PRESENT, WHEN (PRESENT WHEN only), GROUP, OCCURS, TO, DEPENDING, STEP; unruled = IS, WHEN (BLANK WHEN ZERO), INDICATE, TIMES, ON. OVERSTATED IN THE CLAIM, two corrections: (a) 'page 389 is the outlier' is too strong - 13.14.2 on page 388 (report description entry, lines 16636-16644) likewise carries no underline record and is not even fenced, and OCCURS Format 3, the report-writer STEP format at line 19557, is also transcribed bare; the omission is a small cluster in this stretch, not a unique singleton; (b) the specific facts are partly recoverable elsewhere in the transcription, since 13.18.8.2, 13.18.28.2 and 13.18.41.2 do preserve BLANK/ZERO, GROUP and PRESENT/WHEN via <u> markup - so the WHEN asymmetry is reconstructible from those subclauses, which lowers the practical severity from 'normative information destroyed' to 'normative marking absent where the composite format is read'. The one item recorded nowhere is STEP's status as a required word. FIX: add a Figure-notes blockquote after the 13.15.2 fence listing the ruled and unruled words above, and note that the source/sum/value-clause stack is bracketed (at most one) with no choice indicators. ADJACENT, NOT PART OF THIS CLAIM but worth a separate finding: the PDF wraps '[ USAGE IS ] { DISPLAY | NATIONAL }' in an OUTER square bracket making the whole USAGE clause optional, and the markdown (lines 16694-16695) drops that outer bracket, so the transcription reads as if a DISPLAY-or-NATIONAL choice were mandatory in every report group description entry.

### p390 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** PDF page 390 (printed 360) ends syntax rule 14 with:
"c)  If the literal is national, 'PICTURE N(length)'"
and nothing further. PDF page 391 (printed 361) then opens with a SEPARATE paragraph, set at the subrule indent and NOT lettered:
"where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals."
followed by "15) If BLANK WHEN ZERO or JUSTIFIED is specified, ...".
- **Markdown:**    c) If the literal is national, 'PICTURE N(length)' where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals.
- **Why:** The 'where length is ...' sentence is the closing definition for ALL THREE implied PICTURE strings of syntax rule 14 (X(length), 1(length), N(length)). Splicing it onto the tail of subrule c) makes it read as if 'length' were defined only for the national case, leaving 'length' undefined for the alphanumeric and boolean subrules. The transcription itself renders the identical construct correctly at 13.16.3 syntax rule 9 (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 16953), where the same sentence stands as its own indented paragraph after subrule c), which confirms the p390 rendering is the defect and not the convention.
- **Repair:** Split the sentence out of subrule c). Leave 'c) If the literal is national, 'PICTURE N(length)'' as the last subrule of rule 14 in the page-390 slice, and place 'where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals.' as its own indented paragraph at the head of the page-391 slice, immediately before syntax rule 15 - matching both the printed page break and the existing correct pattern at 13.16.3 rule 9.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed, with one refinement to the claim's typographic description. On PDF p391 the paragraph 'where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals.' is set at x=407, which is the SUBRULE-LETTER / rule-14 hanging-body indent -- it is outdented from the subrule TEXT indent (x=483 on that page's margins) where a continuation of subrule c) would sit, and it carries no letter marker. So it is not merely 'at the subrule indent'; it is a hanging closing paragraph of syntax rule 14 as a whole, which strengthens rather than weakens the claim that it governs all three implied PICTURE strings. Fix: in specs/ISO_COBOL.md, split line 16769 so that 13.15.3 syntax rule 14 reads

   c) If the literal is national, 'PICTURE N(length)'

   where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals.

matching the already-correct rendering at 13.16.3 SR 9 (line 16953) and 13.17.3 SR 10 (line 17251). The standalone sentence belongs to the p390 rule-14 block even though the PDF prints it at the top of p391, so it should be placed before the <a id="page-391"></a> anchor.

### p391 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** Page 391 opens with a stand-alone hanging paragraph, indented to the level of the a)/b)/c) sub-item text of syntax rule 14) which ends on page 390: 'where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals.'  Page 390's sub-item c) ends at "If the literal is national, 'PICTURE N(length)'" with no trailing text (verified at 300 dpi).
- **Markdown:** The page-391 slice begins directly at '15) If BLANK WHEN ZERO or JUSTIFIED is specified, a COLUMN clause shall also be specified.' The sentence instead appears glued onto page 390's sub-item c):  "c) If the literal is national, 'PICTURE N(length)' where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals."
- **Why:** In the PDF the paragraph is a hanging qualifier for ALL THREE implied-PICTURE cases a) X(length), b) 1(length) and c) N(length); merged into c) it reads as defining 'length' only for the national case, leaving the alphanumeric and boolean cases with an undefined 'length'. It also mis-attributes page-391 text to page 390, so a page-anchored citation to this sentence lands on the wrong page. Note the same sentence is transcribed correctly as a stand-alone paragraph elsewhere in the file (two other occurrences), so this is a local regression, not a house style.
- **Repair:** Split the sentence back out: end page 390's item c) at "'PICTURE N(length)'" and open the page-391 slice with the stand-alone paragraph 'where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals.' indented as a continuation of rule 14).
- **Verifier:** REAL, with two corrections to the description.

What is actually wrong: the sentence 'where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals.' is a stand-alone paragraph whose sole location in the PDF is the first body line of page 391 (printed 361), closing syntax rule 14) of 13.15.3. ISO_COBOL.md instead appends it to the end of sub-item c) inside the page-390 slice (ISO_COBOL.md:16769), so the page-391 slice opens at '15) If BLANK WHEN ZERO...'. Two defects: the paragraph break is lost (fused into c)), and the content is attributed to page 390 instead of 391.

Correction 1 -- indent level. The claim says the paragraph is 'indented to the level of the a)/b)/c) sub-item text'. It is not. Measured at 300 dpi and de-shifted for the recto/verso mirrored margin (+71 px on p391), it sits at x=407 = the a)/b)/c) LABEL column (340 in p390's frame), which is also rule 14)'s own body/continuation column. The sub-item text column would be 484 on p391. The paragraph is thus one level OUTDENTED from a)/b)/c)'s text, at the level that terminates rule 14) as a whole -- which is precisely why it governs all three implied PICTUREs. The file's own two correct transcriptions (ISO_COBOL.md:16953 on page 395, ISO_COBOL.md:17251 on page 401) place it at exactly that indent, level with their a) label, confirming both the geometry and the intended house style.

Correction 2 -- nothing is missing. Every word is present in the markdown. This is a scoping and page-attribution defect, not an omission: the normative definition of 'length' is demoted from qualifying a) X(length), b) 1(length) and c) N(length) to qualifying only c) N(length), and a page-anchored citation to it resolves to page-390 instead of page-391.

Correct fix: delete " where length is the length of the literal as specified in 8.3.3, Literals." from ISO_COBOL.md:16769 so c) ends at "'PICTURE N(length)'", and emit the sentence as its own paragraph at the top of the page-391 slice, indented three spaces to match the '   a)' / '   b)' / '   c)' labels above it -- i.e. the same shape already used at ISO_COBOL.md:16953.

### p399 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **Markdown:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 17157, inside the Page 399 slice: "where entry-name-clause is described in 13.18.20, Entry-name clause where screen-attribute-clauses is:"

- **Repair:** Split line 17157 into two paragraphs: leave "where entry-name-clause is described in 13.18.20, Entry-name clause" as the last line of the Page 399 slice, and move "where screen-attribute-clauses is:" below the '<a id="page-400"></a>' / '## Page 400' scaffolding, immediately before the screen-attribute-clauses code fence (currently at lines 17164-17181).
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, and the claim is accurate as written. Precise form: specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 17157 concatenates two sentences that the PDF prints on separate pages -- 'where entry-name-clause is described in 13.18.20, Entry-name clause' (last body line of PDF page 399 / printed 369, closing 13.17.2 Format 2) and 'where screen-attribute-clauses is:' (first body line of PDF page 400 / printed 370, introducing the screen-attribute-clauses figure). Two aggravating details worth recording: (a) the merged line is the ONLY occurrence of 'where screen-attribute-clauses is' in the entire markdown, so the page-400 figure has no lead-in sentence anywhere -- the '## Page 400' slice opens directly with the fenced figure; and (b) the merged sentence is grammatically well-formed, so the corruption is silent to a reader and misattributes the content of 13.18.20. Fix: split into two lines and move 'where screen-attribute-clauses is:' below the '<a id="page-400"></a>' / '## Page 400' boundary, immediately above the screen-attribute-clauses fence, matching the sibling pattern already correct for 'where picture-clause is described in 13.18.40, PICTURE clause.' and 'where source-destination-clauses is:'.

### p400 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** Page 400 opens with its own paragraph: "where screen-attribute-clauses is:" immediately above the BELL / BLINK / HIGHLIGHT-LOWLIGHT / REVERSE-VIDEO / UNDERLINE / FOREGROUND-COLOR / BACKGROUND-COLOR figure. Page 399 ends with a separate, unrelated paragraph: "where entry-name-clause is described in 13.18.20, Entry-name clause".
- **Markdown:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 17157 (before the <a id="page-400"></a> anchor) reads as ONE run-on line: "where entry-name-clause is described in 13.18.20, Entry-name clause where screen-attribute-clauses is:" — and the page-400 region then jumps straight from the "# ISO/IEC 1989:2023 (E)" running header into the ``` fence with no introducer.
- **Why:** The sentence that names what the figure defines has been fused onto the tail of the previous page's unrelated sentence, producing a nonsensical run-on and leaving the screen-attribute-clauses figure with no caption. Any tool or reader slicing the transcription by page loses the binding between the name `screen-attribute-clauses` (referenced from the Format 1 diagram on page 398) and the diagram that defines it, and the fused sentence misreads as though entry-name-clause were described 'where screen-attribute-clauses is'.
- **Repair:** Split line 17157 into two paragraphs: leave "where entry-name-clause is described in 13.18.20, Entry-name clause" on the page-399 side, and move "where screen-attribute-clauses is:" to immediately after the page-400 running header, directly above the ``` fence.
- **Verifier:** Real, and described accurately, with two refinements of nuance. (a) The defect is a lost PARAGRAPH BOUNDARY, not lost text: every word of both sentences is present. Two distinct spec paragraphs - the last body line of page 399, 'where entry-name-clause is described in 13.18.20, Entry-name clause', and the first body line of page 400, 'where screen-attribute-clauses is:' - are fused into a single markdown line (17157) separated only by a space. The correct repair is to split line 17157 into two paragraphs and move the second one to AFTER the <a id="page-400"></a> anchor and the '# ISO/IEC 1989:2023 (E)' running header, immediately above the opening ``` fence at line 17164, matching the file's own convention at lines 17185 and 17187. Note that neither sentence takes a terminating period in the printed standard, so no period should be introduced when splitting. (b) The figure is not left entirely unnamed in the transcription: the editorial block immediately below the fence begins '> **Figure notes (screen-attribute-clauses syntax diagram).**', so a reader of the page-400 slice can still recover the name. That is a transcriber's annotation rather than spec text, however, so the spec's OWN introducer sentence is still misattributed to page 399 and the structural severity rating stands.

### p400 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 13.17.3   Syntax rules — printed at the same heading depth as 13.17.1 General, 13.17.2 General formats and 13.17.4 General rules (all three-component subclause numbers under 13.17).
- **Markdown:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 17201: "## 13.17.3 Syntax rules" — an H2, while its siblings are H3: line 17051 "### 13.17.1 General", line 17056 "### 13.17.2 General formats", line 17254 "### 13.17.4 General rules", under line 17048 "## 13.17 Screen description entry".
- **Why:** 13.17.3 is promoted a level, so it renders as a peer of the clause 13.17 itself rather than as one of its subclauses. Any generated table of contents, anchor map, or section-extraction tool that walks heading depth will place the Syntax rules of the screen description entry outside 13.17, breaking §-citation lookups for exactly the rules a compiler needs (e.g. 13.17.3 SR 1 on entry-name-clause ordering).
- **Repair:** Change line 17201 to "### 13.17.3 Syntax rules" to match 13.17.1, 13.17.2 and 13.17.4.
- **Verifier:** Not a missing heading — a heading-DEPTH error, and a document-wide one. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 17201 writes '## 13.17.3 Syntax rules' (H2) where the PDF's embedded bookmark outline records '13.17.3 Syntax rules' at level 3, alongside 13.17.1 / 13.17.2 / 13.17.4 (level 3) under 13.17 Screen description entry (level 2); the markdown's own siblings at lines 17051 / 17056 / 17254 are correctly H3, so the file contradicts itself. The printed page carries no type-size cue — 13.17 through 13.17.4 are all Cambria,Bold 11.67 pt — so depth comes from the clause number and the outline, not the glyphs. Impact is narrower than claimed: the '<a id="section-13-17-3"></a>' anchor and the contents entry at line 657 are both correct, so §-citation lookup still resolves; only heading-depth-walking tools misplace the clause, and those are already confounded by the '## Page NNN' H2 page markers. Scope is far wider than claimed: 1334 numbered headings across the file sit shallower than their PDF outline level (13.16.2/.3/.4 and the whole 13.18.2–13.18.64 run behave identically), so this should be closed by one mechanical re-derivation of heading depth from the PDF outline, not by a one-line edit at 17201.

### p460 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 13.18.38.2 General formats, 'Format 3 (report-writer):' is printed as a general-format figure in the diagram font with four words UNDERLINED: 'OCCURS [ integer-1 TO ] integer-2 TIMES [ DEPENDING ON data-name-1 ] [ STEP integer-3 ]' - OCCURS, TO, DEPENDING and STEP each carry an underline rule; TIMES, ON, integer-1, integer-2, integer-3 and data-name-1 do not.
- **Markdown:** Line 41 of the page-460 slice: 'OCCURS [ integer-1 TO ] integer-2 TIMES [ DEPENDING ON data-name-1 ] [ STEP integer-3 ]' - transcribed as an ordinary markdown paragraph, outside any fenced block, with NO accompanying 'Figure notes' paragraph. Formats 1, 2 and 4 on the same page are each fenced and each carry a figure-notes paragraph recording their underlining; Format 3 alone records none.
- **Why:** This transcription carries the required-word (underline) information in the figure-notes paragraphs, because plain-text code fences cannot show underlining. For Format 3 that information is simply absent, so the markdown no longer records that OCCURS, TO, DEPENDING and STEP are required words while TIMES and ON are optional words. Rendering the format as prose rather than a fenced figure also loses its identity as a general format for any tool that walks the fenced blocks.
- **Repair:** Wrap the Format 3 line in a fenced code block like its siblings and add: '> **Figure notes (OCCURS clause Format 3 (report-writer) syntax diagram).** `OCCURS`, `TO`, `DEPENDING`, and `STEP` are underlined in the printed standard (required words); `TIMES`, `ON`, `integer-1`, `integer-2`, `integer-3`, and `data-name-1` are not. The three brackets `[ integer-1 TO ]`, `[ DEPENDING ON data-name-1 ]`, and `[ STEP integer-3 ]` are independent optional phrases. No choice indicators appear in this figure.' Do the same for the first line of Format 4 (line 45), which is likewise printed as prose (its underlining is at least recorded in the Format 4 figure note).
- **Verifier:** REAL, with the framing corrected. Page 460, 13.18.38.2, Format 3 (report-writer): the PDF prints 'OCCURS [ integer-1 TO ] integer-2 TIMES [ DEPENDING ON data-name-1 ] [ STEP integer-3 ]' with underline rules under OCCURS, TO, DEPENDING and STEP only (verified glyph-by-glyph in a 300-dpi zoom). The markdown (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 19557 = page-460 slice line 41) reproduces the text and its bracket structure exactly, but records the underlining by none of the file's three available mechanisms - no <u> tags, no \underline{}, and no 'Figure notes' paragraph - so the required-vs-optional-word distinction that the standard's own conventions clause makes normative (md lines 2628/5770/5805) is lost for this figure. The one datum recorded nowhere in the whole file is that STEP is a required word: STEP's underline means the keyword must be written whenever the bracketed phrase is used. Two claim details are wrong: Format 4's opening line is ALSO unfenced prose (md line 19561), so the page does not contrast 'fenced formats vs prose Format 3' - it contrasts formats that have a figure-notes paragraph recording their underlining against Format 3, which has none; and unfenced single-line general formats are this transcription's standard style (63 of 166 'Format N' headings), so the missing fence is not itself a defect and no tool contract is broken by it. Fix: add the underline record, either as inline markup - '<u>OCCURS</u> [ integer-1 <u>TO</u> ] integer-2 TIMES [ <u>DEPENDING</u> ON data-name-1 ] [ <u>STEP</u> integer-3 ]' - or as a Format 3 figure-notes paragraph naming OCCURS, TO, DEPENDING, STEP as underlined and TIMES, ON as not. Severity is better characterized as content-loss/minor than structural: one figure's required-word annotation, not a structural corruption. Separate whitespace nit spotted while checking, not part of this claim: the Format 4 line transcribes '[FROM integer-4 ]' where the PDF prints '[ FROM integer-4 ]'.

### p471 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 13.18.40.2 General formats / Format 1 (basic): the PICTURE Format 1 general-format diagram is printed EXACTLY ONCE on page 471 (one diagram: '{PICTURE/PIC} IS character-string-1 [ EDITING character-1 { IS literal-1 / FOR {| NEGATIVE IS literal-2 / POSITIVE IS literal-3 |} } ] ...'), followed immediately by 'Format 2 (locale):'.
- **Markdown:** The Format 1 diagram is transcribed TWICE. Under the single 'Format 1 (basic):' heading there are two consecutive fenced code blocks, each followed by its own paragraph beginning '> **Figure notes (PICTURE clause Format 1 (basic) syntax diagram).**' — the first block's middle row reads '⎨         ⎬ IS character-string-1  ⎢ EDITING character-1 ⎨     ⎧| NEGATIVE IS literal-2 |⎫  ⎬ ⎥ …' and the second block's middle row reads '⎨         ⎬  IS character-string-1  ⎢ EDITING character-1  ⎨       ⎧ | NEGATIVE IS literal-2 | ⎫  ⎬ ⎥ …'. There is no second Format 1 in the PDF; the second copy also renders the inner FOR brace malformed (bottom row uses '⎨ | POSITIVE IS literal-3 | ⎬' instead of the closing '⎩ ... ⎭').
- **Why:** One printed general format is presented as two, and the two renderings disagree with each other (different underline lists, different brace glyphs). A reader or a generator walking the markdown sees two Format 1 diagrams for PICTURE and cannot tell which is authoritative; the malformed inner brace in the second copy also misrepresents the FOR choice group's delimiters.
- **Repair:** Delete the second fenced block (source lines 33-37 of the page-471 slice) and its accompanying figure-notes paragraph (line 39-40), keeping ONE Format 1 diagram. Retain the SECOND note's underline list (it is the accurate one - see the companion finding) merged onto the surviving diagram.
- **Verifier:** REAL, as described, with one adjudication the claim left open.

Location: specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 19948-19971 (13.18.40.2 General formats), reproduced in the page-471 work unit at lines 17-40.

Defect: the PICTURE Format 1 (basic) general format — printed exactly once on PDF page 471 (folio 441) — is transcribed twice under one 'Format 1 (basic):' caption, as two consecutive fenced blocks each with its own 'Figure notes (PICTURE clause Format 1 (basic) syntax diagram)' paragraph, before 'Format 2 (locale):'.

The two copies disagree, and NEITHER is wholly correct, so the fix is not simply deleting one:
  - Copy 1 (lines 19950-19962): brace glyphs are correct (inner FOR brace closes with ⎩ ... ⎭), but its figure note OMITS `FOR` from the underline list. The printed page DOES underline FOR (verified on a 300 dpi crop: a distinct rule under 'FOR'), so copy 1's note misstates the print.
  - Copy 2 (lines 19964-19971): the underline list is correct (PICTURE, PIC, EDITING, FOR, NEGATIVE, POSITIVE), but its inner FOR brace is malformed — the bottom row uses the middle brace pieces '⎨ | POSITIVE IS literal-3 | ⎬' (U+23A8/U+23AC) where the closing pieces '⎩ ... ⎭' (U+23A9/U+23AD) belong, so the two-row brace never closes.

Correct resolution: collapse to a SINGLE Format 1 (basic) diagram using copy 1's brace glyphs together with copy 2's underline list (PICTURE, PIC, EDITING, FOR, NEGATIVE, POSITIVE underlined; IS, character-string-1, character-1 and the literals not underlined), and retain exactly one figure-notes paragraph.

The choice indicators must be preserved: I independently confirmed on the printed page that a tall vertical bar stands just inside each inner brace of the FOR group, bracketing the stacked NEGATIVE/POSITIVE alternatives. Per 5.2.6.4 that means one or more of NEGATIVE/POSITIVE, each at most once, in any order — not an exclusive either/or — so a single FOR phrase may carry NEGATIVE alone, POSITIVE alone, or both. Both existing notes state this correctly; keep that wording (dropping the inconsistent '900 dpi' / '600 dpi' verification asides, which differ between the copies and carry no normative content).

Severity 'structural' is appropriate: a duplicated general format is directly consumable by a grammar generator and there is no way to tell from the markdown which of the two conflicting renderings is authoritative.

### p489 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** Table 10 — Format 1 picture symbol order of precedence: the 'First Symbol' column-group band spans the 24 symbol columns as Simple, special, and fixed insertion symbols = columns 1-9 (B 0 / , . + +- +- CRDB cs cs); Zero-suppression and floating insertion symbols = columns 10-15 (Z* Z* +- +- cs cs); Other symbols = columns 16-24 (9 A X S V P P 1 N E). The rule between the 'Zero-suppression and floating insertion symbols' band and the 'Other symbols' band falls immediately to the LEFT of the '9' column (verified: vertical rule at x=1696px, and the '9' cell spans x=1698-1759px).
- **Markdown:** | | | **Simple, special, and fixed insertion symbols** | | | | | | | | | **Zero-suppression and floating insertion symbols** | | | | | | | **Other symbols** | | | | | | | |  — in this row cell 19 holds '**Other symbols**', but in the symbol-header row directly beneath it cell 19 is 'A X' and cell 18 is '9'. The group label therefore starts one column late, placing the '9' column under 'Zero-suppression and floating insertion symbols'.
- **Why:** The three band labels are the standard's own classification of the picture symbols. As transcribed, '9' — an ordinary digit-position symbol — is presented as a zero-suppression / floating-insertion symbol, contradicting 13.18.40.5 rule 7 (only 'Z' and '*' are zero-suppression symbols) and rule 6 (only cs, the extended editing sign control symbols, '+' and '-' are floating insertion symbols). Anyone reading the precedence table to derive PICTURE validation would mis-classify the '9' column. The x-mark data itself is correct and unaffected.
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 20686, move '**Other symbols**' one cell to the left so it sits in cell 18 (aligned with the '9' column of the symbol-header row), i.e. delete one empty cell between '**Zero-suppression and floating insertion symbols**' and '**Other symbols**' and add one empty cell after '**Other symbols**', keeping the row at 26 cells.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed as described; no correction needed. Precise form: in E:/CobolSharp/specs/ISO_COBOL.md page-489 Table 10, the band-label row places '**Other symbols**' in pipe cell 19, which aligns with symbol column 17 ('A X'). The PDF's band rule falls at x=1696 px (300 dpi), immediately left of symbol column 16 ('9', x 1696-1759), so the label belongs in pipe cell 18. Fix: move '**Other symbols**' one cell left (cell 19 -> cell 18), leaving the trailing empty cell at the end of the row, so that the bands read 9 / 6 / 9 columns as printed instead of 9 / 7 / 8. Only the band-label row needs to change; the symbol-header row and all sixteen x-mark data rows on this page are faithful to the PDF.

### p490 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** Table 10 (continued) has exactly 24 'First Symbol' data columns, headed (from p.489): 1 B 0 / | 2 , | 3 . | 4 + | 5 + - | 6 + - | 7 CR DB | 8 cs | 9 cs | 10 Z * | 11 Z * | 12 + - | 13 + - | 14 cs | 15 cs | 16 9 | 17 A X | 18 S | 19 V | 20 P | 21 P | 22 1 | 23 N | 24 E. On p.490 the printed marks are: row 9 -> 1,2,3,4,5,8,10,12,14,16,17,18,19,21,24; row A X -> 1,16,17; row S -> none; row V -> 1,2,5,8,10,12,14,16,18,20; row P(1st) -> 1,2,5,8,10,12,14,16,18,20; row P(2nd) -> 5,8,18,19,21; row 1 -> 22; row N -> 1,23; row E -> 1,2,3,5,16.
- **Markdown:** The nine transcribed rows carry inconsistent cell counts (27, 27, 27, 27, 26, 26, 27, 27, 26) against a 28-column separator row, and the first row '| Other symbols | | 9 | x | ...' carries THREE leading cells while every other row ('| | A X | ...', '| | S | ...', ...) carries only TWO. Consequently marks land under the wrong First-Symbol column: row 'N' = '| | N | x | ... | x | |' puts its second mark in data column 24 (E) instead of 23 (N); row 'E' = '| | E | x | x | x | | x | ... | x | ...' puts its right-hand mark in column 15 (cs) instead of 16 (9); row 'A X' puts its two marks in columns 17 and 18 (A X, S) instead of 16 and 17 (9, A X); row 'P' (2nd) puts its right-hand marks in columns 17, 18, 20 (A X, S, P-1st) instead of 18, 19, 21 (S, V, P-2nd).
- **Why:** Every cell of Table 10 is a normative 'symbol X may precede symbol Y' assertion. A one-column shift silently re-attributes each assertion to a different picture symbol, so a reader (or a generated conformance test) derives permissions and prohibitions for the wrong symbols - e.g. reading 'E may precede N' where the standard says 'N may precede N'.
- **Repair:** Re-emit the Table 10 continuation as a fixed 26-column markdown table: column 1 = the 'Other symbols' row-group label, column 2 = the Second-Symbol row label, columns 3-26 = the 24 First-Symbol columns in the p.489 header order, with every row padded to the same width. Then re-place each x at the column indices listed in pdf_says.
- **Verifier:** REAL, and one notch worse than claimed. page-490.md's Table 10 (continued) is structurally desynchronized from the 2-leading-cell + 24-data-cell convention that page-489.md uses for the same table (whose line 11 is the authoritative column-header row: B 0 / , . + +- +- CR DB cs cs Z* Z* +- +- cs cs 9 A X S V P P 1 N E). Every element of the claim is verified: the row cell counts are 27/27/27/27/26/26/27/27/26 against a 28-cell separator; the '9' row uniquely carries three leading cells ('| Other symbols | | 9 | ...') instead of two; and rows A X, P(2nd), N and E place marks in exactly the wrong columns the claim names (A X at 17,18 instead of 16,17; P(2nd) at 17,18,20 instead of 18,19,21; N at 24 instead of 23; E at 15 instead of 16). Two facts the claim omits: (1) the P (first) row does not merely shift - it LOSES a mark outright, transcribing 9 marks (1,2,5,8,10,12,14,16,18) where the PDF prints 10 (1,2,5,8,10,12,14,16,18,20), deleting the normative assertion 'P may precede P'; and (2) rows S, V and 1 do transcribe correctly under the 2-leading reading, and the '9' row transcribes correctly only under a 3-leading reading - it is precisely this per-row inconsistency, not a uniform offset, that makes the table unreadable. The fix is to normalize all nine rows to 26 cells (rotated label / row symbol / 24 data) matching page-489.md, re-seat every mark from the measured PDF positions listed in the reasoning, and restore the dropped P-row mark at data column 20.

### p528 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** Sub-items are printed indented under their parents: the continuation paragraph 'If a report footing is defined and is not on a page by itself, the page footing on the last page is immediately followed by the report footing.' sits at the body indent of General rule 6) f) (it is the tail of f)); 'g) The report footing, if defined, is printed, ...' sits at the a)-g) marker indent under 6); '7) The upper limit is defined to be the uppermost permitted line ...' sits at the top-level rule indent; 'a) The upper limit for a report heading or a page heading ...' through 'g) The upper limit for a report footing that does not appear on a page by itself ...' are indented one level under 7); and '1. If the body group is a control heading at the same or a higher control level ...' through '4. If the body group is a control footing, ...' are indented a further level under 7) d).
- **Markdown:** Every one of those lines is flush left at column 0 with no indentation, e.g.:
"If a report footing is defined and is not on a page by itself, the page footing on the last page is immediately followed by the report footing."
"g) The report footing, if defined, is printed, as the very last report group in the report, ..."
"7) The upper limit is defined to be the uppermost permitted line on the page that may be occupied by the report group's first line. It is calculated as follows:"
"a) The upper limit for a report heading or a page heading where no report heading appears on the same page is the line given by the HEADING integer."
"1. If the body group is a control heading at the same or a higher control level than the highest-level control heading that has an OR PAGE phrase, the upper limit is the line given by the FIRST DETAIL integer."
"g) The upper limit for a report footing that does not appear on a page by itself is the line following the last line of the page footing, ..."
- **Why:** Indentation is the only thing that binds a sub-item to its parent rule in 13.18.57.4, and here it is the only thing lost. Flattened, page 528 shows two different 'g)' items at the same level as '7)' - one belonging to General rule 6) (carried over from page 527) and one belonging to 7) - so a reader or a tool walking the list cannot tell which rule either 'g)' qualifies, and the '1.' through '4.' cases read as top-level enumerations instead of the four sub-cases of 7) d) (upper limit for a body group when some control heading carries OR PAGE). The transcription preserves this nesting correctly on pages 527, 529 and 535 of the same clause, which confirms page 528 is drift rather than a house style.
- **Repair:** Re-indent the page-528 slice to match the convention already used on pages 527/529/535: three spaces before 'a)'..'g)' of rule 7) and before the 'g)' that belongs to rule 6), six spaces before '1.'..'4.' under 7) d), and three spaces before the 'If a report footing is defined ...' continuation paragraph so it stays attached to 6) f).
- **Verifier:** Real and essentially as described; one supporting citation in the claim is wrong. Page 528 flattens all of 13.18.57.4's nesting to column 0: the tail continuation of General rule 6) f), item 6) g), rule 7), the seven lettered sub-items a)-g) under 7), and the four numbered sub-cases 1.-4. under 7) d) are all at indent 0, where the transcription's own convention (and the PDF's typography) is 0 / 3 / 6 spaces for the rule / letter / numeral levels. The claim cites pages 527, 529 and 535 as same-clause counterexamples; only 527 and 529 are in clause 13.18.57.4 - page 535 is clause 13.18.60 (USAGE) and merely corroborates the house style. The strongest evidence, which the claim understates, is that General rule 6)'s own lettered enumeration is transcribed at indent 3 on page 527 (a through f) and indent 0 on page 528 (g), so a single list is split across the page break at two different levels - unambiguous per-page drift. Fix is indentation only: no text is missing, misquoted, or reworded on this page.

Any grep-based citation workflow gets two hits for one rule and cannot tell which is canonical.
