- **Verifier:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md duplicates the entire printed-page-546 body. PDF page 576 (printed 546) carries it exactly once: the NOTE 'Any open files in an object are closed before the object is destroyed.', 14.6.11 items 5) 6) 7), all of 14.6.12 Abnormal run unit termination, and 14.6.13 / 14.6.13.1 / 14.6.13.1.1 General through '...specified in the RAISING phrase.' PDF page 575 (printed 545) ends cleanly at '4) All instance objects are destroyed.' with nothing after it but the footer. In the markdown, that block appears twice: a spurious copy stranded inside the page-575 slice at lines 24073-24109, introduced by a stray '---' rule (24073) and a stray H1 '# ISO/IEC 1989:2023 (E)' (24075); and the correct copy in the page-576 slice at 24113-24150. A diff of the two ranges shows they are byte-identical except for two heading-depth lines: the stray copy flattens '### 14.6.13.1 Exception conditions' to '##' and '#### 14.6.13.1.1 General' to '##'. Four section anchors are emitted twice (section-14-6-12 at 24085/24126, section-14-6-13 at 24100/24141, section-14-6-13-1 at 24103/24144, section-14-6-13-1-1 at 24106/24147); since the spurious copy comes first in file order, every intra-document link and every heading-derived TOC entry resolves to the copy that sits under the wrong page anchor and at the wrong heading depth, and the stray H1 additionally breaks the heading hierarchy mid-clause-14. Fix: delete lines 24073-24110 (the stray rule, the stray H1, and the duplicated block plus its trailing blank), leaving the page-575 slice to end at '4) All instance objects are destroyed.'

### p576 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** Printed page 546 (PDF page 576) ends with '... in which the object is specified in the RAISING phrase.' and then the footer '546   ©ISO/IEC 2023'. Printed page 547 (PDF page 577) OPENS with the paragraph 'An exception status indicator is a conceptual entity that exists for each function, method, or program for each exception condition and has two states, set or cleared. The initial state of all exception status indicators is cleared. ...'
- **Markdown:** Line 24149, still inside the page-576 slice and BEFORE '<a id="page-577"></a>' at line 24151: '546 �ISO/IEC 2023 An exception status indicator is a conceptual entity that exists for each function, method, or program for each exception condition and has two states, set or cleared. ...'. The page-577 slice then starts at its SECOND paragraph, 'In addition to exception status indicators, a last exception status exists for the entire run unit.'
- **Why:** The opening normative paragraph of 14.6.13.1.1 on page 577 - the definition of an exception status indicator, its two states, its initial cleared state and how TURN / PERFORM WHEN enable it - is attributed to the wrong page, so a page-cited lookup of page 577 misses it. The page-576 running footer has additionally been OCR'd into the body as literal text with a U+FFFD replacement character where '©' belongs, so the paragraph reads '546 �ISO/IEC 2023 An exception status indicator ...'.
- **Repair:** Strip the inlined footer text '546 �ISO/IEC 2023 ' from line 24149 and move the remaining paragraph ('An exception status indicator is a conceptual entity ... to be executed.') to immediately after the '# ISO/IEC 1989:2023 (E)' header line of the page-577 slice, ahead of 'In addition to exception status indicators, ...'.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED as described, with one correction to the cited line numbers - the claim's line references are each off by 3. Accurate statement: in specs/ISO_COBOL.md the page-576 anchor is at line 24113 and the page-577 anchor at line 24154 (not 24151). The misplaced paragraph is at line 24152 (not 24149), reading '546 <U+FFFD>ISO/IEC 2023 An exception status indicator is a conceptual entity that exists for each function, method, or program for each exception condition and has two states, set or cleared. ...'; it lies inside the page-576 slice although the PDF prints it as the opening paragraph of printed page 547 / PDF page 577. The page-577 slice consequently begins at line 24159 with the printed page's second paragraph, 'In addition to exception status indicators, a last exception status exists for the entire run unit.' The substance of the claim - one normative paragraph of 14.6.13.1.1 attributed to the wrong page, plus the page-546 running footer OCR'd into the body of that paragraph with U+FFFD in place of '©' - is fully verified. Two supporting facts the claim did not state: footers are otherwise stripped throughout the document (only 4 non-header 'ISO/IEC 2023' occurrences exist file-wide, and this is the only one embedded mid-sentence), and this U+FFFD is the only replacement character in the entire file. Suggested fix: move line 24152 to just after the page-577 anchor and delete the leading '546 <U+FFFD>ISO/IEC 2023 ' footer fragment from it.

### p606 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** Page 606 ends after the Format 2 (temporal) diagram (blank space, then the footer '576 (c)ISO/IEC 2023'). The label 'Format 3 (screen):' is the FIRST line printed on page 607, above 'ACCEPT screen-name-1'.
- **Markdown:** $$\underline{\text{ACCEPT}} \text{ identifier-2 } ... \text{ ]}$$ Format 3 (screen):    <-- appended to the end of the Format 2 math line, on page 606, immediately before the <a id="page-607"></a> anchor; page 607's slice has no 'Format 3 (screen):' line at all
- **Why:** The figure label is both on the wrong page and glued onto the closing '$$' of the preceding display-math block, so it renders as trailing text on the Format 2 formula instead of as the heading for the Format 3 diagram that follows the page break. Anyone reading page 607 sees 'ACCEPT screen-name-1' with no format label; anyone reading page 606 sees a stray label attached to the wrong figure.
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md around line 25254, split 'Format 3 (screen):' off the '$$...$$' line and move it below the <a id="page-607"></a> / '## Page 607' scaffolding, directly above '<u>ACCEPT</u> screen-name-1'.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with the harm narrowed. The transcription omits the required line break between the Format 2 display-math block and the 'Format 3 (screen):' label: specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 25254 ends '...\text{ ]}$$ Format 3 (screen):', concatenating the label onto the closing '$$'. Because that line falls before the '<a id="page-607"></a>' anchor on line 25256, the label also lands in the page-606 slice, whereas the PDF prints it as the first body line of page 607 (folio 577) above 'ACCEPT screen-name-1'; PDF page 606 (folio 576) genuinely ends with the Format 2 diagram, whitespace, and the footer. The label is the only one of the three format labels in 14.9.1.2 that is malformed -- 'Format 1 (device):' (line 25248) and 'Format 2 (temporal):' (line 25252) are each correctly on their own line. Two consequences, both verified: the label renders as trailing text captioning the Format 2 formula rather than as a heading for Format 3, and the page-607 slice presents 'ACCEPT screen-name-1' plus its diagram with no format label at all. Scope correction to the original claim: no text is lost and linear reading order is preserved -- the label still precedes the Format 3 syntax in document order, so a top-to-bottom reader is not misled about which format is which, and no syntax element, bracket, brace, choice indicator, or underline is affected. The defect is presentational and page-attribution only. Fix: split line 25254 so the label begins a new paragraph, and move it below the '<a id="page-607"></a>' anchor to match the PDF's page assignment.

### p607 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 'ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-1' with EXCEPTION underlined; 'NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-2' with NOT and EXCEPTION underlined (ON is NOT underlined in either row).
- **Markdown:** \text{ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-1} \\ \text{NOT ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-2}
- **Why:** The whole exception block is transcribed with zero underline markup even though the surrounding figure (ACCEPT, FROM, LINE, COLUMN, END-ACCEPT) uses \underline throughout, so the required-word/optional-word distinction is silently lost exactly where it matters: EXCEPTION and NOT are required, ON is optional. A reader would not be able to tell that 'NOT EXCEPTION' is legal and that 'EXCEPTION' cannot be omitted.
- **Repair:** Mark up as \text{ON } \underline{\text{EXCEPTION}} \text{ imperative-statement-1} and \underline{\text{NOT}} \text{ ON } \underline{\text{EXCEPTION}} \text{ imperative-statement-2}, or move the figure to a code block with an explicit figure note listing EXCEPTION and NOT as the underlined words.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with a broadened scope. In the ACCEPT Format 3 (screen) general format on page 607, the markdown loses THREE pieces of figure notation, not one. (1) The exception block is transcribed as plain \text{} with no underline markup: the PDF underlines EXCEPTION in row 1, and underlines NOT and EXCEPTION (but not ON) in row 2, so the transcription silently erases that ON is an optional word while EXCEPTION and NOT are required -- 'ACCEPT scr EXCEPTION imp-1' / 'NOT EXCEPTION imp-2' are legal and the transcription cannot show it. (2) The same figure drops the underline on COL: the PDF underlines both COLUMN and COL as required alternatives of the brace, the markdown writes \underline{\text{COLUMN}} but plain \text{COL}, wrongly demoting COL to an optional word. (3) More severely, the exception block's delimiters are mis-transcribed: the PDF prints BRACKETS WITH CHOICE INDICATORS -- verified in exc_left.png as '[' immediately followed by a full-height vertical bar, and in exc_right.png as a full-height vertical bar immediately followed by ']' (the '⎡| ... |⎤' form) -- which per ISO 5.2.6.4 means zero or more of the enclosed alternatives, each at most once, in any order, i.e. ON EXCEPTION and NOT ON EXCEPTION may BOTH appear in one ACCEPT, in either order. The markdown renders it as a plain \left[ ... \right] around a two-row stack, which reads as ordinary zero-or-one selection and thus wrongly forbids specifying both phrases. Note the same transcription already documents the correct '⎡| ... |⎤' semantics for the identical CALL Format 2 block (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 25738), so page 607 is inconsistent with the file's own established treatment. Fix: mark \underline on EXCEPTION (row 1), NOT and EXCEPTION (row 2), and COL; and render the exception block with choice indicators plus a figure note matching the CALL Format 2 note.

### p609 · other · **structural**

- **PDF:** FORMAT 3
- **Markdown:** ## FORMAT 3
- **Why:** In the PDF, 'FORMAT 3' is an ordinary (non-bold, body-size) label paragraph that scopes the general rules that follow it inside 14.9.1.4 - exactly like 'FORMAT 1' / 'FORMAT 2' on pages 620 and 623, which the transcription renders as plain paragraphs. Promoting it to a level-2 markdown heading injects a phantom section into the document outline at the SAME level as '## 14.9.1.3 Syntax rules', so General rule 13) and everything after it appears to belong to a top-level section named 'FORMAT 3' rather than to 14.9.1.4, General rules. The transcription is overwhelmingly consistent the other way: 209 plain 'FORMAT n' label lines vs only 8 promoted to headings.
- **Repair:** Change line 25360 of specs/ISO_COBOL.md from '## FORMAT 3' to the plain paragraph 'FORMAT 3', matching the 209 other FORMAT label lines. (The same fix applies to the other 7 promoted labels at lines 6280, 6284, 6288, 6298, 19800, 28029, 28033, which lie outside this batch.)
- **Verifier:** REAL, with one correction to the claim's supporting evidence. The claim cites 'FORMAT 1 / FORMAT 2 on pages 620 and 623' as the parallel case; those FORMAT labels do exist and are plain, but they belong to the CALL statement (14.9.4.3 Syntax rules, page 620), a different section. The decisive siblings are nearer and stronger: 'FORMAT 1' on page 607 (ISO print page 577) and 'FORMAT 2' on page 608 (ISO print page 578) are the labels for the very same general-rules list of 14.9.1.4 that 'FORMAT 3' on page 609 belongs to; the PDF prints all three in identical plain non-bold body type, and the transcription renders the first two as plain paragraphs (lines 25290 and 25312) while promoting only the third to '## FORMAT 3' (line 25363). Because 14.9.1.4 is itself emitted as '## 14.9.1.4 General rules', the injected heading is its peer and detaches general rule 13) plus its page-610 continuation table from 14.9.1.4. Fix: demote line 25363 to a plain 'FORMAT 3' paragraph. Same defect class, worth sweeping in the same change: 7 further promoted 'FORMAT n' headings at page-146 (x4), page-466 (x1), and page-680 (x2), against 209 correctly-plain instances.

### p636 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.10.4   General rules  — printed in the same bold subsection style and at the same depth as 14.9.10.1 General, 14.9.10.2 General formats and 14.9.10.3 Syntax rules on page 635.
- **Markdown:** <a id="section-14-9-10-4"></a>
## 14.9.10.4 General rules   (ISO_COBOL.md line 26362)
- **Why:** A fourth-level subsection is emitted at heading level 2, the same level as its own parent '## 14.9.10 DELETE statement' and as '## 14.9.11 DISPLAY statement'. Its three siblings on page 635 are correctly at level 3 (### 14.9.10.1/.2/.3). Any tool that navigates by heading level — the doc index, a TOC generator, or a section-slicing spec lookup — will treat the DELETE General rules as a peer of the statement itself and detach them from 14.9.10, silently truncating what a '14.9.10 DELETE statement' section fetch returns.
- **Repair:** Change '## 14.9.10.4 General rules' to '### 14.9.10.4 General rules'. Sweep the siblings: '## 14.9.11.4 General rules' at line 26538 has the identical defect.
- **Verifier:** Wrong heading LEVEL, not a missing heading. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 26365 emits `## 14.9.10.4 General rules` (anchor `<a id="section-14-9-10-4"></a>` at 26364) at markdown level 2, while its siblings `### 14.9.10.1 General` (26314), `### 14.9.10.2 General formats` (26319) and `### 14.9.10.3 Syntax rules` (26344) are at level 3 and its parent `## 14.9.10 DELETE statement` (26311) is at level 2. The PDF prints 14.9.10.4 as a normal fourth-level subsection of 14.9.10; the transcription encodes it as a peer of the DELETE statement itself. No normative text is lost — the FORMAT 1 general rules on page 636 transcribe faithfully. Reclassify kind as `heading-level` and correct the cited line from 26362 to 26365. Repair as a PATTERN, not a one-off: 162 headings with four dotted components sit at `##` versus 620 at `###`, including 14.9.11.4 at line 26541, which is mid-page and therefore rules out page-break furniture as the sole cause. Note when scoping the fix that scripts/spec/extract_rule_catalog.py already accepts both levels by design, so the rule catalog is not currently truncated by this; the exposure is to any future heading-level-based navigator or TOC generator.

### p645 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** [|  ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1  |]  /  [|  NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2  |]  — a bracket with a vertical CHOICE-INDICATOR bar just inside each side; DIVIDE, INTO, BY, GIVING, REMAINDER, ON, SIZE, ERROR, NOT and END-DIVIDE are all underlined (required words).
- **Markdown:** ```
⌐⌐ ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1       ¬¬
⌊⌊ NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2   ┘┘
[ END-DIVIDE ]
```
(no '> **Figure notes ...**' paragraph follows either Format 4 or Format 5)
- **Why:** The choice indicators survive only as doubled corner glyphs, which read as two nested brackets rather than as 5.2.6.4 choice indicators, and — unlike DIVIDE Formats 1, 2 and 3 on page 644, each of which carries a 'Figure notes' paragraph stating '⚠ The ON SIZE ERROR group is enclosed in CHOICE INDICATORS' and listing the underlined required words — Formats 4 and 5 carry no Figure notes at all. So for Formats 4 and 5 the file records neither the 5.2.6.4 semantics (ON SIZE ERROR, NOT ON SIZE ERROR, BOTH, or neither, in any order) nor which words are required.
- **Repair:** Normalize the SIZE ERROR group to the file's '⎡|  ... |⎤' / '⎣|  ... |⎦' form and append a Figure notes paragraph to Format 4 and Format 5 matching the Format 1–3 notes, naming DIVIDE, INTO/BY, GIVING, REMAINDER, ON, SIZE, ERROR, NOT, END-DIVIDE as the underlined required words.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with two corrections to the claim's description.

(1) The claim overstates the underlining. In the printed page 645 the word ON is NOT underlined in either 'ON SIZE ERROR' or 'NOT ON SIZE ERROR' - the underlines sit under SIZE, ERROR and NOT only (ON is an optional word: '[ON] SIZE ERROR'). Underlined in Format 4: DIVIDE, INTO, GIVING, REMAINDER, SIZE, ERROR, NOT, END-DIVIDE. In Format 5: DIVIDE, BY, GIVING, REMAINDER, SIZE, ERROR, NOT, END-DIVIDE. Note this also means the page-644 Figure notes are themselves slightly wrong where they list ON among the underlined required words - a separate, lower-severity defect to raise against page 644.

(2) The lost glyph is described precisely as follows: the PDF prints, on each side of the two-line ON SIZE ERROR group, a square bracket plus a separate plain full-height vertical bar just inside it (5.2.6.4 choice indicators). The markdown has dropped the bar entirely and duplicated the bracket corner instead: U+2310 U+2310 / U+00AC U+00AC on the upper line and U+230A U+230A / U+2518 U+2518 on the lower line, with no U+007C present anywhere in the group. Both DIVIDE Format 4 (into-remainder) and Format 5 (by-remainder) are affected identically, at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 26705-26706 and 26719-26720.

Everything else in the claim holds: the markdown is quoted verbatim, the choice-indicator semantics of 5.2.6.4 are unrecoverable from the file for these two formats, and no Figure notes paragraph exists anywhere in the page-645 span to compensate - unlike Formats 1-3 on page 644, which both render the bars correctly as '⎡| ... |⎤' AND carry the explanatory note.

Additional in-scope defect found in the SAME two figures while verifying: the operand-selection BRACES are also degraded. The PDF prints true curly braces around the identifier-1/literal-1 and identifier-2/literal-2 stacks (braces = choose exactly one, mandatory). The markdown (lines 26699-26701 and 26713-26715) renders them as a bracket-like box outline built from U+2310/U+00AC/U+2514/U+2518 corners with '|' sides, which reads as a BRACKET (optional), not a brace - whereas page 644 renders the identical construct correctly with the proper brace glyphs U+23A7/U+23A8/U+23A9 and U+23AB/U+23AC/U+23AD. Recommend the fix for page 645 restore both the choice-indicator bars and the brace glyphs, and add Figure notes paragraphs for Formats 4 and 5 matching the page-644 pattern.

Recommended replacement for each of the two ON SIZE ERROR groups:
    ⎡|  ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-1      |⎤
    ⎣|  NOT ON SIZE ERROR imperative-statement-2  |⎦

### p646 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.12.4   General rules  (bold run-in heading, one level below '14.9.12 DIVIDE statement', same level as 14.9.12.3 Syntax rules)
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.12.4 General rules   (H2 — while the immediately preceding sibling on page 645 is '### 14.9.12.3 Syntax rules' and the parent is '## 14.9.12 DIVIDE statement')
- **Why:** Same defect as 14.9.11.4: the General rules block is promoted to sibling-of-the-statement level, breaking the outline and detaching the DIVIDE general rules from the DIVIDE statement. It is also internally inconsistent with 14.9.12.3 two pages earlier, which is correctly H3.
- **Repair:** Change '## 14.9.12.4 General rules' to '### 14.9.12.4 General rules'.
- **Verifier:** Real, but mislabeled and slightly misquoted. (1) kind should be 'wrong-heading-level', not 'missing-heading': no text is missing or altered — the heading reads '14.9.12.4 General rules' in both sources, and the general-rules body text on page 646 transcribes faithfully. The defect is purely that the markdown emits it at depth H2 instead of H3. (2) The PDF heading is a bold STANDALONE heading on its own line, not a 'run-in' heading (no body text follows on the same line). (3) 14.9.12.3 Syntax rules is ONE page earlier (page 645), not two. (4) Scope is wider than a pair: this is one instance of a systematic transcription inconsistency — of the 204 four-component 14.9.x.y headings, 42 carry the wrong depth (40 promoted to H2, 2 demoted to H4), including 14.9.1.3/1.4, 14.9.2.3/2.4, 14.9.4.3, 14.9.10.4, 14.9.11.4, 14.9.12.4, and on through 14.9.49.4. Impact is navigational/structural only (outline, TOC, anchor hierarchy, and any tooling that derives ISO clause parentage from markdown heading depth); no normative content is lost. Fix should be a mechanical sweep that sets heading depth from clause-number component count, not a one-line patch at line 26744.

### p651 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.13.4   General rules  (bold run-in heading, one level below '14.9.13 EVALUATE statement', same level as 14.9.13.1 General and 14.9.13.2 General format)
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.13.4 General rules   (H2 — while '### 14.9.13.1 General' and '### 14.9.13.2 General format' are H3 and the parent is '## 14.9.13 EVALUATE statement')
- **Why:** Same defect: the EVALUATE general rules become a sibling of the EVALUATE statement rather than a child, breaking the outline. (14.9.13.3 Syntax rules, on page 650, is H2 for the same reason.)
- **Repair:** Change '## 14.9.13.4 General rules' to '### 14.9.13.4 General rules' (and likewise '## 14.9.13.3 Syntax rules' → '### ...').
- **Verifier:** Real defect, but the kind label 'missing-heading' is inaccurate: nothing is missing. The heading is present on page 651 with the correct number and the correct title, verbatim. The defect is heading DEPTH -- specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 26939 emits '## 14.9.13.4 General rules' at H2, making it a sibling of its own parent '## 14.9.13 EVALUATE statement' (line 26811) and one level shallower than its own siblings '### 14.9.13.1 General' (26814) and '### 14.9.13.2 General format' (26819). Correct depth is H3. The right kind is 'heading-level' / 'outline-depth', severity structural. Two further corrections to the claim: (a) the parenthetical misplaces the companion defect -- 14.9.13.3 Syntax rules is on page 649 (printed folio 619), inside the markdown's '## Page 649' block at line 26868, not page 650; page 650 carries only syntax rules 7)-10) and Table 15. (b) This is not a page-651 one-off but one instance of a document-wide class: 162 four-component clause headings are emitted at H2 against 620 correctly at H3, so the ledger should record it as a systemic pattern to be swept in one pass, not repaired page by page.

### p654 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.14.3   Syntax rules  (bold run-in heading, one level below '14.9.14 EXIT statement', same level as 14.9.14.1 General and 14.9.14.2 General formats)
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.14.3 Syntax rules   (H2 — while '### 14.9.14.1 General' and '### 14.9.14.2 General formats' are H3 and the parent is '## 14.9.14 EXIT statement')
- **Why:** Same defect: the EXIT syntax rules become a sibling of the EXIT statement rather than a child. Compare 14.9.15.1-.4 (FREE statement), which is correctly '## 14.9.15' + '### 14.9.15.N'.
- **Repair:** Change '## 14.9.14.3 Syntax rules' to '### 14.9.14.3 Syntax rules'.
- **Verifier:** The finding is real but was mislabelled and under-scoped on three counts. (1) kind='missing-heading' is wrong - no heading is missing. The heading text, number and anchor are all present and correct; the defect is the heading LEVEL (H2 emitted where H3 is required), i.e. a heading-depth error. (2) The PDF characterization 'bold run-in heading' is wrong - on p.654 '14.9.14.3   Syntax rules' is a standalone bold heading on its own line, typographically identical to '14.9.14.1 General' and '14.9.14.2 General formats' on p.653; nothing runs in with the following text. The correct PDF-side evidence is the identical heading style shared by .1/.2/.3, not a run-in. (3) The scope is far larger than one page. 14.9.14.4 General rules (line 27110) carries the identical defect, and a sweep of all 255 headings under 14.9 found 43 mis-leveled - overwhelmingly the '.3 Syntax rules' / '.4 General rules' pairs (14.9.1.3/.4, 14.9.2.3/.4, 14.9.4.3, 14.9.10.4, 14.9.11.4, 14.9.12.4, 14.9.13.3/.4, 14.9.14.3/.4, 14.9.18.4, 14.9.19.4, 14.9.20.4, 14.9.22.3/.4, 14.9.23.4, 14.9.24.4, 14.9.25.4, 14.9.26.4, 14.9.27.4, 14.9.28.3/.4, 14.9.30.3/.4, 14.9.37.3/.4, 14.9.38.4, 14.9.39.3/.4, 14.9.40.3/.4, 14.9.44.3/.4, 14.9.48.4, 14.9.49.3, and more). A second variant runs the other way at 14.9.1, where the parent is '### 14.9.1' and the children are '#### 14.9.1.1/.1.2'. This should therefore be ledgered once as a systemic heading-depth normalization pass over ISO_COBOL.md (derive the hash count from the dotted section number), not as a per-page EXIT-specific finding. Severity 'structural' is right but the blast radius is bounded: no prose is lost and the dotted number inside each heading still carries the true hierarchy, so grep- and anchor-based citation is unaffected; only heading-tree/subtree navigation breaks.

### p655 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.14.4  General rules  — a fourth-level clause heading, one level below 14.9.14 EXIT statement, identical in rank to 14.9.15.4, 14.9.16.4 and 14.9.17.4.
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.14.4 General rules   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27107 — level 2, the same level as '## 14.9.14 EXIT statement' at line 27013, whereas '### 14.9.15.4 General rules' at line 27170 is level 3)
- **Why:** The heading is promoted a level, so General rules for the EXIT statement renders as a sibling of the EXIT statement clause itself instead of a child. That breaks the generated outline/TOC nesting and any tooling that walks the markdown heading tree to map clause numbers to sections.
- **Repair:** Demote line 27107 to '### 14.9.14.4 General rules' to match the 14.9.15.x / 14.9.16.x / 14.9.17.x pattern. (Line 27058, '## 14.9.14.3 Syntax rules', has the same defect on page 654 — outside this batch.)
- **Verifier:** REAL, but mislabeled and misquoted on details; also under-scoped. (1) Nothing is missing — kind 'missing-heading' is a misnomer. The heading text '14.9.14.4 General rules' is present and verbatim correct, and its anchor '<a id="section-14-9-14-4"></a>' is present and correct. The defect is heading DEPTH only: '##' where the file's own convention and the ISO clause numbering both require '###'. Correct kind is 'heading-level' / 'wrong-heading-depth'. (2) All three cited line numbers are off by 3. Actual: '## 14.9.14.4 General rules' is line 27110 (not 27107); '## 14.9.14 EXIT statement' is line 27016 (not 27013); '### 14.9.15.4 General rules' is line 27173 (not 27170). (3) The claim treats this as an isolated instance; it is not. Its immediate sibling '## 14.9.14.3 Syntax rules' (line 27061, PDF page 654) has the identical defect, as does '## 14.9.13.3 Syntax rules' (26868), '## 14.9.13.4 General rules' (26939) and '## 14.9.18.4 General rules' (27392). Doc-wide there are 162 four-component headings emitted at level 2 against 620 correctly at level 3 — a systemic OCR/transcription class that should be fixed by a single sweep normalizing heading depth to the component count of the clause number, not by a one-off edit to page 655. (4) Severity should be tempered: no normative CONTENT, wording or COBOL semantics is affected, and cross-references that resolve by clause number or by the section-14-9-14-4 anchor still work. The breakage is limited to consumers that infer hierarchy from '#' depth.

### p657 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.15.2 General format:   FREE { data-name-1 } ...   — FREE is UNDERLINED (verified by rendering page 657 at 300 dpi and cropping the figure; the underline spans all four letters).
- **Markdown:** FREE { data-name-1 } ...   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27162 — no <u> markup)
- **Why:** FREE is a required word; without the underline the figure formally reads as if FREE were an optional word (5.2.6.2). The braces and the trailing ellipsis (which correctly binds to the closing brace per 5.2.7) are transcribed correctly, so this is the only defect in the figure. The markdown already uses <u> in neighbouring formats (14.9.17.2, 14.9.18.2), so the loss is local.
- **Repair:** Change line 27162 to '<u>FREE</u> { data-name-1 } ...'.
- **Verifier:** REAL, but the claim is inaccurate in three respects and its severity label is arguable. (a) Line number: the format line is 27165, not 27162; 27162 is the '<a id="section-14-9-15-2"></a>' anchor. (b) Wrong citation: the required-versus-optional distinction is governed by 5.2.2 (Keywords: uppercase and underlined = required) and 5.2.3 (Optional words: uppercase and not underlined), NOT by 5.2.6.2, which is 'Brackets'. The claim's other citation, 5.2.7 for the ellipsis, is correct. (c) 'The loss is local' is false, and one of its two supporting examples is wrong: 14.9.17.2 does use <u> ('<u>GO</u> TO procedure-name-1'), but 14.9.18.2 (GOBACK, line 27330) is a fenced block with no <u> and no figure note. A survey of the whole file shows underline loss is systemic, not local: of 322 'General format' headings, only 74 have any <u> in the body; restricting to the fairest comparison class - inline (non-fenced) formats, the same style as 14.9.15.2 - 71 carry <u> and 79 do not. Page 657 is one instance of a document-wide transcription weakness, so the remediation should be a sweep over unfenced general formats rather than a one-line patch. Corrected finding: at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27165 the required-word underline printed on FREE in the PDF is absent, and should be restored as '<u>FREE</u> { data-name-1 } ...' to match the file's existing convention. Severity note: the figure's structure (braces, operand, ellipsis binding) is intact; what is lost is a required-word marker, so this is more precisely a notation/markup loss than a structural one - though it does change the formal reading of the format.

### p662 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.18.4  General rules  — a fourth-level clause heading under 14.9.18 GOBACK statement, identical in rank to 14.9.18.1/.2/.3.
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.18.4 General rules   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27389 — level 2, while its siblings '### 14.9.18.1 General', '### 14.9.18.2 General format' and '### 14.9.18.3 Syntax rules' are level 3)
- **Why:** Within a single clause the four sub-clauses are transcribed at two different heading levels, so General rules renders as a sibling of the GOBACK statement clause rather than as its fourth sub-clause. The outline nesting and clause-to-section mapping are wrong.

The PDF (page 662, printed 632) prints '14.9.18.4  General rules' as the fourth sub-clause of 14.9.18 GOBACK statement, in the same bold style as 14.9.18.1/.2/.3 on the preceding page. The markdown transcribes the heading text correctly and verbatim, with a correct '<a id="section-14-9-18-4"></a>' anchor, but emits it at heading level 2 ('## 14.9.18.4 General rules', specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27392 — the claim cites 27389) while its three siblings are at level 3, making it a peer of '## 14.9.18 GOBACK statement' in any depth-derived outline. Anchor-based cross-references are unaffected; only heading-depth nesting is wrong. This is one instance of a file-wide artifact: 162 four-part clause headings are at level 2 against 620 at level 3, so the fix should be a systematic re-levelling sweep rather than a one-line patch on page 662.

### p666 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.19.4  General rules  — a fourth-level clause heading under 14.9.19 IF statement, identical in rank to 14.9.19.1/.2/.3.
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.19.4 General rules   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27501 — level 2, while '### 14.9.19.1 General', '### 14.9.19.2 General formats' and '### 14.9.19.3 Syntax rules' are level 3)
- **Why:** Same promotion defect: General rules for the IF statement renders as a sibling of the IF statement clause instead of a child, corrupting the heading tree.
- **Repair:** Demote line 27501 to '### 14.9.19.4 General rules'.
- **Verifier:** REAL, but mislabeled and off-by-three on the line number. (1) kind should be 'heading-level' / 'heading-promotion', NOT 'missing-heading' — nothing is missing; the heading text '14.9.19.4 General rules' is present and character-for-character correct. The sole defect is that it is emitted at markdown level 2 (##) instead of level 3 (###). (2) The cited line 27501 is wrong: line 27503 holds the anchor '<a id="section-14-9-19-4"></a>' and line 27504 holds '## 14.9.19.4 General rules'. (3) Most important for remediation: this is NOT an isolated instance and must not be fixed as a one-line edit. specs/ISO_COBOL.md contains 162 four-digit headings emitted at level 2 against 620 emitted correctly at level 3 — a systematic ~21% transcription defect spanning the document (e.g. '## 6.2.3.2 Syntax rules' line 2947, '## 7.2.4.4 General rules' line 3587, and the immediate neighbours of this instance: '## 14.9.18.4 General rules' line 27392 for GOBACK and '## 14.9.20.4 General rules' line 27603 for INITIALIZE, whose own .1/.2/.3 siblings are all level 3). ### p667 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** In the 14.9.20.2 general format the underlines mark exactly these words as required: INITIALIZE, FILLER, ALL, VALUE, REPLACING, BY, DEFAULT. The words WITH, TO, THEN and DATA are printed WITHOUT underlines and are therefore optional words — so `INITIALIZE X FILLER`, `INITIALIZE X ALL VALUE`, `INITIALIZE X REPLACING NUMERIC BY 0` and `INITIALIZE X DEFAULT` are all legal.
- **Markdown:** ```
INITIALIZE { identifier-1 } ... [ WITH FILLER ]

⎡ ⎧ ALL        ⎫              ⎤
⎢ ⎨            ⎬ TO VALUE     ⎥
⎣ ⎩ category-name ⎭           ⎦

⎡                    ⎧                    ⎧ identifier-2 ⎫ ⎫    ⎤
⎢ THEN REPLACING     ⎨ category-name DATA BY ⎨             ⎬ ⎬ ...⎥
⎣                    ⎩                    ⎩ literal-1    ⎭ ⎭    ⎦

[ THEN TO DEFAULT ]
```  — with no figure note attached.
- **Why:** The code block carries no underlining and, unlike the `where category-name is:` figure immediately below it on the same page (which DOES get a note ending "Every one of the thirteen words is underlined (required word) in the printed figure"), this figure gets no compensating note. The required/optional split is therefore unrecoverable from the transcription for five separate word pairs — WITH/FILLER, TO/VALUE, THEN/REPLACING, DATA/BY and THEN TO/DEFAULT — so a reader cannot tell that the noise words may be omitted. The failure mode is the falsely-restrictive one: an implementation built from this text requires WITH, TO, THEN and DATA and rejects legal source.
- **Repair:** Add a figure note under the 14.9.20.2 code block in the same form already used elsewhere on the page: "INITIALIZE, FILLER, ALL, VALUE, REPLACING, BY and DEFAULT are underlined in the printed standard (required words); WITH, TO, THEN and DATA are printed without underlines (optional words)."
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with two small refinements to the wording.

(1) Taxonomy. The figure's *text* is transcribed correctly — words, brackets, braces, and ellipses all match the print. What is missing is the underline (required-word) annotation and the compensating `Figure notes` block that the transcription applies to comparable figures elsewhere. "incorrect-figure" is defensible but "figure missing its required-word / underline note" is the precise defect; the fix is to append a note to specs/ISO_COBOL.md after line 27550 rather than to alter the code block's characters.

(2) Count. It is five *sites* but four distinct optional words: WITH (before FILLER), TO (before VALUE), THEN (before REPLACING), DATA (before BY), and THEN + TO (before DEFAULT) — TO and THEN each appear twice. Required words are INITIALIZE, FILLER, ALL, VALUE, REPLACING, BY, DEFAULT; `identifier-1`, `identifier-2`, `literal-1` and `category-name` are lowercase metavariables and unaffected.

Suggested note text: "**Figure notes (14.9.20.2 general format).** INITIALIZE, FILLER, ALL, VALUE, REPLACING, BY and DEFAULT are underlined in the printed standard (required words). WITH, TO (in both `TO VALUE` and `THEN TO DEFAULT`), THEN (in both `THEN REPLACING` and `THEN TO DEFAULT`) and DATA are printed **without** underlines — they are optional words per 8.3.1.1.2 and may be omitted. Hence `INITIALIZE X FILLER`, `INITIALIZE X ALL VALUE`, `INITIALIZE X REPLACING NUMERIC BY 0` and `INITIALIZE X DEFAULT` are all legal."

Actionable follow-up beyond the transcription: src/Cobol.Net.Frontend/Grammar/Core/CobolData.g4 lines 567 and 578 make TO mandatory and must become optional (`(ALL | initializeCategory)? TO? VALUE` and `THEN? TO? DEFAULT`), with an edition-gate sweep and goldens covering `INITIALIZE X ALL VALUE` and `INITIALIZE X DEFAULT`.

### p668 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.20.4  General rules  — a fourth-level clause heading under 14.9.20 INITIALIZE statement.
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.20.4 General rules   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27600 — level 2, while the sibling sub-clauses 14.9.20.1/.2/.3 are level 3)
- **Why:** Same promotion defect: General rules for the INITIALIZE statement renders as a sibling of the INITIALIZE statement clause instead of a child, corrupting the heading tree.
- **Repair:** Demote line 27600 to '### 14.9.20.4 General rules'.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with two corrections to how it was described.

(1) Line number: the heading is at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 27603, not 27600. Line 27600 is the body text of Syntax rule 8 ('If the REPLACING phrase is specified, ...'); line 27602 is the anchor '<a id="section-14-9-20-4"></a>'; line 27603 is '## 14.9.20.4 General rules'.

(2) The kind label 'missing-heading' is a misnomer. Nothing is missing: the heading is present with its clause number and title transcribed verbatim and correctly ('14.9.20.4 General rules'), and all four of its numbered general rules follow intact. The defect is purely heading DEPTH — it is emitted as level 2 ('##') when its three siblings 14.9.20.1 General, 14.9.20.2 General format and 14.9.20.3 Syntax rules (lines 27534/27539/27576) are level 3 ('###'), and its parent 14.9.20 INITIALIZE statement (line 27531) is itself level 2. A more accurate kind would be 'heading-level-promotion' or 'wrong-heading-depth'.

The PDF corroborates the intended depth: on page 667 the parent and .1/.2/.3 all print in one bold sub-clause face, and on page 668 '14.9.20.4  General rules' prints in that identical face at the identical margin and number/title tab — a fourth peer, not a new top-level clause.

Likely root cause, worth sweeping rather than patching in isolation: 14.9.20.4 is the first heading after the 667/668 page boundary, and 57 of the 162 document-wide '## N.N.N.N' promotions fall within 8 lines of a page marker, so the transcription appears to lose heading depth across page breaks. Remediation: '##' -> '###' at line 27603, plus a document-wide pass normalizing 4-part clause numbers to level 3 (162 candidates, dominant convention 620x '###').

### p673 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** Format 4 (converting):  INSPECT [ BACKWARD ] identifier-1 CONVERTING { identifier-6 / literal-4 } TO { identifier-7 / literal-5 } [ after-before-phrase ]  — the printed figure has exactly TWO braces, each a balanced open/close pair spanning two stacked rows: the first encloses identifier-6 over literal-4, the second encloses identifier-7 over literal-5.
- **Markdown:** Line 1: "<u>INSPECT</u> [ <u>BACKWARD</u> ] identifier-1 <u>CONVERTING</u> { identifier-6 } <u>TO</u> { identifier-7 } [ after-before-phrase ]"  Line 2 (an inline continuation built from a long run of `&nbsp;` padding): "…{ literal-4 …{ literal-5 }"
- **Why:** The two-line inline rendering produces FOUR brace tokens with mismatched pairing: `{ identifier-6 }` and `{ identifier-7 }` are each closed on line 1, `{ literal-4` on line 2 opens a brace that is never closed at all, and `{ literal-5 }` reads as a third, separate brace group. A reader (or a generator driven off this text) sees identifier-6 and literal-4 as belonging to different enclosures rather than as the two stacked alternatives of one brace, and the unclosed `{ literal-4` is the same class of defect already confirmed in the CALL Format 2 figure. The `&nbsp;`-padded alignment also collapses in any renderer that is not fixed-width, so the visual pairing is not recoverable.
- **Repair:** Replace the two `&nbsp;`-padded lines with a fenced code block using the same box-drawing convention as the rest of page 673, e.g. `<u>INSPECT</u> [ <u>BACKWARD</u> ] identifier-1 <u>CONVERTING</u>` followed by a code block showing `⎧ identifier-6 ⎫ / ⎨ … ⎬ / ⎩ literal-4 ⎭` then `TO` then `⎧ identifier-7 ⎫ / ⎨ … ⎬ / ⎩ literal-5 ⎭` then `[ after-before-phrase ]`, with a figure note recording that INSPECT, BACKWARD, CONVERTING and TO are the underlined (required) words.
- **Verifier:** Page 673, INSPECT Format 4 (converting) general format. PDF prints TWO plain (non-choice) brace enclosures, each balanced and each spanning two stacked rows: { identifier-6 over literal-4 } TO { identifier-7 over literal-5 }, followed by [ after-before-phrase ]. The markdown at specs/ISO_COBOL.md:27786-27787 flattens this into an unfenced two-source-line inline approximation containing FOUR '{' against THREE '}': `{ identifier-6 }` and `{ identifier-7 }` are closed on line 1, while line 2 (69 leading &nbsp;) contributes an unclosed `{ literal-4` and a spurious standalone `{ literal-5 }`. Beyond the mispairing described in the original claim, the &nbsp; padding does not align even in monospace (braces at columns 46/66 on line 1 versus 70/92 on line 2), and because the block is not fenced and line 1 carries no hard break, CommonMark joins the two lines into one rendered line - placing `{ literal-4 ... { literal-5 }` after `[ after-before-phrase ]` and destroying the stacked-alternative relationship entirely rather than merely degrading it. Fix by transcribing Format 4 the same way the tallying-phrase figure on the same page already is: a fenced 2D block with box-drawing brace glyphs plus a Figure notes paragraph recording that both enclosures are plain braces (exactly one alternative selected from each).

### p680 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** FORMAT 3

19) A format 3 INSPECT statement is interpreted and executed as though two successive INSPECT statements ...

FORMAT 4

20) A format 4 INSPECT statement is interpreted and executed as though a format 2 INSPECT statement ...   [both 'FORMAT 3' and 'FORMAT 4' are printed as ordinary body text in the same size/weight as the sibling labels 'FORMAT 1', 'FORMATS 2 AND 3' and 'FORMAT 2' on pages 675/676/679 - they are format-applicability labels inside 14.9.22.4 General rules, not headings]
- **Markdown:** ## FORMAT 3

- **Why:** '## FORMAT 3' is the same heading rank as '## 14.9.22 INSPECT statement' (line 27762). Any heading-based section extraction over ISO_COBOL.md therefore terminates 14.9.22.4 General rules at rule 18 and re-parents General rules 19 through 23 (the entire normative definition of INSPECT formats 3 and 4, including EC-RANGE-INSPECT-SIZE for format 4) into two bogus top-level sections named 'FORMAT 3' and 'FORMAT 4'. A citation lookup for 14.9.22.4 GR 20 will silently miss it. The inconsistency is local and self-evident: the identical labels on the immediately preceding and following pages are plain text.

(a) Direction is inverted. kind should be 'spurious-heading' / 'over-promoted-heading', not 'missing-heading'. No heading is missing and no text is missing; two lines of ordinary body text were wrongly promoted to level-2 ATX headings. The claim's own prose ('promoted to level-2 ATX headings') is accurate - only the kind label contradicts it.

(b) Every line number in the claim is 3 too low. Actual: '## FORMAT 3' is at line 28032 (claimed 28029); '## FORMAT 4' at 28036 (claimed 28033); '## 14.9.22 INSPECT statement' at 27765 (claimed 27762); the plain-text 'FORMAT 2' at 28010 (claimed 28007).

(c) The truncation collision is nearer than described. The heading whose block actually gets cut is '## 14.9.22.4 General rules' at line 27895 - which is itself anomalously '##' where its sibling '### 14.9.21.4 General rules' (line 27738) is '###'. Naming '## 14.9.22 INSPECT statement' as the rank-peer is true but indirect; the operative peer is the General rules heading two levels down that shares the same '##' rank.

(d) Scope is broader than one page. The same over-promotion recurs at 6 further sites in specs/ISO_COBOL.md - lines 6283 ('## FORMAT 1'), 6287 ('## FORMAT 2'), 6291 ('## FORMAT 3'), 6301 ('## FORMAT 4'), 19803 ('## FORMAT 4') and 25363 ('## FORMAT 3') - against roughly 200 correctly-plain FORMAT labels. The fix should demote all 8, not just the two on page 680.

(e) Impact confirmed by execution, and worth recording as such: replaying extract_rule_catalog.py's ANY_HEADING/PAGE/RUNNING_HEADER logic truncates 14.9.22.4 General rules after GR 18, orphaning GR 19-23 including GR 22's EC-RANGE-INSPECT-SIZE size-equality requirement for format 4.

### p696 · missing-text · **structural**

- **PDF:** Table 16 has a two-level header: a spanning cell 'Category of receiving operand' across all six data columns, above the individual column labels 'Alphabetic', 'Alphanumeric-edited, Alphanumeric', 'Boolean', 'National, National-edited', 'Numeric, Numeric-edited', 'Type-name'; the stub cell to its left reads 'Category of sending operand'.
- **Markdown:** | Category of sending operand | | Alphabetic | Alphanumeric-edited, Alphanumeric | Boolean | National, National-edited | Numeric, Numeric-edited | Type-name |
- **Why:** The spanning header 'Category of receiving operand' is printed in the PDF but absent from the transcription. It is the label that identifies the column axis; without it the table is only interpretable by inferring the axis from the stub, and a reader consulting the table to decide whether a MOVE is legal has no printed statement of which axis is the receiving operand. The Yes/No body of the table was checked cell by cell against the PDF and is transcribed correctly.
- **Repair:** Reintroduce the column-group label, e.g. add a first header row '|  |  | Category of receiving operand | | | | | |' or fold it into the header text as '| Category of sending operand \\ Category of receiving operand | | Alphabetic | ... |'.
- **Verifier:** The claim is accurate as filed; two refinements for the ledger. (a) Scope: only the SPANNING row is lost. The transcription does preserve the stub 'Category of sending operand' and does preserve the empty second column that carries the Integer/Noninteger sub-labels, so the row axis remains inferable - the omission is the printed column-axis label, not the whole header. In the PDF the stub cell spans two header rows AND both stub columns; the 'Category of receiving operand' cell spans the six data columns. (b) Impact, stated concretely: because Table 16 is asymmetric (Alphabetic -> National = Yes but National -> Alphabetic = No; Numeric/Integer -> National-edited = Yes but National-edited -> Numeric = No), a transposed reading yields wrong MOVE-legality verdicts, so this is a semantic loss and not merely cosmetic. Remedy note: a GFM pipe table cannot express a colspan, so the fix is to restore the label textually - e.g. rename the six column headers as 'Receiving: Alphabetic', ... or add an italic line above the table reading 'Columns: Category of receiving operand. Rows: Category of sending operand.' Severity 'structural' is appropriate.

### p705 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** where sharing-phrase is:  SHARING WITH { ALL OTHER / NO OTHER / READ ONLY } — verified at 800 dpi: SHARING, ALL, NO, READ and ONLY carry underlines; WITH and BOTH occurrences of OTHER are printed without an underline and are therefore optional words.
- **Markdown:** ```
                ⎧ ALL OTHER  ⎫
SHARING WITH    ⎨ NO OTHER   ⎬
                ⎩ READ ONLY  ⎭
```  — with no figure note attached.
- **Why:** The OPEN general format directly above this on the same page DOES carry a figure note that records its underlining ("OPEN, INPUT, OUTPUT, I-O, EXTEND, NO and REWIND are underlined in the printed figure (required words); WITH is not underlined (optional word)"), but that note scopes only to the OPEN figure and says nothing about the sharing-phrase. Without it the transcription makes OTHER look like a required word, so `OPEN INPUT F SHARING WITH ALL` and `SHARING WITH NO` — both legal — read as illegal, and `WITH` likewise reads as mandatory.
- **Repair:** Add a figure note under the sharing-phrase code block: "SHARING, ALL, NO, READ and ONLY are underlined in the printed standard (required words); WITH and OTHER are printed without underlines (optional words), so SHARING ALL, SHARING WITH ALL, SHARING WITH ALL OTHER, SHARING NO and SHARING WITH NO OTHER are all the same phrase."
- **Verifier:** Accurate as filed; only one immaterial correction. The claim says the underlining was 'verified at 800 dpi' — I verified it at 900 dpi and, more decisively, by extracting the underline rules as PDF vector geometry (the page's 24 drawing objects), which removes any reliance on visual judgement. The substance is exactly right: in the §14.9.27.2 sharing-phrase figure the printed rules cover SHARING, ALL, NO, READ and ONLY, while WITH and both occurrences of OTHER are printed bare. FIX: append a figure note after the closing fence at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 28841, worded to match the existing sibling note at line 28613/15613 — e.g. '> **Figure notes (14.9.27.2, sharing-phrase):** `SHARING`, `ALL`, `NO`, `READ`, and `ONLY` are underlined in the printed figure (required words). `WITH` and both occurrences of `OTHER` are **not** underlined — they are optional words. The braces are a plain required choice: exactly one of the three alternatives shall be selected. No choice indicators appear in this figure.' Two consequences worth carrying into the compiler: `SHARING [WITH] ALL [OTHER]`, `SHARING [WITH] NO [OTHER]` and `SHARING [WITH] READ ONLY` must all parse (OTHER and WITH optional in every combination), and READ ONLY must require BOTH words since both are underlined. Note also that the OPEN figure note at line 28833 is itself correct and complete for the OPEN figure — the defect is solely the absent note on the subordinate sharing-phrase figure, not an error in the existing one.

### p723 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.30.3   Syntax rules   (a fourth-level clause subordinate to 14.9.30 READ statement, printed in the same style as 14.9.30.1 General and 14.9.30.2 General formats on page 722)
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.30.3 Syntax rules   (H2 - while the immediately preceding siblings on page 722 are '### 14.9.30.1 General' and '### 14.9.30.2 General formats', and the parent is '## 14.9.30 READ statement')
- **Why:** The Syntax rules clause is hoisted to the same outline depth as the READ statement clause that contains it, so it detaches from READ in any TOC/outline and splits the statement's four sub-clauses across two levels. Neighbouring statements are transcribed correctly ('### 14.9.29.3 Syntax rules', '### 14.9.31.3 Syntax rules').
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md change line 29496 from '## 14.9.30.3 Syntax rules' to '### 14.9.30.3 Syntax rules'.
- **Verifier:** Real, but mislabelled and under-scoped. Correct kind is 'wrong-heading-level', not 'missing-heading': no text is absent from the transcription - the heading '14.9.30.3 Syntax rules', its anchor 'section-14-9-30-3' and all twelve syntax rules are present and faithful. The defect is that specs/ISO_COBOL.md:29499 emits the clause as H2 ('## 14.9.30.3 Syntax rules'), making it a sibling of its own parent '## 14.9.30 READ statement' (line 29441) instead of a child alongside '### 14.9.30.1 General' (29444) and '### 14.9.30.2 General formats' (29449). Scope is larger than claimed: '## 14.9.30.4 General rules' (line 29545) carries the identical defect, so READ loses two of its four sub-clauses, not one. Further, this is a systemic transcription pattern rather than an isolated page-723 slip - about 40 fourth-level clauses across section 14.9 are emitted at H2 (14.9.1.3/.4, 14.9.2.3/.4, 14.9.4.3, 14.9.10.4, 14.9.11.4, 14.9.12.4, 14.9.13.3/.4, 14.9.14.3/.4, 14.9.18.4, 14.9.19.4, 14.9.20.4, 14.9.22.3/.4, 14.9.23.4, 14.9.24.4, 14.9.25.4, 14.9.26.4, 14.9.27.4, 14.9.28.3/.4, 14.9.30.3/.4, 14.9.37.3/.4, 14.9.38.4, 14.9.39.3/.4, 14.9.40.3/.4, 14.9.44.3/.4, 14.9.48.4, 14.9.49.3/.4, 14.9.51.3/.4), against 35 transcribed correctly at H3. Any repair should be a document-wide sweep normalizing four-component clause numbers to H3, not a one-line edit to page 723. Finally, the claim's phrase 'printed in the same style as 14.9.30.1' is true but not itself the evidence of depth: the PDF sets 14.9.30, .1, .2 and .3 in one identical bold face, so subordination is established by the dotted clause numbering alone.

### p724 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.30.4   General rules   (a fourth-level clause subordinate to 14.9.30 READ statement, printed in the same style as 14.9.30.1 General and 14.9.30.2 General formats)
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.30.4 General rules   (H2 - the same level as the parent '## 14.9.30 READ statement', while siblings .1 and .2 are '###')
- **Why:** Same structural defect as 14.9.30.3: the General rules clause is promoted out of READ, so the outline no longer shows it as part of the READ statement. Neighbouring statements are transcribed correctly ('### 14.9.29.4 General rules', '### 14.9.31.4 General rules').
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md change line 29542 from '## 14.9.30.4 General rules' to '### 14.9.30.4 General rules'.
- **Verifier:** Real, but mislabelled and mis-scoped by the claim.

(1) Wrong kind. Nothing is missing. The heading '14.9.30.4 General rules' is transcribed verbatim at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 29545. The defect is HEADING PROMOTION / wrong outline depth: it is emitted as '##' (H2) instead of '###' (H3), making it a markdown-level peer of its own parent '## 14.9.30 READ statement' (line 29441). Kind should be 'heading-level' or 'heading-promotion', not 'missing-heading'.

(2) Wrong scope. 'Neighbouring statements are transcribed correctly' is true locally but wrongly implies an isolated slip. Document-wide, 162 of 782 four-component headings (21%) carry the same H2 promotion, and the artifact is heavily concentrated at page boundaries: 45 of the 52 four-component headings that sit at the top of a page marker are H2 (86%), versus 117 of 730 mid-page (16%). Page 724's instance is one of that cohort - 14.9.30.4 is the first heading on the page, exactly like 14.9.30.3 at the top of page 723 (line 29499). The transcription pipeline appears to lose enclosing-hierarchy context when it starts a new page, and sporadically elsewhere.

(3) Correct remediation. Fix by a global sweep that derives heading depth from the dotted clause number's component count (3 components -> H2, 4 -> H3, and so on), re-leveling all 162 offenders, rather than by a one-off edit to line 29545. The PDF provides no font cue to distinguish these levels - on page 722 '14.9.30', '14.9.30.1' and '14.9.30.2' are set in identical bold - so the numbering is the only authority for depth and is the correct basis for the sweep.

(4) Impact, stated precisely. No normative content is lost or altered; rules 1)-8) are complete and correct, and citation by clause number is unaffected. The harm is confined to outline/TOC nesting and any tooling that slices sections by markdown heading depth.

### p751 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.37.3   Syntax rules  — printed in exactly the same bold heading style/size as its siblings 14.9.37.1 General and 14.9.37.2 General formats (p.720), i.e. a fourth-level subsection of 14.9.37 SEARCH statement.
- **Markdown:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 30473: "## 14.9.37.3 Syntax rules" — H2, whereas line 30407 "### 14.9.37.1 General" and line 30412 "### 14.9.37.2 General formats" are H3 and line 30404 "## 14.9.37 SEARCH statement" is itself H2.
- **Why:** The Syntax rules subsection is promoted out of 14.9.37 and becomes a sibling of the SEARCH statement itself. Any outline/TOC/anchor-walking tool (and any human reading the nesting) sees the SEARCH syntax rules as a top-level clause rather than part of SEARCH, and the 14.9.37 section appears to end after its General formats. The same pages' sibling statement 14.9.36 (ROLLBACK, p.748) is transcribed correctly with all four subsections at H3, so this is an inconsistency internal to the transcription, not a rendering of the PDF.
- **Repair:** Change line 30473 from "## 14.9.37.3 Syntax rules" to "### 14.9.37.3 Syntax rules". NOTE: this is a pattern, not a one-off — the same promotion occurs at line 30519 (14.9.37.4), 30682 (14.9.38.4), 30946 (14.9.39.3) and 31157 (14.9.39.4). Sweep every `^## \d+\.\d+\.\d+\.\d+` heading in the file.
- **Verifier:** WRONG HEADING LEVEL (not a missing heading). specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 30476 renders '## 14.9.37.3 Syntax rules' at H2 — the same depth as its own parent '## 14.9.37 SEARCH statement' (line 30407) — whereas the PDF's embedded outline marks 14.9.37 as level 3 and 14.9.37.1/.2/.3/.4 as level 4. It should be H3, matching '### 14.9.37.1 General' (30410) and '### 14.9.37.2 General formats' (30415). Three corrections to the claim as filed: (a) the cited line numbers are each 3 low — use 30407/30410/30415/30476; (b) the claim under-reports the same clause: '## 14.9.37.4 General rules' at line 30522 carries the IDENTICAL defect, so BOTH remaining subsections of SEARCH are promoted out of it, not just Syntax rules; (c) the claim argues from print style, which is not probative — the PDF sets parent and child headings alike at 11.67pt Cambria,Bold, and the only authoritative level signal in the PDF is its 2090-entry bookmark outline. SCOPE — this is emphatically not a page-751 incident. A document-wide sweep comparing every numbered markdown heading against its own transcribed parent finds 751 child headings (of 2079 with a transcribed parent) that fail to nest deeper than their parent; 574 of those sit at H2 under an H2 parent. The defect is strongly enriched at page boundaries: 374 of the 751 (50%) are headings that open a new PDF page, against a 19% baseline (398 of 2098), indicating that page-break furniture ('<a id="page-N">' / '## Page N' / the running head) resets the transcriber's heading-level tracking — exactly the mechanism visible here, where 14.9.37.3 opens p.751 and 14.9.37.4 opens p.752 while the correctly-leveled .1/.2 sit mid-page on p.750. Per the project's 'every bug is a pattern' doctrine, repairing page 751 in isolation leaves 749 siblings; the fix should be a mechanical global re-level pass driven by the PDF outline, which is complete and machine-readable via PyMuPDF get_toc(). MITIGATION on severity: the heading text, the clause number and the '<a id="section-14-9-37-3"></a>' anchor are all intact and correct, so section-anchor citation — the project's actual §-lookup path — still resolves; the practical damage is confined to naive outline/TOC extraction and to human reading of the nesting.

### p752 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.37.4   General rules  — printed in the same bold heading style as 14.9.37.1/.2/.3, i.e. a fourth-level subsection of 14.9.37 SEARCH statement.
- **Markdown:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 30519: "## 14.9.37.4 General rules" — H2, the same level as "## 14.9.37 SEARCH statement" (line 30404), while sibling subsections 14.9.37.1 and 14.9.37.2 are H3.
- **Why:** Same defect as 14.9.37.3: the General rules for SEARCH — the longest and most heavily cited part of the clause (GR 1 through GR 9, pp.722-725) — are detached from 14.9.37 in the document outline and read as an independent top-level section. Cross-references of the form "14.9.37.4, General rules" resolve to a node that is not nested under SEARCH.
- **Repair:** Change line 30519 from "## 14.9.37.4 General rules" to "### 14.9.37.4 General rules", as part of the same sweep of all `^## \d+\.\d+\.\d+\.\d+` headings noted for page 751.
- **Verifier:** REAL, but re-describe it as follows.

Correct kind: wrong-heading-level, NOT missing-heading. The heading text "14.9.37.4 General rules" is present verbatim in the markdown at line 30522, preceded by its correct anchor <a id="section-14-9-37-4"></a>. No text, no rule, and no meaning is lost. The defect is purely that it is emitted as H2 ("##") where the transcription's own convention and the PDF's own outline both require H3 ("###").

Correct line numbers: the heading is at line 30522, not 30519; "## 14.9.37 SEARCH statement" is at line 30407, not 30404. The claim is uniformly 3 lines low, suggesting it was authored against a slightly different revision of the file.

Correct evidence: drop the typography argument. The PDF prints 14.9.37 (a level-3 heading) in the same bold face and size as 14.9.37.1/.2/.4, so heading style carries no level information in this document. The decisive evidence is the PDF's embedded bookmark outline, which explicitly tags '14.9.37 SEARCH statement' as level 3 and '14.9.37.1' through '14.9.37.4' as level 4.

Correct impact: the stated cross-reference harm does not exist. The string "14.9.37.4" occurs exactly ONCE in the entire markdown — the heading itself — and the document contains ZERO cross-references of the form "N.N.N.N, General rules". The spec cites these rules as "(14.9.37, SEARCH statement, General rule 9)", which resolves correctly and is unaffected. The real harm is confined to structure-consuming tooling: any TOC builder, outline walker, or rule-catalog extractor that infers parentage from heading depth will attribute SEARCH's GR 1-9 to a top-level section instead of to 14.9.37, which is precisely the extractor-bug class this reconciliation was commissioned to fix.

Scope note for the ledger: this is not isolated to SEARCH. 14.9.37.3 (line 30476) has the identical defect, as do 14.9.38.4 (30685), 14.9.39.3 (30949) and 14.9.39.4 (31160); document-wide 162 four-part headings are H2 against 620 correctly at H3. Treat this as one instance of a recurring level-demotion class and fix it with a single sweep keyed on heading-number depth, rather than as a page-752 one-off.

Fix: change line 30522 from "## 14.9.37.4 General rules" to "### 14.9.37.4 General rules".

### p760 · misplaced-content · **structural**

- **PDF:** Page 760 ends with 'Format 9 (program-pointer-assignment):' followed by the single syntax line 'SET { identifier-7 } ... TO  identifier-8'. Nothing further is printed on page 760. Page 761 then OPENS with its own first line, the label 'Format 10 (data-pointer-arithmetic):', above the Format 10 syntax diagram 'SET  { identifier-9 } ... { UP / DOWN } BY arithmetic-expression-3'.
- **Markdown:** ISO_COBOL.md line 30846 (inside the page-760 slice, immediately before the <a id="page-761"></a> anchor): 'SET { identifier-7 } ... TO identifier-8 Format 10 (data-pointer-arithmetic):' -- and the page-761 slice then jumps straight into the fenced Format 10 diagram with NO label of its own.
- **Why:** The Format 10 label has been welded onto the end of the Format 9 syntax skeleton. Read literally, the Format 9 general format now appears to contain the trailing tokens 'Format 10 (data-pointer-arithmetic):', which is not COBOL syntax and corrupts a general-format line; simultaneously the Format 10 diagram is left orphaned with no format label, so a reader (or a generator walking the page anchors) cannot tell which SET format the UP/DOWN BY diagram belongs to. Format 9 and Format 10 are distinct SET formats with different syntax rules and general rules.
- **Repair:** Split line 30846 into 'SET { identifier-7 } ... TO identifier-8' (ending the page-760 content) and move 'Format 10 (data-pointer-arithmetic):' to be the first content line AFTER the <a id="page-761"></a> / '## Page 761' scaffolding and before the Format 10 fenced block, matching where it is printed. While there, consider fencing the Format 8 and Format 9 syntax lines (lines 30842 and 30846) in ``` blocks like every other SET format, so they are not mistaken for prose.
- **Verifier:** The claim is accurate as written; verified against the 300 dpi renders of both PDF pages and against the markdown by exact grep. Three refinements for the fix record. (a) Page mapping: PDF page 760 is printed page 730 and PDF page 761 is printed page 731, so the label crosses a real printed-page boundary, not just a slice boundary. (b) Partial mitigation, which does not defeat the finding: the transcription's editorial figure-notes line at ISO_COBOL.md:30858 does read 'Figure notes (SET statement Format 10 (data-pointer-arithmetic) syntax diagram)', so a human reader can still recover which format the orphaned diagram belongs to. But that annotation is transcriber-added apparatus, not the standard's own printed label, and it sits AFTER the diagram; the normative label line printed at the top of page 761 remains absent from the page-761 slice, and the Format 9 general-format line on page 760 remains corrupted regardless. (c) Root cause and scope of the fix: the Format 9 syntax skeleton at line 30846 is bare paragraph text, unlike Formats 4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 12 and 14 in the same SET run, which are all inside ``` fences. Formats 8 (line 30842) and 13 (line 30887) are unfenced in the same way. The correct repair is to move 'Format 10 (data-pointer-arithmetic):' to be the first content line of the page-761 slice, immediately above its fenced diagram, and to fence the Formats 8, 9 and 13 skeletons so the same fusion cannot recur -- rather than merely splitting line 30846 in place, which would leave the label on the wrong page.

### p762 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** '14.9.39.3   Syntax rules' is printed in exactly the same bold heading style and at the same outline depth as '14.9.39.1   General' and '14.9.39.2   General formats' on page 759 -- all three are fourth-level clause headings subordinate to '14.9.39  SET statement'.
- **Markdown:** ISO_COBOL.md line 30946: '## 14.9.39.3 Syntax rules' (heading level 2), whereas its siblings are line 30738 '### 14.9.39.1 General' and line 30760 '### 14.9.39.2 General formats' (heading level 3), and their parent is line 30735 '## 14.9.39 SET statement' (heading level 2).
- **Why:** At level 2 the Syntax rules clause is promoted to a SIBLING of '14.9.39 SET statement' instead of a child of it, so any tool that builds a table of contents, resolves 'see 14.9.39.3', or extracts the rules belonging to the SET statement will detach the syntax rules from their statement. The same defect recurs at line 31157 ('## 14.9.39.4 General rules', page 763) and at lines 31494 / 31570 for 14.9.40.3 / 14.9.40.4, so the repair should be swept -- but only 14.9.39.3 falls on a page in this batch.
- **Repair:** Demote line 30946 to '### 14.9.39.3 Syntax rules' to match its siblings, and sweep the .3/.4 subclause headings of the other 14.9.x statement sections for the identical promotion (14.9.38.4, 14.9.39.4, 14.9.40.3, 14.9.40.4 are already known instances).
- **Verifier:** Real, but re-scope and re-label it. KIND is not 'missing-heading' -- the heading '14.9.39.3 Syntax rules' is present at specs/ISO_COBOL.md:30946 with its number and title character-exact; it is MIS-LEVELED, emitted as '##' (level 2) where its siblings 14.9.39.1 (:30738) and 14.9.39.2 (:30760) are '###' (level 3), which makes it a sibling of its own parent '## 14.9.39 SET statement' (:30735). The PDF (folio 732) does not distinguish these levels typographically -- 14.9.39, 14.9.39.1, 14.9.39.2 and 14.9.39.3 are all set in the same bold face at the same size -- so the depth is established solely by the four-part clause number, which per ISO numbering makes 14.9.39.3 a child of 14.9.39. IMPACT is narrower than claimed: the anchor '<a id="section-14-9-39-3"></a>' at :30945 is present and correct, so 'see 14.9.39.3' cross-references still resolve; what breaks is TOC depth and any heading-nesting-based extraction of a statement's subclauses. SCOPE is much wider than the four sites listed: 162 of the file's 806 four-part clause headings are at level 2. It is a page-slice artifact -- when the parent three-part heading is on the same transcribed page the child is '###' (551 vs 12), when the parent is on an earlier page the child is '##' (150 vs 69). Fix it as one document-wide normalization pass that sets heading level from the clause number's component count, not as a per-page repair; 14.9.39.3 is merely the instance that happens to land in this batch.

### p778 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.40.4   General rules   (printed bold, the same heading style/level as 14.9.41.4 General rules on page 785)
- **Markdown:** <a id="section-14-9-40-4"></a>
## 14.9.40.4 General rules
- **Why:** Same defect as 14.9.40.3: the clause is transcribed at markdown level 2, level with its parent '## 14.9.40 SORT statement', while the identically-ranked '### 14.9.41.4 General rules' (page 785) is level 3. The SORT clause's general rules therefore fall outside the SORT section in any outline built from heading depth.
- **Repair:** Demote to '### 14.9.40.4 General rules'.
- **Verifier:** Real, but mis-kinded and under-scoped. Correct kind is 'heading-level' (or 'heading-depth'), not 'missing-heading' - the heading text '14.9.40.4 General rules' is transcribed verbatim at the correct position. The actual defect: it is emitted at markdown level 2 ('##') where the transcription's own convention for a 4-component clause number under 14.9 is level 3 ('###'). Best evidence is internal to the same section rather than the cross-section comparison the claim makes: 14.9.40.1 and 14.9.40.2 are '###' while 14.9.40.3 and 14.9.40.4 are '##', so one clause's subsections straddle two depths. Scope correction - this is NOT a page-778 one-off but one instance of a systemic pattern: within 14.9.NN.N alone, 162 such headings are level 3, 40 are level-2 outliers (and 2 are level 4). The outliers are overwhelmingly the '.3 Syntax rules' and '.4 General rules' subsections, including 14.9.1.3/.4, 14.9.13.3/.4, 14.9.39.3/.4, 14.9.40.3/.4, 14.9.51.3/.4. It should therefore be tracked and fixed as a single normalization sweep over the transcription's heading levels (assert markdown level == a fixed offset from clause-number depth, region by region), not patched line by line. Severity 'structural' is appropriate.

### p796 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.46.2 General format:  TERMINATE { report-name-1 } …   - with TERMINATE underlined (confirmed on a 300 dpi crop: a continuous rule runs under the whole word); report-name-1 is not underlined.
- **Markdown:** TERMINATE { report-name-1 } …   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 32275) - no <u> markup, no figure note.
- **Why:** The underline marks TERMINATE as a required word (5.2.6.2). The immediately preceding statement in the same file preserves exactly this marking - 14.9.45.2 is transcribed as '<u>SUPPRESS</u> PRINTING' (line 32228) - so the TERMINATE figure has silently lost information its neighbour kept, and a reader or generator consuming the markdown has no way to know TERMINATE is required rather than optional.
- **Repair:** Transcribe as '<u>TERMINATE</u> { report-name-1 } …' to match the SUPPRESS convention two sections earlier.
- **Verifier:** Real, and the substance is correctly reported, but two citations in the claim are wrong and should be fixed before the finding is filed.

CORRECTED LINE NUMBERS. The claim cites the <a id=...> anchor lines rather than the content lines, off by three in both cases. The TERMINATE general format is at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 32278 (32275 is '<a id="section-14-9-46-2"></a>'). The SUPPRESS comparison is at line 32231 (32228 is '<a id="section-14-9-45-2"></a>').

CORRECTED CONVENTION CITATION. The underline rule is NOT 5.2.6.2 - that section is 'Brackets' (line 2691) and concerns [ ] optionality. The governing text is 5.2.2 Keywords (line 2631), 'shown in uppercase and underlined in general formats. They are required in order to select the functionality associated with that keyword', paired with 5.2.3 Optional words (line 2644), 'shown in uppercase and not underlined... They may be written to add clarity.'

STRONGER EVIDENCE THAN THE CLAIM OFFERS. The claim rests its case on the preceding statement, SUPPRESS. The better witness is INITIATE at line 27726, '<u>INITIATE</u> { report-name-1 } …' - byte-for-byte the same format shape as TERMINATE (one-line plain text, brace-wrapped report-name-1, trailing ellipsis) and the paired statement that opens the report TERMINATE closes. Its underline is preserved. This rules out the main innocent explanation, that this transcription simply does not carry underlines in general formats: 156 <u> tags exist file-wide and the exact structural twin retains its own.

SHARPENED IMPACT. Because 5.2.3 assigns a definite meaning to an uppercase word printed WITHOUT an underline, the markdown does not merely omit information - it affirmatively encodes TERMINATE as an optional word that may be omitted from the statement. A grammar or reserved-word generator reading the transcription would derive an omittable verb for the TERMINATE statement.

FIX. Change line 32278 to '<u>TERMINATE</u> { report-name-1 } …' to match INITIATE at 27726 and SUPPRESS at 32231.

### p798 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.47.2 General format:  UNLOCK  file-name-1  [ RECORD / RECORDS ]  - with UNLOCK underlined (confirmed on a 300 dpi crop); file-name-1, RECORD and RECORDS are not underlined, and the RECORD/RECORDS bracket is plain (no choice-indicator bars).
- **Markdown:** ```
UNLOCK   file-name-1  [RECORD ]
                      [RECORDS]
```  (specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 32332-32336) - no underline markup and no '> **Figure notes ...**' paragraph.
- **Why:** UNLOCK is a required word and its underline is the only thing in the printed figure that says so (5.2.6.2). Because the figure is inside a code fence the underline cannot be shown inline, and this file's established remedy - a figure-notes paragraph recording the underlined words - is missing here, so the information is simply gone. (The bracket itself is correctly plain; only the underline is lost.)
- **Repair:** Add '> **Figure notes (UNLOCK statement general format).** `UNLOCK` is underlined in the printed standard (required word); `file-name-1`, `RECORD` and `RECORDS` are not. The bracket is plain - no choice indicators - so at most one of RECORD / RECORDS is written.' after the fence.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with two corrections. (a) Line numbers: the fenced figure is at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 32335-32338; 32332-32336 spans the <a id> anchor and the '### 14.9.47.2 General format' heading. (b) Severity is better recorded as minor/informational rather than structural. Nothing in the transcription is WRONG: the words, their order, the single tall plain bracket rendered as stacked [RECORD ] / [RECORDS], and the absence of choice-indicator bars all match the printed page exactly. What is missing is an annotation, not a correction - the figure-notes paragraph recording that UNLOCK is underlined (required word, 5.2.6.2 / lines 2631, 2644) and that file-name-1, RECORD and RECORDS are printed without underlines (optional words). The practical implementable loss is small, because a statement verb is required by definition and a non-underlined single word alone inside a bracket is syntactically indistinguishable from an underlined one inside the same bracket - RECORD, RECORDS or nothing, either way. The fidelity gap is nonetheless real and should be closed with the file's standard remedy: '> **Figure notes (UNLOCK statement general format).** `UNLOCK` is underlined in the printed standard (required word). `RECORD` and `RECORDS` are **not** underlined - they are optional words. The two alternatives are enclosed in a single plain bracket, so at most one may be specified; there are no choice indicators in this figure.' INCIDENTAL, outside the claim and not adjudicated here: the ASCII rendering as two per-line brackets differs from this file's usual tall-bracket drawing (the corner glyphs used in the adjacent UNSTRING figure) and could be misread as two independent sequential optional phrases; the figure-notes paragraph above would also neutralize that.

### p799 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** In 14.9.48.2 the following words are printed with underlines (required words): UNSTRING, DELIMITED, ALL (both occurrences), OR, INTO, DELIMITER, COUNT, POINTER, TALLYING, OVERFLOW (both occurrences), NOT, END-UNSTRING. BY, WITH, IN and ON are printed WITHOUT underlines (optional words).
- **Markdown:** "UNSTRING identifier-1" ... "| DELIMITED BY [ ALL ] { identifier-2 } [ OR [ ALL ] { identifier-3 } ] … |" ... "INTO { identifier-4 [ DELIMITER IN identifier-5 ] [ COUNT IN identifier-6 ] } …" ... "[ WITH POINTER identifier-7 ]" ... "[ TALLYING IN identifier-8 ]" ... "[ END-UNSTRING ]" - no underline markup anywhere and no '> **Figure notes ...**' paragraph after the figure (specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 32372-32392).
- **Why:** Underlining is the standard's only marker separating required words from optional words (5.2.6.2). With it gone and no figure-notes paragraph to carry the information, the transcription cannot tell an implementer that e.g. BY, WITH and IN are omissible while DELIMITED, POINTER and TALLYING are not. Every other statement figure in this file that uses a code fence records the underlines in a figure note (START 31759, STRING 31970, CALL 25700, SEND 30641, SET 30770-30838 ...), so UNSTRING is an unswept gap rather than a different convention.
- **Repair:** Add a '> **Figure notes (UNSTRING statement general format syntax diagram).**' paragraph after the figure listing the underlined required words (UNSTRING, DELIMITED, ALL, OR, INTO, DELIMITER, COUNT, POINTER, TALLYING, OVERFLOW, NOT, END-UNSTRING) and the non-underlined optional words (BY, WITH, IN, ON).
- **Verifier:** The claim is accurate as written; two additional defects in the SAME figure were found during verification and should be fixed in the same sweep. (1) LOST CHOICE INDICATORS. Looking at the printed glyphs of the ON OVERFLOW group: on each side there is an outer square bracket with visible top and bottom serif corners, and immediately inside it a plain FULL-HEIGHT VERTICAL BAR WITH NO CORNERS - the choice indicator. The markdown renders this as `⌐ ⌐ ... ¬ ¬` over `| | ... | |`, i.e. two corner glyphs per side, which draws a second NESTED BRACKET rather than a bracket plus a bar. Per 5.2.6.4 a bracket enclosing choice indicators means zero or more of the alternatives, each at most once, in ANY ORDER - so UNSTRING may carry neither phrase, ON OVERFLOW alone, NOT ON OVERFLOW alone, or BOTH IN EITHER ORDER. Rendered as a plain nested bracket that permission is lost. This is precisely the construct the START (line 31763) and STRING (line 31974) figure notes each flag with a ⚠, so UNSTRING needs the same ⚠ note. (2) FRAGMENTED FIGURE.
