In the PDF 14.9.48.2 is one continuous syntax diagram, but the markdown splits it across two code fences (lines 32377-32382 and 32388-32393) with `UNSTRING identifier-1` (32375), the `INTO …` line (32384), `[ WITH POINTER identifier-7 ]` (32385), `[ TALLYING IN identifier-8 ]` (32386) and `[ END-UNSTRING ]` (32395) left OUTSIDE any fence as ordinary Markdown prose, so the general format never renders as a single monospaced diagram and its alignment is not preserved. Minor cosmetic note: both fences close with `L ... ⌐`, using the top-left corner glyph ⌐ for the bottom-RIGHT corner. Also, the figure's true line span is 32372-32395 (the claim wrote 32372-32392, which stops before the `[ END-UNSTRING ]` line); this does not affect the finding.

### p816 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 14.9.51.3   Syntax rules  (printed in the same bold sub-clause style as 14.9.51.1 General and 14.9.51.2 General formats on page 815)
- **Markdown:** ## 14.9.51.3 Syntax rules   (H2, while its siblings on page 815 are '### 14.9.51.1 General' and '### 14.9.51.2 General formats')
- **Why:** A four-level clause number (14.9.51.3) is promoted to the same heading level as the statement clause itself (## 14.9.51 WRITE statement). The syntax rules stop being nested inside the WRITE statement clause, which breaks the document outline, any TOC generated from it, and any tooling that resolves a citation like '14.9.51.3' by walking the heading tree. ISO_COBOL.md line 33010 (and 33074 for 14.9.51.4, on a later page) carry the same defect.
- **Repair:** Demote to '### 14.9.51.3 Syntax rules' (ISO_COBOL.md line 33010) to match 14.9.51.1/.2. Sweep 14.9.51.4 at line 33074 in the same change.
- **Verifier:** Real, but mislabeled and under-scoped. (a) The kind is not 'missing-heading' -- nothing is missing. The heading is present at ISO_COBOL.md:33010 with text matching the PDF exactly; the defect is a heading-LEVEL / nesting error ('wrong-heading-level'). (b) This is not a two-line local defect. It is one instance of a document-wide transcription pattern: 162 four-level clause headings in ISO_COBOL.md are emitted as '##' against 620 emitted as '###', spanning 6.2.3.2 through 16.2.1.2. The correct remedy is a single mechanical sweep normalizing every N.N.N.N heading to H3 (and re-checking the corresponding N.N.N and N.N depths), not a per-page edit of lines 33010 and 33074. (c) The stated impact is overstated on one point: citation resolution does not in fact break, because each heading carries an explicit anchor -- line 33009 is '<a id="section-14-9-51-3"></a>' -- and anchors, not heading depth, are the resolution mechanism; furthermore the file already interleaves '## Page NNN' markers at H2 (e.g. line 32939 '## Page 816' region), so no consumer can be walking a pure clause tree in the first place. The genuine impact is narrower: a generated TOC/outline and any depth-based chunking that attributes rule text to its owning clause will place 14.9.51.3/.4 outside 14.9.51. (d) No normative content is affected -- the 9 syntax rules on page 816 transcribe correctly, including the rule 7) a)/b) sub-list. Severity is fairly 'structural' only in the markup sense; it is not a semantic divergence from the standard.

### p832 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 15.3.3.3   Permissible values for data associated with common time formats / 15.3.3.4   Local time formats / 15.3.3.5   UTC time formats / 15.3.3.6   Offset time formats / 15.3.3.6.1   Offset subformats
- **Markdown:** `## 15.3.3.3 ...`, `## 15.3.3.4 Local time formats`, `## 15.3.3.5 UTC time formats`, `## 15.3.3.6 Offset time formats`, `## 15.3.3.6.1 Offset subformats` (all level 2), whereas siblings `### 15.3.3.1 Common time formats with integer seconds representation` and `### 15.3.3.2 Common time formats with fractional seconds representation` (page 831) and `### 15.3.3.6.2 Permissible values for data associated with offset time formats` (page 833) are level 3
- **Why:** Same defect as page 830 and additionally one level deeper: 15.3.3.6.1 is emitted at level 2 while its own sibling 15.3.3.6.2 is at level 3, so the two halves of the offset-subformat specification end up at different depths and 15.3.3.6.1 detaches from 15.3.3.6.
- **Repair:** Demote 15.3.3.3, 15.3.3.4, 15.3.3.5 and 15.3.3.6 to `###` (matching 15.3.3.1/.2) and 15.3.3.6.1 to `####` — or, at minimum, make 15.3.3.6.1 match 15.3.3.6.2.
- **Verifier:** The finding is real and the claim describes it accurately. Two refinements. First, on the intended levels: the enclosing subtree fixes the convention as 15.3.3 = level 2 with its .N children = level 3 (honoured at 15.3.3.1, 15.3.3.2 and 15.3.3.7), so 15.3.3.3 through 15.3.3.6 should each be level 3; for 15.3.3.6.1 the transcription's own handling of its sibling 15.3.3.6.2 at line 33630 sets level 3 as the file's local convention for that depth, so 15.3.3.6.1 should be level 3 to match it (a strictly depth-proportional scheme would want level 4, but matching 15.3.3.6.2 is what makes the pair self-consistent). Second, and more important for the fix: this is NOT page-832-local, so a five-heading patch would leave the pattern alive. The identical demotion runs through the preceding sibling group on pages 830-831 -- '## 15.3.1.5 Permissible values for data associated with ordinal date formats' (line 33489), '## 15.3.1.6 Week date formats' (33496) and '## 15.3.1.7 Permissible values for data associated with week date formats' (33503) are level 2 while their siblings 15.3.1.1 through 15.3.1.4 (lines 33443, 33458, 33465, 33474) are level 3. Worse, the subtree root is inverted: '### 15.3 Arguments' (line 33366) is emitted DEEPER than its own child '## 15.3.1 Format arguments to international date and time functions' (line 33440). In every instance the demotion begins at the heading that follows a general-format diagram or a table, which points at a single root cause in the heading-level assignment pass rather than a set of independent slips. The remediation should be a re-level of the whole 15.3 subtree, root included, verified against the numeric depth of each heading.

### p847 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 15.8.2   General format

  FUNCTION  ACOS  ( argument-1 )      [FUNCTION and ACOS are both UNDERLINED in the printed general format]
- **Markdown:** ### 15.8.2 General format

```
FUNCTION ACOS ( argument-1 )
```
- **Why:** Underlining in a general format is normative notation (5.2.6): an underlined word is a REQUIRED word. The transcription drops the underline on both FUNCTION and ACOS and wraps the format in a fenced code block instead. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 34068. The house convention everywhere else in clause 15 is `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>NAME</u>` (e.g. ABS line 34026, ANNUITY line 34102, ATAN line 34190), so any tool or reader deriving required-vs-optional words from the markup reads FUNCTION/ACOS as non-required here while reading them as required three formats away.
- **Repair:** Replace the fenced block at specs/ISO_COBOL.md:34066-34069 with `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>ACOS</u> ( argument-1 )` to match the ABS/ANNUITY/ATAN convention on the surrounding pages.
- **Verifier:** Page 847, 15.8.2 ACOS general format. The printed standard sets `FUNCTION  ACOS  ( argument-1 )` with two separate underlines, one under FUNCTION and one under ACOS; `( argument-1 )` is not underlined. specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 34067-34069 transcribe this as a fenced code block containing bare `FUNCTION ACOS ( argument-1 )`, dropping both underlines with no compensating figure note. Underlining is normative notation -- the transcription's own text at lines 2631 and 2644 states that underlined uppercase words in a general format are required keywords and non-underlined uppercase words are optional words -- so a markup-driven consumer derives that FUNCTION and ACOS are optional, the opposite of the truth. CORRECTION TO THE ORIGINAL REPORT: this is not an isolated deviation from a consistent clause-15 convention. Across clause 15 (lines 34009-38781) only 20 function general formats preserve `<u>FUNCTION</u>`, while 53 are transcribed bare (26 fenced, 27 plain), with only 2 of those 53 carrying any prose note about underlining. ACOS is one instance of a systematic clause-wide gap and should be remediated as such. Also note the fenced block itself is not the defect -- fencing preserves the significant intra-format spacing and is used for 26 other formats -- the defect is solely the dropped underlining; and for ACOS specifically the format contains no optional words, so the loss mis-labels keywords rather than creating an in-format required/optional ambiguity (formats like `FUNCTION LENGTH ( argument-1 [ PHYSICAL ] )`, transcribed bare in the same pattern, carry the higher risk).

### p849 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 15.10.2   General format

  FUNCTION  ASIN  ( argument-1 )      [FUNCTION and ASIN are both UNDERLINED in the printed general format]
- **Markdown:** ### 15.10.2 General format

`FUNCTION ASIN ( argument-1 )`
- **Why:** Underlining in a general format is normative notation (5.2.6): an underlined word is a REQUIRED word. The transcription drops the underline on both FUNCTION and ASIN and renders the format as inline code. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 34152. Adjacent formats in the same clause do preserve it (`<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>ATAN</u>` at line 34190), so the required-word marking is inconsistent within a two-page span.
- **Repair:** Replace specs/ISO_COBOL.md:34152 with `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>ASIN</u> ( argument-1 )`.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED with corrections to the claim's framing. The substance is right: PDF page 849, clause 15.10.2 prints 'FUNCTION  ASIN  ( argument-1 )' with separate underlines under FUNCTION and under ASIN (visually verified at 1.8x; the parenthesized argument is not underlined), while specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 34152 renders it as unmarked inline code '`FUNCTION ASIN ( argument-1 )`', losing the required-word marking that 8.3.1.1 (lines 2631/2644) makes normative.

Two corrections:
(a) The cited counter-example is in a DIFFERENT clause, not 'the same clause'. '<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>ATAN</u>' at line 34190 is clause 15.11.2 on page 850. The nearest same-page/same-clause context is 15.10 itself, which contains only this one format.
(b) It is NOT true that the adjacent formats uniformly preserve the underline -- the inconsistency is wider and runs in both directions. Across five consecutive intrinsic-function clauses: 15.7.2 ABS (34026) uses <u>; 15.8.2 ACOS (34068) drops it and uses a FENCED CODE BLOCK; 15.9.2 ANNUITY (34102) uses <u>; 15.10.2 ASIN (34152) drops it and uses INLINE code; 15.11.2 ATAN (34190) uses <u>. A survey of every 'FUNCTION ...' general-format line in clause 15 shows roughly 20 formats using <u>, roughly 20 using backtick code, and the majority as unmarked plain text.

Therefore this page-849 item is a genuine, individually-verified instance of a SYSTEMIC clause-15 defect (three competing renderings of the same normative construct) rather than a one-off local slip. Fixing it should be scoped as a clause-15 sweep to the <u> convention, with ACOS (15.8.2, line 34068) as the immediately adjacent sibling, not as a single-line patch at 34152.

### p851 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 15.12.2   General format

  FUNCTION  BASECONVERT  ( argument-1  argument-2  argument-3 )      [FUNCTION and BASECONVERT are both UNDERLINED in the printed general format]
- **Markdown:** ### 15.12.2 General format

FUNCTION BASECONVERT ( argument-1 argument-2 argument-3 )
- **Why:** Underlining in a general format is normative notation (5.2.6): an underlined word is a REQUIRED word. The transcription emits the format as bare prose with no underline markup at all. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 34228. BASECONVERT is a COBOL-2023 addition, so this is precisely the kind of format an implementer reads fresh; losing the required-word marking removes the only signal distinguishing required words from optional keywords.
- **Repair:** Replace specs/ISO_COBOL.md:34228 with `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>BASECONVERT</u> ( argument-1 argument-2 argument-3 )`.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with two corrections to the write-up.

Correction 1 - wrong section cited. The claim attributes the underline convention to 5.2.6. 5.2.6 is 'Options' and governs brackets, braces, choice indicators and ellipses, not underlining. The normative basis for underlining is 5.2.2 (Keywords: 'shown in uppercase and underlined in general formats ... required') with its complement 5.2.3 (Optional words: 'shown in uppercase and not underlined'), restated normatively in 8.3.2.4.2 Required words and 8.3.2.4.3 Optional words.

Correction 2 - understated scope. This is not specific to BASECONVERT or to newly added 2023 functions. 74 of the 94 '15.x.2 General format' blocks in the transcription carry no <u> markup anywhere in the block; only 20 do. Affected entries include long-standing 1985/2002 functions (FACTORIAL 15.36.2, INTEGER 15.44.2, MOD 15.64.2, NUMVAL 15.67.2, REM 15.77.2, REVERSE 15.78.2, SQRT 15.84.2, WHEN-COMPILED 15.99.2) alongside the newer ones. BASECONVERT should be filed as one instance of a systemic chapter-15 defect, and the remediation should be a sweep of all 74, not a one-line patch.

Correction 3 - sharpen the impact statement. The consequence is not that the required/optional distinction is 'removed'; it is that it is REVERSED. 8.3.2.4.3 gives the absence of an underline a positive meaning, so an unmarked uppercase word in a general format affirmatively reads as an optional word omittable with no semantic effect. The transcribed line therefore asserts something false about FUNCTION and BASECONVERT rather than merely omitting something true.

Secondary observation, worth noting for the sweep: 15.12.2 is additionally the bare-prose variant. Sibling non-underlined entries at least wrap the format in backticks or a fence (15.13.2 '`FUNCTION BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER ( argument-1 argument-2 )`'), which preserves the this-is-a-general-format-not-narrative cue. 15.12.2 has neither underline nor code formatting, so it is the weakest form of the defect. Corrected target text: '<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>BASECONVERT</u> ( argument-1 argument-2 argument-3 )', matching the 15.11.2 ATAN precedent already in the file.

### p852 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** 15.13.2   General format

  FUNCTION  BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER  ( argument-1  argument-2 )      [FUNCTION and BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER are both UNDERLINED in the printed general format]
- **Markdown:** ### 15.13.2 General format

`FUNCTION BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER ( argument-1 argument-2 )`
- **Why:** Underlining in a general format is normative notation (5.2.6): an underlined word is a REQUIRED word. The transcription drops the underline on both FUNCTION and BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER and renders the format as inline code. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 34265, against the `<u>…</u>` convention used for the same construct at COMBINED-DATETIME (line 34452) and CONCAT (line 34500) a few clauses later.
- **Repair:** Replace specs/ISO_COBOL.md:34265 with `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER</u> ( argument-1 argument-2 )`.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with two corrections to the claim's framing.

(a) WRONG CITATION. The claim cites 5.2.6 for 'underlined word = REQUIRED word'. 5.2.6 is 'Options' and governs brackets, braces and choice indicators only (5.2.6.1–5.2.6.4, specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 2683–2704) — it says nothing about underlining. The governing clauses are 5.2.2 Keywords ('shown in uppercase and underlined in general formats… They are required', line 2631) and 5.2.3 Optional words ('uppercase words that are not underlined', line 2646), restated normatively at 8.3.2.4.2 Required words (line 5773) and 8.3.2.4.3 Optional words (line 5808). Any ledger entry should cite 5.2.2/5.2.3 (+8.3.2.4.2/8.3.2.4.3), not 5.2.6.

(b) UNDERSTATED SCOPE — this is a systemic pattern, not a one-line slip, so the fix is a sweep and not a single edit. In Chapter 15, 21 intrinsic-function general formats are rendered as plain backtick inline code with the underline dropped from BOTH words: lines 34152 (ASIN), 34265 (BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER), 34412 (CHAR-NATIONAL), 34699 (COS), 34786 (DATE-OF-INTEGER), 35005 (DISPLAY-OF), 35047 (E), 35148 (EXCEPTION-FILE-N), 35954 (INTEGER-OF-DATE), 35994 (INTEGER-OF-DAY), 36255 (LOCALE-DATE), 36386 (LOG), 36421 (LOG10), 36670 (MEDIAN), 36761 (MIN), 36912 (NATIONAL-OF), 37148 (NUMVAL-F), 37219 (ORD), 37749 (SIN), 38115 (TAN), 38723 (YEAR-TO-YYYY). Two further formats are half-transcribed — the underline is present on FUNCTION but lost on the function-name: line 34556 `<u>FUNCTION</u> CONVERT (…)` and line 38296 `<u>FUNCTION</u> TEST-NUMVAL (…)`. Only 20 formats carry the full `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>NAME</u>` markup. Correct remedy: rewrite all 23 defective lines to `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>NAME</u> ( … )`, matching the 20 correct siblings, after confirming each against its own printed page.

(c) SEVERITY NOTE. 'structural' is defensible as a notation-fidelity defect but overstates the operational risk for this specific format: the format has no optional uppercase words, and FUNCTION and the function-name are required in every intrinsic-function reference regardless, so no wrong syntax can be derived from the degraded line in practice. Rate it as a notation/convention-fidelity defect to be fixed as a batch sweep, not as a semantics-changing error.

### p859 · incorrect-figure · **structural**

- **PDF:** FUNCTION CONVERT ( argument-1 source-format destination-format )  — BOTH 'FUNCTION' and 'CONVERT' carry an underline in the printed general format (verified by rendering page 859 at 300 dpi and cropping the line: two separate underline rules sit beneath FUNCTION and beneath CONVERT).
- **Markdown:** <u>FUNCTION</u> CONVERT ( argument-1 source-format destination-format )   (ISO_COBOL.md line 34553 — only FUNCTION is wrapped in <u>…</u>; CONVERT is left plain)
- **Why:** Per 5.2.6.2 an underlined word is a REQUIRED word and a non-underlined word is an OPTIONAL word that may be omitted without changing the meaning. Marking FUNCTION underlined but CONVERT not asserts a positive (and false) distinction: it reads as though 'FUNCTION ( argument-1 … )' were legal. Every sibling function in clause 15 uses the '<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>NAME</u>' form (ABS, ANNUITY, ATAN, COMBINED-DATETIME, CONCAT, CURRENT-DATE, …), so CONVERT is the outlier. The identical defect exists at line 38293 for TEST-NUMVAL.
- **Repair:** Change ISO_COBOL.md line 34553 to '<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>CONVERT</u> ( argument-1 source-format destination-format )'. Sweep the sibling defect at line 38293 (<u>FUNCTION</u> TEST-NUMVAL) in the same change set.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with one citation correction. On printed page 859, clause 15.19.2 General format, the standard prints two underline rules — one spanning FUNCTION (x 342-541) and one spanning CONVERT (x 567-749) — pixel-aligned to those two word boxes, with no rule under the operands. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 34553 renders this as '<u>FUNCTION</u> CONVERT ( argument-1 source-format destination-format )', dropping the underline on CONVERT only. Because the line does mark FUNCTION, the omission is not a neutral loss of formatting but an affirmative miscategorization: under 5.2.2/5.2.3 (NOT 5.2.6.2, which is 'Brackets' — the claim's citation is wrong) an unmarked uppercase word in a general format is an optional word, so the transcription implies 'FUNCTION ( argument-1 … )' is a legal CONVERT reference. Within the file's inline underline-marked family of function general formats (18 lines), 16 correctly use '<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>NAME</u>' and only CONVERT (34553) and TEST-NUMVAL (38293) are defective; the second instance was independently verified against printed page 956, where both FUNCTION and TEST-NUMVAL are underlined. Fix: wrap both function names in <u>…</u>. Note that a larger set of clause-15 general formats is transcribed inside code fences with no underline markup at all (e.g. 'FUNCTION ACOS ( argument-1 )' at line 34065); that is a separate, uniform transcription-style gap, not part of this defect.

### p924 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 15.67.4   Returned value rules  — printed in the same subclause style as 15.67.1 General / 15.67.2 General format / 15.67.3 Argument rules (all fourth-level subclauses of 15.67 NUMVAL function)
- **Markdown:** ## 15.67.4 Returned value rules   (line 37005 of specs/ISO_COBOL.md — H2), whereas the siblings are '### 15.67.1 General' (36946), '### 15.67.2 General format' (36955) and '### 15.67.3 Argument rules' (36960) — H3
- **Why:** The heading level makes 15.67.4 a peer of the '## 15.67 NUMVAL function' clause instead of one of its subclauses, so the returned-value rules detach from the NUMVAL function in every TOC/outline/anchor-walk built from the markdown. The same defect recurs for 15.68.4 and 15.69.4, so it is a pattern, not a one-off.
- **Repair:** Change '## 15.67.4 Returned value rules' to '### 15.67.4 Returned value rules' (and sweep the sibling occurrences at lines 37121 for 15.68.4 and 37189 for 15.69.4).
- **Verifier:** REAL, with three corrections to the description. (a) The kind label 'missing-heading' is a misnomer — nothing is missing. The heading '15.67.4 Returned value rules' is present at specs/ISO_COBOL.md:37005 with its anchor '<a id="section-15-67-4"></a>' and both of its rules intact. The defect is a heading-LEVEL / nesting error: H2 where the sibling subclauses and the file's own convention use H3, which makes 15.67.4 a peer of '## 15.67 NUMVAL function' (line 36943) rather than its child. Re-file as kind=heading-level. (b) The claim's typographic evidence is wrong even though its conclusion is right. Rendering page 925 shows that in this PDF the clause heading '15.68  NUMVAL-C function' and the subclause headings '15.68.1   General' / '15.68.2   General format' / '15.68.3   Argument rules' are set in the SAME bold serif face at the SAME size, distinguished only by the number depth and the wider tab after the number. So 'printed in the same subclause style as 15.67.1/2/3' cannot by itself establish the level — the printed face is equally consistent with a clause heading. What actually establishes 15.67.4 as a subclause of 15.67 is ISO's clause NUMBERING (a three-part number under a two-part parent), not the typeface. The finding should cite the numbering, not the style, or a reviewer can legitimately knock it down. (c) The pattern is four times wider than stated. It is not just 15.67.4 / 15.68.4 / 15.69.4; twelve '15.x.y' headings are H2 against 364 that are H3 — lines 33440 (15.3.1), 33516 (15.3.2), 33531 (15.3.3), 33664 (15.4.1), 33736 (15.5.3), 33741 (15.5.4), 33746 (15.5.5), 34600 (15.19.3), 34623 (15.19.4), 37005 (15.67.4), 37121 (15.68.4), 37189 (15.69.4). Every one has an H2 parent clause, so all twelve detach identically; 15.5.3-15.5.5 and 15.19.3-15.19.4 are especially clear because their own immediate siblings 15.5.1/15.5.2 and 15.19.1/15.19.2 are correctly H3. Fix all twelve in one pass. Two adjacent observations found while verifying, out of scope for this page but worth logging separately: line 37189 reads '15.69.4 Returned values rules' (plural 'values'), and clause 15's own top level is inconsistent — '## 15 Intrinsic functions' (33331) and '## 15.4 Returned values' (33659) are both H2 while '### 15.3 Arguments' (33366) is H3.

### p927 · missing-heading · **structural**

- **PDF:** 15.68.4   Returned value rules  — printed in the same subclause style as 15.68.1 General / 15.68.2 General format / 15.68.3 Argument rules (all fourth-level subclauses of 15.68 NUMVAL-C function)
- **Markdown:** ## 15.68.4 Returned value rules   (line 37121 of specs/ISO_COBOL.md — H2), whereas '### 15.68.1 General' (37021), '### 15.68.2 General format' (37028) and '### 15.68.3 Argument rules' (37036) are H3
- **Why:** Same as 15.67.4: the H2 promotes a subclause to clause level, breaking the 15.68 outline and making the NUMVAL-C returned-value rules look like a separate top-level clause.
- **Repair:** Change '## 15.68.4 Returned value rules' to '### 15.68.4 Returned value rules'.
- **Verifier:** Real, but the kind label 'missing-heading' is wrong and should be 'heading-level' (or 'wrong-heading-depth'). Nothing is missing: the heading '15.68.4 Returned value rules' is present at line 37121 with its number and title transcribed verbatim. The defect is its depth — it is emitted as H2 ('## 15.68.4 Returned value rules') while its three siblings in the same clause are H3 (15.68.1 at 37021, 15.68.2 at 37028, 15.68.3 at 37036) and its parent 15.68 is itself H2 at 37018. The fix is a one-character change, '##' -> '###', at line 37121. Note the PDF prints clause and subclause headings in effectively the same bold body-size style, so the level is established by the three-component section number rather than by typography — the claim's 'printed in the same subclause style as 15.68.1' overstates what the glyphs alone prove, though the conclusion it draws is correct. This is one instance of a small pattern: 15.67.4 (line 37005) and 15.69.4 (line 37189) carry the identical H2 defect, and all three should be swept in one change set; 15.69.4 additionally reads 'Returned values rules', which needs separate checking against its own page. Impact is confined to the derived outline/TOC and section-range extraction; no normative NUMVAL-C text is altered.

### p27 · misplaced-content · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** (second-level indent, continuing the "— The following were general enhancements:" list opened on page 26)
      — Inline exception handling using the exception-checking format of the PERFORM statement
      ...
      — Type declarations may now be external items

(first-level indent, a new top-level bullet)
  — The following intrinsic functions were added or enhanced:
      — BASECONVERT function
- **Markdown:** — Inline exception handling using the exception-checking format of the PERFORM statement
...
— Type declarations may now be external items

— The following intrinsic functions were added or enhanced:
    — BASECONVERT function
- **Why:** The ten items at the top of page 27 are indented one level deeper in the PDF than "The following intrinsic functions were added or enhanced:" — they are the continuation of the "The following were general enhancements:" sub-list opened on page 26, which page 26's markdown does nest correctly. Page 27 flattens them to column zero, making them siblings of the two group headings instead of members of the general-enhancements group. The items themselves are all present, in the right order, with the right wording, so nothing normative is lost; this is a list-nesting break across a page boundary in the non-normative Foreword. Reported for completeness only — lowest priority in this batch.
- **Repair:** Indent the ten items on page 27 ("Inline exception handling" through "Type declarations may now be external items") one level, so they continue the page-26 `- — The following were general enhancements:` sub-list, leaving "The following intrinsic functions..." and "Additional compiler directives..." at the outer level.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed as described, with two additions for the fixer. (1) The mis-nesting is verifiable within page 27 alone — it does not require the page-26 context to detect — because the ten items measure left=482, identical to the BASECONVERT..TRIM sub-items further down the same page, while the two group headings measure left=409. (2) The two pages also disagree on transcription STYLE, which is the likely root cause: page 26 encodes the list with real markdown bullets ('- — heading' / '  - — child'), whereas page 27 encodes it as literal em-dash text lines with four-space indentation for children and none for parents. A fix should indent the ten items to the child level and, ideally, normalize page 27 to page 26's markdown-list style so the sub-list rejoins its parent across the page break.

### p136 · incorrect-text · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** The special-character-word table's column headings, printed once at the head of the table on page 135, are: "Word" and "Meaning". Page 136 is a continuation of that table and prints no heading row.
- **Markdown:** | Symbol | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| – | Arithmetic operator - unary minus or subtraction |
- **Why:** The continuation half of the table is given an invented heading row whose labels differ from the standard's own terms. 8.3.2.4.2 defines these entries as special character *words*; labelling the column "Symbol" instead of "Word" drifts from the spec's terminology, and "Description" replaces "Meaning". The page-135 half of the same table correctly uses `| **Word** | **Meaning** |`.
- **Repair:** Change the page-136 continuation header to `| **Word** | **Meaning** |` to match the printed headings carried over from page 135 (or merge the two halves into a single table).
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with the scope narrowed. Page 136 of the PDF is a mid-table continuation and prints no heading row; the transcription's page-136.md opens the continuation with a synthesized header `| Symbol | Description |`. The existence of a header row is NOT the defect — a GFM table cannot omit one, so a page-per-page transcription must synthesize a header for any table split across a page boundary; that is legitimate page-break furniture. The defect is the label text: "Symbol" and "Description" are words that appear nowhere in the standard for this table, and they contradict the standard's own bold column headings for the same two columns, printed on page 135 and transcribed correctly there as `| **Word** | **Meaning** |`. "Symbol" is additionally the wrong term of art — 8.3.2.4.2 classes these entries as special character *words*, a kind of reserved word. Correct fix: change page-136.md's synthesized header to `| **Word** | **Meaning** |` so the continuation matches the standard's own labels. The table body is otherwise faithful — all 15 entries are present, in order, with the "=" entry's wrapped second line correctly joined. Severity cosmetic; no normative content is affected.

### p205 · other · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** Table 3 — Combinations of symbols in arithmetic expressions: header 'First symbol' in the corner, 'Second symbol' spanning, then the five column headers 'Identifier or literal', '+ – * / **', 'Unary + or –', '(' and ')'.
- **Markdown:** | First symbol | Second symbol | | | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| | **Identifier or literal** | **+ – * / \*\*** | **Unary + or –** | **(** | **)** |
- **Why:** The header/delimiter rows declare 5 columns but the sub-header row supplies 6 cells. Under CommonMark/GFM table rules cells beyond the declared column count are discarded, so the ')' column header disappears from the rendered table, and the continuation rows on page 206 (which are written as a separate 6-column table) no longer line up with this header.
- **Repair:** Make the whole of Table 3 one 6-column table: header '| First symbol | Second symbol | | | | |' (6 cells) with a 6-column delimiter row, keep the sub-header row as the second header line, and join the page-206 body rows to the same table.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed, with two refinements to the original write-up. (1) The mechanism is precisely as claimed and was reproduced: header row = 5 cells, delimiter row = 5 cells, sub-header row = 6 cells; markdown-it renders a 5-column table ending at '<td><strong>(</strong></td>', dropping '**)**' entirely. The transcription should declare SIX columns (delimiter '|---|---|---|---|---|---|'), with the header row carrying the stub 'First symbol' plus the five 'Second symbol' sub-headers, and the 'Second symbol' spanner recorded as a note or an explicit second header line, since markdown tables cannot express the PDF's two-level spanning header. (2) The page-206 half has an additional, distinct defect the claim only gestures at: E:/Temp/specrecon/v206/page-206.md lines 6-7 make the FIRST data row of the continuation ('| Identifier or literal | — | P | — | — | P |') serve as that table's header row, so besides being orphaned from the page-205 header, the 'Identifier or literal' row is rendered as column headings rather than as data. The two fragments should be reunited into one 6-column table spanning the page boundary. The normative content — the P / — permissibility matrix itself — is intact and unaltered on both pages, so 'cosmetic' remains the right severity.

### p209 · misplaced-content · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** Items 'c)', 'd)' and 'e)' are indented one level, subordinate to item '2) When the value of operand-2 is greater than zero, the results shall be determined as follows:' (begun on page 208); items '3)' and '4)' then return to the outer margin.
- **Markdown:** c) When operand-2 is an integer and the following condition is true
...
e) Otherwise, the equivalent arithmetic expression is defined by the implementor. ...

3) When operand-2 is less than zero, ...

4) When both operand-1 and operand-2 are equal to zero, ...   (c/d/e written flush left, at the same indentation as 3) and 4))
- **Why:** In 8.8.1.4.4 the a)–e) cases apply only when operand-2 is greater than zero, whereas 3) and 4) are independent rules. Page 208 does indent a) and b) three spaces under 2); losing the indentation for c)–e) at the page break detaches them from their parent and makes them look like peers of 3) and 4).
- **Repair:** Indent the c), d), e) blocks (and their embedded expression lines) three spaces, matching the a)/b) items on page 208, so the sub-list stays attached to item 2).
- **Verifier:** The claim is accurate as written and needs no correction of fact; two refinements are worth recording.

SCOPE IS WIDER THAN THE THREE MARKERS. The flattening is not limited to the 'c)', 'd)', 'e)' labels -- every subordinate display line inside c) and d) is flattened to column 0 as well: '(operand-2 = 3)', 'the equivalent expression shall be', '((operand-1 * operand-1) * operand-1)', '(operand-2 = 4)', 'the equivalent expression shall be', '((operand-1 * operand-1) * (operand-1 * operand-1))' (ISO_COBOL.md lines 9085-9097). In the PDF these sit at left=629 and left=554 versus the c)/d) marker at left=409, i.e. one and two further levels in; page 208 correctly renders the a)/b) equivalents at 6 spaces. The fix is to indent lines 9083-9099 by 3 spaces (markers) and 6 spaces (display/body sub-lines) to match the established page-208 convention, restoring the a)-e) run to a single consistent level.

SEVERITY SHOULD BE RAISED FROM COSMETIC TO STRUCTURAL/SEMANTIC. Losing the level does not merely look wrong; it reparents c)-e) out of the 'operand-2 greater than zero' branch of 8.8.1.4.4 and makes e)'s 'Otherwise' fallback appear to govern the negative-exponent and 0**0 cases that 3) and 4) define explicitly. Any rule extraction driven off the markdown would mis-derive exponentiation semantics.

RELATED BUT SEPARATE, NOT A CONTENT LOSS: the PDF's first physical line on page 209 is the display expression '(operand-1 * operand-1)' (y=388, left=629), the equivalent expression for item b). The markdown does not carry it on page 209; it was merged onto the tail of page 208 line 9076 as 'the equivalent expression shall be (operand-1 * operand-1)'. The text therefore survives and no content is lost -- only the display-line break and the page assignment differ -- so this does not constitute a separate discrepancy, but it is the same page-break handling weakness that produced the indentation loss and should be fixed alongside it.

### p231 · incorrect-figure · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** 8.8.4.10.2 General format --- the figure prints 'NOT condition-1' with NOT UNDERLINED (verified by cropping the 300 dpi render at x=320..720, y=620..700: a clear rule sits under N-O-T and stops before 'condition-1').
- **Markdown:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md:10048 --- 'NOT condition-1' (plain text; no <u> markup, no other required-word marker).
- **Why:** Underlining is the standard's marker for a REQUIRED word (5.2.6). The transcription already has a convention for it and uses it correctly two figures earlier on page 229 ('data-name-1 IS [ <u>NOT</u> ] <u>OMITTED</u>'), so the marker was silently dropped here rather than deliberately omitted. A reader or generator that treats unmarked figure words as optional/user-supplied would mis-classify the negated-condition keyword.
- **Repair:** Change specs/ISO_COBOL.md:10048 to '<u>NOT</u> condition-1' to match the p229 convention.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED with two corrections to the framing. (a) Clause citation: the marker's meaning is fixed by 5.2.2 Keywords ('uppercase and underlined ... required') and 5.2.3 Optional words ('uppercase and NOT underlined ... may be written'), not by 5.2.6 (Options / brackets and braces). (b) Scope: this is NOT a page-231-local slip. A sweep of all 281 'General format' sections shows 154 with non-fenced bodies, of which 92 carry no underline markup at all — including siblings that print underlines in the PDF, e.g. 7.3.8.4.2 L3878 'compilation-variable-name-1 IS [ NOT ] DEFINED' and most of the 15.x FUNCTION formats. So p231 L10048 is one instance of a document-wide systemic loss of the keyword underline, and any fix should be swept across that population rather than patched at this line. What IS locally damning, and what makes the finding worth keeping at page 231, is the intra-page inconsistency: the second figure on the same page (8.8.4.11.2, L10063-L10071) preserves its underlines via a prose 'Figure notes' block, while the first figure gets neither that note nor the inline <u> markup that the same transcription applies to the identical word NOT at L9691 and L9984. Severity 'cosmetic' is right for a human reader — 8.8.4.10.1 states in prose that a condition is negated 'by use of the logical operator NOT' — but it is meaning-bearing for any mechanical consumer that derives required-vs-optional word status from the figure.

### p254 · missing-heading · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** 9.1.13.2   Successful completion  (a fourth-level subclause of 9.1.13 I-O status, peer of 9.1.13.1 General and 9.1.13.3 through 9.1.13.11)
- **Markdown:** ## 9.1.13.2 Successful completion  (whereas ISO_COBOL.md has '## 9.1.13 I-O status', '### 9.1.13.1 General', '### 9.1.13.3 ...', '### 9.1.13.4 ...', '### 9.1.13.5 ...' etc.)
- **Why:** 9.1.13.2 is emitted at the same markdown level as its own parent 9.1.13 and one level shallower than all its siblings, so a generated outline detaches it from the I-O status clause and shows the nine remaining status subclauses as children of 9.1.13.2. Heading text and number are correct; nothing normative is lost.
- **Repair:** Change '## 9.1.13.2 Successful completion' to '### 9.1.13.2 Successful completion' as part of the document-wide heading-depth normalization.
- **Verifier:** kind is mislabeled: nothing is missing. The heading '9.1.13.2 Successful completion' IS present in ISO_COBOL.md (line 11290) with correct number, correct text, and a correct '#section-9-1-13-2' anchor. The real defect is a WRONG HEADING LEVEL (kind should be 'heading-level' / wrong nesting depth): it is emitted as '##', the same depth as its own parent '## 9.1.13 I-O status', instead of '###' like its peers. Two further corrections to the claim: (a) the defect is NOT unique to 9.1.13.2 - '## 9.1.13.8 Record operation conflict condition with unsuccessful completion' (ISO_COBOL.md line 11457) has the identical wrong level, so any fix must sweep both; (b) the stated outline consequence is overstated - because 9.1.13.8 is also '##', only the FIVE subclauses 9.1.13.3 through 9.1.13.7 get misparented under 9.1.13.2, while 9.1.13.9 through 9.1.13.11 get misparented under 9.1.13.8, and 9.1.13.1 alone remains correctly under 9.1.13. Fix: change lines 11290 and 11457 from '##' to '###'. No normative content is affected.

### p258 · missing-heading · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** 9.1.13.8   Record operation conflict condition with unsuccessful completion  (a fourth-level subclause of 9.1.13, peer of 9.1.13.7 and 9.1.13.9)
- **Markdown:** ## 9.1.13.8 Record operation conflict condition with unsuccessful completion  (whereas its immediate neighbours read '### 9.1.13.7 Logic error condition with unsuccessful completion' and '### 9.1.13.9 File sharing conflict condition with unsuccessful completion')
- **Why:** Same outline break as 9.1.13.2: the heading is one level shallower than its siblings, so 9.1.13.9, 9.1.13.10 and 9.1.13.11 become children of 9.1.13.8 in any generated TOC. No normative content is affected.
- **Repair:** Change '## 9.1.13.8 ...' to '### 9.1.13.8 ...' as part of the document-wide heading-depth normalization.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed, with two refinements to the description. First, the defect is a heading-LEVEL error only, not missing text: the heading's wording is present and verbatim-correct in the markdown ('## 9.1.13.8 Record operation conflict condition with unsuccessful completion', specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 11457), so the kind is better characterized as 'heading-level' than 'missing-heading'. Second, the structural damage is slightly worse than stated: because the parent clause 9.1.13 is itself transcribed as '## 9.1.13 I-O status', giving 9.1.13.8 a '##' does not merely make it shallower than its siblings - it detaches it from its own parent and emits it as a SIBLING of 9.1.13, on par with 9.1.12 and 9.1.14. The three following subclauses 9.1.13.9, 9.1.13.10 and 9.1.13.11 (all correctly '###') then nest under 9.1.13.8 instead of under 9.1.13, exactly as claimed. Independently verified against the printed page: PDF page 258 (folio 228) sets '9.1.13.8   Record operation conflict condition with unsuccessful completion' in the same bold serif, same point size and same left margin as '9.1.13.7   Logic error condition with unsuccessful completion' on page 257 (folio 227) and '9.1.13.9   File sharing conflict condition with unsuccessful completion' on page 259 (folio 229) - there is no typographic basis in the source for the promotion. The fix is a one-character change of '##' to '###' on line 11457; the identical fix is needed on line 11290 for 9.1.13.2, which is the only other member of the 9.1.13.1-9.1.13.11 run with the same defect. No normative content is affected.

### p264 · missing-heading · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** 9.1.18.5   Run unit termination ... 9.1.18.6   Object orientation ... 9.1.18.7   Exception conditions  (fourth-level subclauses of 9.1.18 Commit and Rollback, peers of 9.1.18.1 through 9.1.18.4)
- **Markdown:** ## 9.1.18.5 Run unit termination ... ## 9.1.18.6 Object orientation ... ## 9.1.18.7 Exception conditions  (whereas ISO_COBOL.md has '## 9.1.18 Commit and Rollback', '### 9.1.18.1 General', '### 9.1.18.2 Files and data items to be included', '### 9.1.18.3 Committing changes to files', '### 9.1.18.4 Rolling back changes to files and data items')
- **Why:** The last three subclauses of the Commit and Rollback clause are emitted at the same level as the clause itself, so an outline shows 9.1.18 as containing only .1 to .4 and lists .5, .6, .7 as sibling clauses. Heading text, numbers and anchors are correct; nothing normative is lost.
- **Repair:** Change the three '## 9.1.18.x' headings to '### 9.1.18.x' as part of the document-wide heading-depth normalization.
- **Verifier:** Real, but the 'missing-heading' label is wrong -- no heading is missing. All three headings (9.1.18.5 Run unit termination, 9.1.18.6 Object orientation, 9.1.18.7 Exception conditions) are present in specs/ISO_COBOL.md with correct text, correct clause numbers and correct anchors; their following paragraphs are complete and verbatim. The actual defect is a heading-LEVEL error (kind should be 'heading-level' / wrong nesting depth): lines 11633, 11638 and 11643 use '##' where their four sibling subclauses at lines 11605-11620 use '###' under the '## 9.1.18 Commit and Rollback' parent at line 11602, so the three tail subclauses become peers of their own parent and of '## 9.1.19 Sort file'. The trigger is the page 263->264 break immediately preceding 9.1.18.5. Not a one-off: the file contains 162 four-part clause numbers emitted at level 2 against 620 at level 3, of which 58 sit within eight lines of a 'Page N' marker -- so the transcription has a recurring heading-depth reset that is only partly page-break-driven and warrants a file-wide sweep rather than a single-line patch. Severity cosmetic is correct for prose fidelity; the only functional risk is heading-scoped section extraction of 9.1.18 truncating after .4.

### p289 · incorrect-figure · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** In the interface-definition general format, INTERFACE-ID, AS, INHERITS, USING, END and INTERFACE are underlined (required words) while FROM is NOT underlined: '[ INHERITS FROM { interface-name-2 } … ]' shows an underline under INHERITS only (verified on a 300 dpi crop of the printed line).
- **Markdown:** The interface-definition figure (lines 12581-12592) is a plain fenced code block with no underline marking and — unlike the adjacent method-definition figure, which carries a '> **Figure notes (method-definition general format).** ... are underlined in the printed standard (required words); `IS` is not underlined.' blockquote — no compensating figure note: '[ INHERITS FROM { interface-name-2 } … ]'
- **Why:** Underlining is how the standard's meta-language distinguishes required words from optional words. With it dropped and no figure note, the markdown gives no way to tell that FROM in 'INHERITS FROM' is an optional word (so 'INHERITS interface-name-2' is legal) while INHERITS itself is required. The immediately following figure on the same page does carry that note, so the omission is an oversight, not a transcription policy.
- **Repair:** Add a figure-notes blockquote after the interface-definition block, on the model of the method-definition one: INTERFACE-ID, AS, INHERITS, USING, END, INTERFACE and IDENTIFICATION/DIVISION are underlined (required); FROM is not underlined (optional word).
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED with a corrected framing and an enlarged scope. PDF (verified on a ~600 dpi-equivalent magnification of the 300 dpi render): in the page-289 interface-definition general format, IDENTIFICATION, DIVISION, INTERFACE-ID, AS, INHERITS, USING, END and INTERFACE are underlined (required words); FROM carries no underline — the rule stops in the space after 'INHERITS'. Markdown: specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 12581-12592 render the figure as a plain fenced block with no underline marking and no compensating figure note, so the markdown provides no way to know that FROM is an optional word and 'INHERITS interface-name-2' is therefore legal (the standard's own examples at lines 45770/46001/46047/46420/46487/46609 write 'INHERITS Base' with no FROM, independently confirming FROM is optional). This is normative meta-language loss, not formatting: lines 2628/2641/5805 of the transcription state that non-underlined uppercase words are optional words. Two amendments to the original claim: (a) the inference that the missing note 'is an oversight, not a transcription policy' is not established by the adjacent figure alone — about half of the transcription's ~510 fenced figures carry no note, though 194 do carry explicit 'underlined in the printed standard' statements, which is what makes this a hole in a demonstrated convention; (b) the defect is a pattern of four, not one — the same uncompensated 'INHERITS FROM' figure appears at lines 12539 (class-definition, p288), 12585 (interface-definition, p289), 12817 (11.6.2's sibling 11.3.2 CLASS-ID paragraph) and 12975 (11.6.2 INTERFACE-ID paragraph). The fix must add the underline note to all four, and the two paragraph-level formats (12817, 12975) are the normative ones. Severity is better read as minor/semantic than cosmetic, since the lost bit determines whether a conforming compiler must accept 'INHERITS <name>' without FROM.

### p315 · other · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** locae-name-1   [the printed standard, verified on the 300 dpi render of the locale-phrase-1 brace group, spells it with the 'l' missing - an ISO typographical defect; the locale-phrase-2 stack below it spells 'locale-name-2' correctly, as does syntax rule 3) on the same page]
- **Markdown:** - `locale-name-1`   [silently corrected, with no note that the print is defective]
- **Why:** The corrected spelling is the right text to implement against (syntax rule 12.3.6.3 3) 'Locale-name-1 and locale-name-2 shall be locale names defined in the SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph' confirms the intent), but the transcription is supposed to record ISO's own defects rather than quietly normalize them - otherwise a later reader who renders the page sees a mismatch and cannot tell whether the markdown or the print is wrong.
- **Repair:** Keep `locale-name-1` and add a bracketed editorial note to the figure notes, e.g. '[ISO defect: the printed standard spells this locae-name-1 in the locale-phrase-1 stack; corrected here per 12.3.6.3 rule 3.]' Do NOT change the text back.
- **Verifier:** Accurate as filed; one clarification about the remedy and one about scope.

Remedy: the fix is an ANNOTATION, not a text change. The markdown should keep `locale-name-1` as the implementable spelling (12.3.6.3 rule 3 confirms the intent) and add a note to the 12.3.6.2 figure notes recording that the printed locale-phrase-1 brace group reads 'locae-name-1' (missing 'l') while the locale-phrase-2 group reads 'locale-name-2' correctly - matching the house style already used at ISO_COBOL.md:4613 and :16294. Reproducing the typo in the body text instead would be a regression, since downstream tooling and readers take the markdown as the term of reference.

Scope: the claim describes the markdown as showing '- `locale-name-1`', which is exact, but it is worth noting that the whole locale-phrase-1/-2 region is already a prose paraphrase ('A brace group containing:') rather than a reproduction of the printed brace figure - unlike the general-format figure above it, which is transcribed with box-drawing delimiters and full figure notes. So the lost typo sits inside a region whose figure fidelity is downgraded generally; the annotation should probably be attached to whichever note covers those two brace groups. This does not weaken the finding - the token still differs between the two documents by one character with no note - it only locates it.

Evidence paths: E:/Temp/specrecon/v315/spec_p315.png (300 dpi render), E:/Temp/specrecon/v315/crop_lp1.png and E:/Temp/specrecon/v315/crop_lp2.png (native-resolution crops of the two brace groups), E:/CobolSharp/specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 13751-13765 (markdown), E:/Temp/specrecon/v315/page-315.md lines 34-48 (work unit).

### p342 · incorrect-text · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** In both `ALTERNATE RECORD KEY IS` and `RECORD KEY IS`, only ALTERNATE and RECORD carry an underline; KEY and IS are NOT underlined (verified at 300 dpi on the printed glyphs).
- **Markdown:** The figure note (ISO_COBOL.md line 14770) lists `RECORD KEY` among the underlined words - "...`ORGANIZATION`, `INDEXED`, `RECORD KEY`, `RESERVE`, `SHARING`..." - and then in the very next sentence lists `KEY` among the words that are NOT underlined: "`IS`, `TO`, `MODE`, `KEY`, `WITH`, `WHEN`, `FILE`, `AREA`, `AREAS`, and `OTHER` are not underlined."
- **Why:** The figure note contradicts itself about a word whose underlining decides whether it is a required word or an optional word. A reader taking the first list at face value would treat KEY as required and reject the legal `RECORD IS data-name-5` / `ALTERNATE RECORD IS data-name-2` spellings - the falsely-restrictive direction.
- **Repair:** In the underlined list on line 14770 replace `RECORD KEY` with `RECORD`; the second sentence (KEY not underlined) is already correct and should stand.
- **Verifier:** REAL, but slightly over-stated in impact. The defect: in the page-342 figure note (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 14770), the list of words underlined in the printed standard contains the two-word entry `RECORD KEY`. The printed page shows the underline running under RECORD only - two separate rules (ALTERNATE, RECORD) in `ALTERNATE RECORD KEY IS`, one rule (RECORD) in the standalone `RECORD KEY IS` - with KEY and IS bare in both places. The entry should read `RECORD`, or simply be dropped since `RECORD` already appears twice in the same list; the following sentence's placement of `KEY` in the not-underlined list is correct and matches the PDF. Impact is bounded because the contradiction is self-flagging: the two lists disagree one sentence apart, so a careful reader is warned rather than silently misled - 'cosmetic' severity is defensible, though 'minor' is arguably the better label since the note is the only place underlining (required vs optional word) survives transcription. Fix is a one-token edit to line 14770.

### p344 · incorrect-figure · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** The three clause lines printed as plain (non-boxed) format lines carry selective underlining: `ACCESS` and `SEQUENTIAL` are underlined while MODE and IS are not; `STATUS` is underlined while FILE and IS are not; `ORGANIZATION` and `SEQUENTIAL` are underlined while IS is not; `SELECT` and `OPTIONAL` are underlined.
- **Markdown:** ISO_COBOL.md renders these with no underline markup and no compensating figure note - e.g. line 14835 `[ ACCESS MODE IS SEQUENTIAL ]`, and likewise `[ FILE STATUS IS data-name-4 ]`, `SELECT [ OPTIONAL ] file-name-1`, `[ [ ORGANIZATION IS ] SEQUENTIAL ] .` in the same page-344 slice.
- **Why:** Underlining is the standard's only marker separating required words from optional words (5.2.6.2). With it gone and no figure note to carry it, MODE, IS and FILE read as required, so legal source such as `ACCESS SEQUENTIAL` or `STATUS IS data-name-4` looks illegal. Elsewhere the transcription does preserve this - page 310 uses `<u>PROGRAM-ID</u>`, page 322 uses `<u>SYMBOLIC</u> CHARACTERS`, and every code-block figure on page 344 itself carries a figure note listing the underlined words - so these plain lines are an omission rather than a convention.
- **Repair:** Add `<u>` markup (or a one-line figure note) to the four plain-text clause lines on page 344 the same way page 310 line 32 and page 322 line 8 already do.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with three corrections to the claim as written.

(a) COUNT: it is not 'three clause lines'. Five physical lines in the page-344 slice are affected, covering four distinct clause patterns - `SELECT [ OPTIONAL ] file-name-1` appears TWICE (slice line 8 under 'Format 3 (sequential):' and slice line 65 under 'Format 4 (sort-merge):', i.e. ISO_COBOL.md lines 14823 and 14880), plus `[ ACCESS MODE IS SEQUENTIAL ]` (14835), `[ FILE STATUS IS data-name-4 ]` (14837) and `[ [ ORGANIZATION IS ] SEQUENTIAL ] .` (14892).

(b) CITATION: the underlining convention is 5.2.2 (Keywords) and 5.2.3 (Optional words), not '5.2.6.2'. 5.2.6 is 'Options', which governs the bracket/brace meta-language, a different convention. The claim's rationale is right but points at the wrong section.

(c) SCOPE OF THE ORGANIZATION ITEM: only the FORMAT 4 ORGANIZATION line (`[ [ ORGANIZATION IS ] SEQUENTIAL ] .`) is affected. Format 3's ORGANIZATION clause is inside a fenced block and IS correctly covered by its figure note, which already states that ORGANIZATION, LINE and SEQUENTIAL are underlined and RECORD and IS are not.

Exact underlining to restore, read off the 300dpi render:
  - `<u>SELECT</u> [ <u>OPTIONAL</u> ] file-name-1`  (both occurrences; file-name-1 bare)
  - `[ <u>ACCESS</u> MODE IS <u>SEQUENTIAL</u> ]`
  - `[ FILE <u>STATUS</u> IS data-name-4 ]`  (FILE deliberately NOT underlined)
  - `[ [ <u>ORGANIZATION</u> IS ] <u>SEQUENTIAL</u> ] .`
This matches byte-for-byte how ISO_COBOL.md already renders the same two constructs at lines 14784 and 15269.

Severity is understated as 'cosmetic'. Underlining is semantic in this document - it is the only marker of required vs optional words - so this is a content-fidelity defect that inverts the required/optional status of MODE, IS and FILE for any consumer of the markdown. Recommend re-classifying to minor/substantive. The fix is mechanical: add the `<u>` markup to the five lines, matching the convention already used on the neighbouring pages; no figure note is needed since these are single plain lines rather than fenced diagrams.

### p451 · ocr-error · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** 10) If more than one integer-1 or integer-2 operand is specified ... c) The occurrences of integer-1, if present, shall be in ascending numerical order. ... 12) Identifier-1 shall be described in the file, working-storage, local-storage, or linkage section as an elementary unsigned integer data item.  (all hyphens printed as the ordinary hyphen U+002D, as everywhere else in the document)
- **Markdown:** integer‑1 / integer‑2 / Identifier‑1 / working‑storage / local‑storage  (specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 19250, 19256, 19264 encode the hyphen as U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN instead of U+002D)
- **Why:** These are COBOL metavariable and section names. Encoded with U+2011 they are no longer byte-identical to 'integer-1', 'Identifier-1', 'working-storage', 'local-storage' as spelled in the surrounding text and everywhere else in the standard, so grep/citation tooling that searches for the operand names silently misses these three general rules of 13.18.35.3. The artifact is sporadic (107 occurrences on 38 lines file-wide), not a deliberate typographic convention.
- **Repair:** Replace U+2011 with U+002D on lines 19250, 19256 and 19264; the same substitution should be swept across the remaining 35 lines that carry U+2011.
- **Verifier:** Real, correctly located and correctly counted, but the evidence is stated one step too strongly and the scope is stated one step too narrowly. (a) EVIDENCE: 'the PDF prints U+002D' cannot be established by looking -- U+002D and U+2011 render as the same glyph, and the ISO text layer is obfuscated. What is establishable, and what I established, is that the PDF encodes ONE hyphen character throughout (subset glyph U+01E6: 157/157 after 'integer', 17/17 before 'storage', over a 190-page sample), and the markdown renders that one character as U+002D 47171 times and as U+2011 107 times. The defect is the transcription's inconsistency against a uniform source, not a provable 2011-for-002D substitution. (b) The strongest proof is internal, and the claim omits it: adjacent pages 450/452/453 spell the same 13.18.35 operands 'integer-1'/'integer-2' with U+002D while page 451 uses U+2011, and 8 lines carry both encodings within one line -- e.g. line 31381 'FARTHEST-FROM-ZERO' (U+002D) beside 'identifier\u201114' (U+2011), where the page-773 text layer shows the identical glyph for both. (c) SCOPE: this is not a page-451 finding. Page 451 is 3 of 38 affected lines; the repair is a single global normalization of U+2011 -> U+002D across all 107 occurrences (leaving U+2013 and U+2014 alone, which are genuine en/em dashes such as the '-' printed in GR 11 on this page), and the reconciliation ledger should carry it once at file scope rather than 13 times per page. (d) Severity cosmetic is correct: no rule's meaning changes; the impact is confined to byte-exact grep and citation tooling.

### p470 · ocr-error · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** e) If FOOTING is omitted, integer-7 will be equal to integer-6, if LAST DETAIL is specified, or otherwise equal to the page limit given by integer-1.  ...  4) A report heading or report footing on a page by itself may occupy any region of the page from HEADING integer-3 through the page limit integer-1 ...  5) If integer-2 is omitted, a value of 999 is assumed for the page width.  [all hyphens printed as ordinary U+002D HYPHEN-MINUS]
- **Markdown:** e) If FOOTING is omitted, integer‑7 will be equal to integer‑6, if LAST DETAIL is specified, or otherwise equal to the page limit given by integer‑1.  ...  4) A report heading or report footing on a page by itself may occupy any region of the page from HEADING integer‑3 through the page limit integer‑1 ...  5) If integer‑2 is omitted, a value of 999 is assumed for the page width.  [all six hyphens are U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN, decimal 8209]
- **Why:** All six 'integer-N' references on this page use U+2011 instead of the ASCII hyphen the PDF prints. The immediately preceding page (469), part of the same clause 13.18.39.4 General rules, uses ASCII hyphens for the identical terms. A grep or citation lookup for 'integer-7' therefore finds general rules 3)c) and 3)d) on page 469 but silently misses general rule 3)e) on page 470 - a retrieval hole in the one document this project treats as the authority for correct behavior. Verified by codepoint dump: the character at that position is decimal 8209.
- **Repair:** Normalize U+2011 to U+002D throughout the page-470 slice. There are 107 U+2011 characters in specs/ISO_COBOL.md overall against thousands of ASCII hyphens; sweep them all in one pass so no normative term is unreachable by exact-match search.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with two corrections to the framing.

(a) EVIDENTIARY BASIS. The claim states as fact that the PDF prints U+002D. That cannot be read off the PDF: its text layer is obfuscated, and the hyphen surfaces as the internal subset code U+01E6 on both pages. What IS directly verifiable, and what I verified, is that page 469 and page 470 use the identical glyph - same font (Cambria), same size (10.7 pt), same advance width (3.552 pt), same internal code - so the PDF sets one and the same character in both places, while the markdown spells it two different ways. That the correct spelling is ASCII follows from the document's own convention (47,171 ASCII hyphens vs 107 U+2011 document-wide; 226 ASCII 'integer-N' vs 12; pages 468/469/471 all ASCII for this same metavariable) and from the printed glyph being an ordinary short hyphen at 600 dpi. Conclusion unchanged; the reasoning should cite glyph identity plus convention rather than a codepoint read of the PDF.

(b) SCOPE - THIS IS A PATTERN, NOT A PAGE. Page 470 is 6 of 107 U+2011 instances spread over 9 pages: 417 (9), 451 (6), 470 (6), 695 (4), 723 (36), 742 (8), 743 (10), 773 (14), 868 (14). The corrupted tokens include metavariables (identifier‑14 x12, identifier‑1 x9, integer‑1 x6, literal‑1, argument‑1/2/3) and - materially worse than page 470 - actual COBOL reserved words and hyphenated terms whose spelling is load-bearing: I‑O, DATE‑TO‑YYYYMMDD, YEAR‑TO‑YYYY, file‑name, record‑key, Data‑name. On those pages a grep for a reserved word fails outright, so the sweep is worth more than the single-page repair. The fix is one global normalization of U+2011 to U+002D across specs/ISO_COBOL.md; there is no context in the document where a non-breaking hyphen is intended.

Severity: cosmetic as to meaning (no general rule changes), but functional as to retrieval - and on the sibling pages, where reserved-word spellings are corrupted, it should be graded above cosmetic.

### p507 · incorrect-figure · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** REVERSE-VIDEO   (13.18.48.2 General format - printed underlined, i.e. a required word, exactly as REQUIRED on p506 and SECURE on p510 are printed)
- **Markdown:** `REVERSE-VIDEO`   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 21445 - wrapped in code backticks, no underline)
- **Why:** The required-word underline is the load-bearing notation in every general format, and this file's own convention for it is <u>...</u> - the immediately neighbouring one-word formats use '<u>REQUIRED</u>' (line 21402) and '<u>SECURE</u>' (line 21545). Rendering this one as code span silently drops the required-word marker and breaks any mechanical scan that harvests required words from <u> tags. Meaning is recoverable here only because the format is a single word.
- **Repair:** Replace `REVERSE-VIDEO` with <u>REVERSE-VIDEO</u> to match the printed underline and the file's own convention for one-word general formats.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED as claimed, with two additions the claim did not mention.

FIRST - the file has a documented fallback for cases where <u> cannot be used: fenced format blocks are followed by a '> **Figure notes ...**' blockquote that states which words are underlined in the printed standard (see 13.18.4.2 at line 17408 and 13.18.29.2 at line 18827). Line 21445 has neither the <u> tag nor a figure note, so the omission is not covered by any existing convention in the file.

SECOND - this is a PATTERN, not a one-off. The complete set of standalone single-word general-format lines rendered as bare inline code spans instead of <u>...</u> is exactly three, and the fix should sweep all of them in one change set:
  - specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 7155  - `EXCEPTION-OBJECT` (8.4.3.6.2 General format)
  - specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 21445 - `REVERSE-VIDEO`   (13.18.48.2 General format)  <- the claimed instance
  - specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 26154 - `COMMIT`          (14.9.7.2 General forms)
Each should become <u>WORD</u> to match the 16 sibling lines already using that form.

Separately observed while surveying (NOT part of this finding, logged only so it is not lost): line 17321 'ANY LENGTH' (13.18.2.2 General format) carries no underline markup at all on either word.

### p545 · ocr-error · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** and each corresponding occurrence of the data item referenced by identifier-2.
- **Markdown:** and each corresponding occurrence of the data item referenced by identifier-**2**.
- **Why:** Stray markdown emphasis delimiters were injected around the operand ordinal. The PDF prints plain 'identifier-2' with no emphasis. Read as plain text (which is how the spec is grepped for operand names) the token becomes 'identifier-**2**', so a literal search for 'identifier-2' misses this sentence. It is a known recurring artifact in this transcription (the same pattern appears at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 32469: 'literal-**1**', 'identifier-**2**', 'character positions**.**'), so it should be swept, not fixed in isolation.
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 22821 replace 'identifier-**2**.' with 'identifier-2.', and sweep the file for the sibling pattern /(?:identifier|literal|subscript|index|data-name)-\*\*\d+\*\*/ plus stray '**.**'.
- **Verifier:** Claim is REAL and accurately quoted; only its scope was understated. Page 545 / specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 22821: the transcription emits 'identifier-**2**' where the PDF prints plain, unstyled 'identifier-2' (verified glyph-by-glyph on a 300dpi render - no bold, no italic, no emphasis anywhere in General rule 8). The stray-emphasis artifact is systematic, not a one-off pair: a sweep of the whole transcription finds 50 occurrences of the operand-ordinal form <operand>-**<digit>** across 29 lines, clustering in four contiguous runs at md pages 346 (lines 14956-14978: file-name, data-name, literal, integer), 418 (lines 17949-17967: integer-1 / integer-2, ~15 occurrences), 545 (this instance, isolated), and 801 (lines 32465-32475: identifier-1/2/4/5/7, literal-1). A related punctuation variant - emphasis wrapped around a trailing period, e.g. 'character positions**.**' - occurs 3 times, one of them on line 32469. Recommended remediation is a single mechanical sweep removing the injected '**' pairs across all 29 lines plus the 3 punctuation cases, rather than an isolated edit to line 22821. Note that only the page-545 instance was visually verified against the PDF here; the other three clusters are pattern matches on the markdown and should be spot-checked against their own renders before the sweep lands.

### p551 · ocr-error · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** the initialization of the associated data item behaves as if the value of the data item referenced by the DEPENDING phrase in the OCCURS clause specified for the variable-occurrence data item is set to the maximum number of occurrences as specified by that OCCURS clause.  [the PDF breaks the compound term across the p.551/p.552 boundary: 'variable-' ends the last line of p.551 and 'occurrence data item' opens p.552]
- **Markdown:** in the OCCURS clause specified for the variable- occurrence data item is set to the maximum number of occurrences as specified by that OCCURS clause.
- **Why:** 'variable-occurrence data item' is a defined ISO term (see 8.5.1.6 / 13.18.38 OCCURS clause). The page-boundary rejoin left a space after the intrinsic hyphen, splitting the defined term into two tokens, so a search of the spec for 'variable-occurrence data item' does not find this General rule 6 of the VALUE clause.
- **Repair:** In specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 23080 replace 'variable- occurrence data item' with 'variable-occurrence data item'; sweep for other '<word>- <word>' page-boundary rejoins of hyphenated defined terms.
- **Verifier:** REAL (cosmetic). specs/ISO_COBOL.md, page-551 block, VALUE clause General rule 6 under 13.18.63.4 FORMAT 1 (file line 23080): the cross-page rejoin of the compound term inserted a spurious space at its intrinsic hyphen, producing 'specified for the variable- occurrence data item' where the PDF prints 'variable-' as the last token of p.551 (folio 521) and 'occurrence data item' as the first tokens of p.552 (folio 522), i.e. the single term 'variable-occurrence data item'. The paragraph join across the page boundary was otherwise performed correctly. This is an isolated defect: the transcription's 6 other occurrences of the term (lines 21149, 21151, 23428, 23453, 25123, 27700) are correctly hyphenated, and no other COBOL compound term in the file exhibits the hyphen-space pattern. Impact is limited to exact-string search and token consistency — a search for 'variable-occurrence data item' misses VALUE clause GR 6; no semantic content is lost or altered. Fix: delete the space, yielding 'variable-occurrence data item'. Note that the claim's supporting citation of 8.5.1.6 is incorrect (8.5.1.6 is 'Alignment of data items in storage'); the term's normative home is the OCCURS clause, 13.18.38.

### p563 · incorrect-text · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** Table 12 — Procedural statements, DELETE row. The 'Conditional phrase' cell prints TWO stacked lines: '[NOT] INVALID KEY' on the first line and '[NOT] ON EXCEPTION' on the second line. Explicit scope terminator: 'END-DELETE'.
- **Markdown:** | DELETE | [NOT] INVALID KEY [NOT] ON EXCEPTION | END-DELETE |
- **Why:** The two stacked entries are independent alternative conditional phrases of the DELETE statement; run together on one line they read as a single compound phrase '[NOT] INVALID KEY [NOT] ON EXCEPTION'. The very same table uses '<br>' to preserve the stacking for every other multi-valued cell (IF -> 'THEN<br>ELSE', READ -> '[NOT] AT END<br>[NOT] INVALID KEY', SEARCH -> 'WHEN<br>AT END'), so this row is inconsistent with its own table. Table 12 is the normative source for which statements have conditional phrases and explicit scope terminators.
- **Repair:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 23558: change to '| DELETE | [NOT] INVALID KEY<br>[NOT] ON EXCEPTION | END-DELETE |'
- **Verifier:** Accurate as filed. One amendment, for completeness rather than correction: the defect is a two-instance pattern, not a lone row. The identical failure occurs in the final continuation of Table 12 on page 565, where the PDF stacks '[NOT] INVALID KEY' over '[NOT] END-OF-PAGE' in the WRITE row (verified against a 300dpi render) but specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 23625 emits '| WRITE | [NOT] INVALID KEY [NOT] END-OF-PAGE | END-WRITE |'. Table 12 has five multi-entry conditional-phrase cells in total; the three on page 564 (IF, READ, SEARCH) carry '<br>' correctly, and both cells that straddle the other two pages (DELETE on 563, WRITE on 565) lost it. The fix for page 563 is '| DELETE | [NOT] INVALID KEY<br>[NOT] ON EXCEPTION | END-DELETE |', and the sibling on page 565 should be repaired in the same change set.

### p565 · incorrect-text · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** Table 12 — Procedural statements (Continued), WRITE row. The 'Conditional phrase' cell prints TWO stacked lines: '[NOT] INVALID KEY' on the first line and '[NOT] END-OF-PAGE' on the second line. Explicit scope terminator: 'END-WRITE'.
- **Markdown:** | WRITE | [NOT] INVALID KEY [NOT] END-OF-PAGE | END-WRITE |
- **Why:** Same defect class as the DELETE row on page 563. '[NOT] INVALID KEY' and '[NOT] END-OF-PAGE' are mutually exclusive alternative conditional phrases of WRITE (INVALID KEY applies to relative/indexed files, END-OF-PAGE to LINAGE files); joined on one line they read as a single compound phrase. Sibling rows in the same table (IF, READ, SEARCH) correctly use '<br>'.
- **Repair:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 23622: change to '| WRITE | [NOT] INVALID KEY<br>[NOT] END-OF-PAGE | END-WRITE |'
- **Verifier:** Accurate as filed; one detail worth recording for the fix. Page 565 (printed folio 535), Table 12 — Procedural statements (Continued), WRITE row: the PDF prints the Conditional-phrase cell as two hard-broken lines, '[NOT] INVALID KEY' over '[NOT] END-OF-PAGE', with 'END-WRITE' as the explicit scope terminator. specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 23625 flattens them to '| WRITE | [NOT] INVALID KEY [NOT] END-OF-PAGE | END-WRITE |', losing the item separator. The break is demonstrably semantic rather than word-wrap: the cell affords ~545-570 px of line width and '[NOT] INVALID KEY [NOT]' occupies only ~500 px, so a wrapping engine would have pulled the second '[NOT]' up to line 1; the PDF instead breaks ~125 px early, at the phrase boundary. Fix: '| WRITE | [NOT] INVALID KEY<br>[NOT] END-OF-PAGE | END-WRITE |', matching the '<br>' convention already used by the IF (23582), READ (23593) and SEARCH (23600) rows of the same table. The DELETE row (23561) carries the identical defect and should be swept in the same change.

### p580 · incorrect-text · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** 14.6.13.1.4 item 3): '... If execution of the declarative completes normally, execution continues as specified in the statement for normal execution. If a conditional phrase with a NOT phrase is specified in the interrupted statement, the imperative-statement in that phrase is not executed.'
- **Markdown:** '... If execution of the declarative completes normally, execution continues as specified in the rules for normal execution. If a conditional phrase with a NOT phrase is specified in the interrupted statement, the imperative-statement in that phrase is not executed.'
- **Why:** Altered wording in a normative general rule: the standard points at 'the statement' (the interrupted statement itself), the transcription at 'the rules'. The practical effect is the same, but the transcription is no longer quotable verbatim as ISO text, and rule 1 of this project requires citing the spec exactly.
- **Repair:** In the page-580 slice, item 3) of 14.6.13.1.4, change 'as specified in the rules for normal execution' to 'as specified in the statement for normal execution'.
- **Verifier:** Claim confirmed as stated; only two refinements. (a) Location: page 580 of the PDF is printed page 550; the text is 14.6.13.1.4 'Nonfatal exception conditions', numbered item 3), and the affected line in the transcription is specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 24248. (b) Mechanism and framing: the substitution is not a random OCR slip but a normalization toward the sibling items — 1), 2) and 4) of the same list all read 'as specified in the rules for ...', while item 3) alone prints 'as specified in the statement for normal execution'. ISO's outlier wording ('the statement', arguably a drafting inconsistency on ISO's part) was regularized away. The characterization that the standard 'points at the interrupted statement itself' is an interpretation, not something the printed text says; the printed text merely reads 'the statement'. Fix: restore 'the statement' in item 3) of 14.6.13.1.4 and, if the reconciliation ledger tracks ISO's own defects, note the outlier so it is not re-normalized by a future pass.

### p591 · missing-heading · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** 14.7.5   SIZE ERROR phrase and size error condition   [same clause depth as 14.7.1 General, 14.7.4 ROUNDED phrase, 14.7.6 CORRESPONDING phrase]
- **Markdown:** ### 14.7.5 SIZE ERROR phrase and size error condition   (specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 24682)
- **Why:** Every sibling clause in the run is transcribed at '##' - 14.7 (24595), 14.7.1 (24598), 14.7.2 (24610), 14.7.3 (24615), 14.7.4 (24620), 14.7.6 (24754), 14.7.7 (24790), 14.7.8 (24846), 14.7.9 (24874). 14.7.5 alone is at '###', so it is nested under 14.7.4 in any TOC or heading-depth walk instead of being its own clause, which misplaces the SIZE ERROR clause relative to ROUNDED.
- **Repair:** Demote the marker from '###' to '##' so 14.7.5 sits at the same heading depth as its nine siblings.
- **Verifier:** specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 24685 transcribes clause 14.7.5 as '### 14.7.5 SIZE ERROR phrase and size error condition' when it should be '## 14.7.5 ...'. The heading text is present and byte-for-byte faithful to the PDF (page 591 / printed 561); nothing is missing. The defect is the heading DEPTH: ISO renders all clause headings in one typeface, so depth is carried by the dotted number alone, and '14.7.5' is a three-component number and hence a sibling of '14.7.4 ROUNDED phrase', whose own subclauses are numbered 14.7.4.1/.2/.3. Because the markdown gives 14.7.5 the same '###' level as 14.7.4.1-.3, any TOC build or heading-depth walk parents the SIZE ERROR clause under the ROUNDED phrase clause. All nine siblings in the run are correctly at '##' (14.7 at 24598, 14.7.1 at 24601, 14.7.2 at 24613, 14.7.3 at 24618, 14.7.4 at 24623, 14.7.6 at 24757, 14.7.7 at 24793, 14.7.8 at 24849, 14.7.9 at 24877), so 14.7.5 is the sole outlier. Fix: change '###' to '##' on line 24685. Reclassify kind from 'missing-heading' to 'wrong-heading-level'; severity 'cosmetic' is correct since no rule text or citable clause number is altered.

### p595 · other · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** a data item described with usage binary-char, binary-short, binary-long, binary-double, float-short, float-long, or float-extended, or of a standard floating-point usage; or
- **Markdown:** — a data item described with usage binary**-**char, binary-short, binary-long, binary-double, float-short, float-long, or float-extended, or of a standard floating-point usage; or
- **Why:** Stray emphasis delimiters around the hyphen of 'binary-char' - an OCR/formatting artifact, not a transcription choice; the other six hyphenated usage names on the same line are plain. It renders as a bold hyphen and, more importantly, breaks literal grep for the reserved word 'binary-char' in this file. No meaning is altered. Both occurrences are on page 595 (specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 24808 and 24816) and are the only two in the whole file.
- **Repair:** Replace 'binary**-**char' with 'binary-char' at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 24808 and 24816.
- **Verifier:** CONFIRMED, with one correction to the line numbers. The stray emphasis delimiters are at specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 24811 and 24819, not 24808 and 24816 (the page-595 anchor is at line 24801). Everything else in the claim holds: the PDF prints 'binary-char' in plain roman with an ordinary hyphen identical to its six siblings on the same line, no bold appears anywhere in the page body, and these are the only two 'X**-**Y' occurrences in the entire file. Worth recording alongside it: the sentence really is hyphenated across a line break in the PDF, but at 'float-/short', which the transcription correctly rejoins - so the '**' pair is not a mis-handled line-break hyphen, it is unrelated conversion noise. Fix is to delete the four '*' characters on each of lines 24811 and 24819, restoring 'binary-char'.

### p640 · other · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** DISPLAY Format 2 prints the AT phrase with choice-indicator bars inside its brace and the exception phrase with choice-indicator bars inside its bracket (both verified on the enlarged crop).
