- **Markdown:** > **Figure notes -- choice indicators (ISO 5.2.6.4).** Two groups in this format carry choice-indicator bars, both lost in transcription.  -- while the code block directly above it DOES render them: '⎧|                ⎧ identifier-2 ⎫             |⎫' and '⎡|  ON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-1      |⎤'
- **Why:** The figure note contradicts the diagram it annotates: it declares the bars 'lost in transcription' when the accompanying ASCII diagram correctly preserves them. A later reader (or a repair pass) may believe this figure is still defective and 'fix' an already-correct diagram, or conversely conclude the bars in the diagram are spurious. This is the only note in the batch that describes the transcription's own history rather than the printed standard.
- **Repair:** Reword to describe the standard, not the transcription: 'Two groups in this format carry choice-indicator bars, both preserved in the diagram above.' (or simply drop the clause 'both lost in transcription').
- **Verifier:** Real, but narrower than 'a PDF-vs-markdown fidelity defect'. The transcription of the standard is CORRECT and COMPLETE: page 640 / section 14.9.11.2 Format 2 prints choice-indicator bars inside the AT brace and inside the exception bracket, and the markdown code block reproduces both groups as '|'. The defect is confined to one clause of the transcriber's annotation directly beneath the diagram, which says the bars were 'both lost in transcription' -- a stale, self-contradictory statement about the markdown itself, not a misstatement of ISO. Fix is a single clause: replace 'both lost in transcription' with a statement of what was verified and preserved (e.g. 'both verified on the rendered page and preserved above as "|"'), matching the house style used at ISO_COBOL.md lines 16242, 19860 and 26850. The remainder of the note -- the (1) braces = one-or-more-any-order and (2) brackets = zero-or-more-any-order readings, and the underlined-required-words list -- is accurate against the PDF and must be left intact. No change to the ASCII diagram is warranted; the risk this finding guards against is precisely that someone edits the already-correct diagram.

### p712 · incorrect-figure · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** The two trailing bracketed lines of PERFORM Format 3 are printed as `[ WHEN OTHER EXCEPTION  imperative-statement-3 ]` and `[ WHEN COMMON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-4 ]`. Verified at 900 dpi: on the OTHER line an underline runs under WHEN and under OTHER; on the COMMON line the underline runs under COMMON only — the word WHEN on the `WHEN COMMON EXCEPTION` line has NO underline beneath it. This is an inconsistency in the printed standard itself, since WHEN is required in both alternatives.
- **Markdown:** Figure note: "`PERFORM`, `LOCATION`, `WHEN`, `EXCEPTION`, `INPUT`, `OUTPUT`, `IO`, `EXTEND`, `FILE`, `OTHER`, `COMMON`, `FINALLY`, and `END-PERFORM` are underlined in the printed standard (required words); `WITH` is printed without an underline, as is the second `EXCEPTION` in each of the `WHEN OTHER EXCEPTION` / `WHEN COMMON EXCEPTION` lines."
- **Why:** The note is otherwise a careful, verified record of this figure's typography — it even captures the un-underlined second EXCEPTION — but it flattens `WHEN` to "underlined" and so silently loses an ISO printing defect that a later reader re-checking the page will trip over and may mistake for a transcription error. The project's practice is to record ISO's own defects rather than normalize them away. No COBOL semantics change: WHEN is required in the COMMON alternative regardless.
- **Repair:** Extend the figure note with a sentence such as: "NOTE (defect in the printed standard): on the `[ WHEN COMMON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-4 ]` line the word WHEN is printed WITHOUT its underline, inconsistently with the `WHEN OTHER EXCEPTION` line above it and with the WHEN inside the main brace group. WHEN is a required word in all three places."
- **Verifier:** Accurate as filed; one detail worth adding for precision. On page 712 the PERFORM Format 3 diagram prints WHEN three times: in the brace group (underlined — rule y=553.55, x 76.99..100.57), on the `[ WHEN OTHER EXCEPTION imperative-statement-3 ]` line (underlined — rule y=634.55, x 75.36..98.93), and on the `[ WHEN COMMON EXCEPTION imperative-statement-4 ]` line, where there is NO underline: the only rule on that line is y=648.79, x 103.60..138.62, under COMMON. Confirmed both visually at 900 dpi and by the PDF's underline rects (thin filled rects, height 0.467 pt) matched against decoded word bounding boxes; the two WHENs sit at effectively identical x-extents (74.58..99.63 and 74.57..99.62), so the absence is real and not an alignment artefact. This is a defect in ISO's printing, not a semantic signal — GRs 17/18/19 all read 'if WHEN COMMON is specified' and the annex examples write `WHEN COMMON`, so WHEN is required in both alternatives. Suggested repair to the figure note at specs/ISO_COBOL.md line 29095: keep WHEN in the underlined list but append the exception, e.g. "...; `WITH` is printed without an underline, as is the second `EXCEPTION` in each of the `WHEN OTHER EXCEPTION` / `WHEN COMMON EXCEPTION` lines. Note also that `WHEN` is underlined in the brace group and on the `WHEN OTHER EXCEPTION` line but is printed WITHOUT an underline on the `WHEN COMMON EXCEPTION` line — an inconsistency in the printed standard, since WHEN is required there too (general rules 17 to 19 all read 'if WHEN COMMON is specified')."

### p801 · ocr-error · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** 'When the UNSTRING statement is initiated, the current receiving area is the data item referenced by identifier-4. Data is transferred from the data item referenced by identifier-1 to the data item referenced by identifier-4 according to the following rules:' ... 'Size is defined as number of character positions.' - all in plain roman type; no bold anywhere on the page.
- **Markdown:** '... the current receiving area is the data item referenced by identifier-**4**. Data is transferred from the data item referenced by identifier-**1** to the data item referenced by identifier-**4** according to the following rules:' ... 'Size is defined as number of character positions**.**' - and likewise identifier-**7**, literal-**1**, identifier-**2**, identifier-**5**, identifier-**6** throughout general rule 11 a)-g).
- **Why:** Spurious markdown emphasis markers were injected around the trailing digit of every operand name (and around one full stop) on this page only. Nothing in the PDF is bold. The literal file text now reads 'identifier-**4**', so a grep, anchor scan or spec-citation tool searching for 'identifier-4' misses every occurrence in UNSTRING general rule 11 - the rule that defines the whole transfer algorithm.
- **Repair:** Strip the ** pairs on this page: 'identifier-**N**' -> 'identifier-N', 'literal-**N**' -> 'literal-N', 'character positions**.**' -> 'character positions.'. Sweep neighbouring pages for the same injection pattern.
- **Verifier:** REAL, with two corrections to the description.

Confirmed: markdown emphasis markers were injected around the trailing digit of operand names in UNSTRING general rule 11 (specs/ISO_COBOL.md lines 32468-32484), and around the terminating full stop of rule 11 b) ('character positions**.**'). PDF page 801 shows no bold in any of these positions - the body text is uniform roman, verified by 300 dpi glyph inspection of both named passages. Affected operands: identifier-**4**, identifier-**1**, identifier-**7**, literal-**1**, identifier-**2**, identifier-**5**, identifier-**6**.

Correction 1 - 'on this page only' is inaccurate. The same artifact appears on two further lines elsewhere in the transcription: line 14963 (ASSIGN clause implementor-defined rules - data-name-**1**, device-name-**1**, literal-**1**) and line 22824 (VALIDATE-STATUS general rule 8 - identifier-**2**). Page 801 accounts for 7 of the 9 affected lines, so it is the densest cluster but not the sole site. A repair sweep should cover all 9 lines, not just this page.

Correction 2 - the injection is not exhaustive within rule 11. The final operand reference in subrule g) ('until either all the characters are exhausted in the data item referenced by identifier-1') is left plain. That clause falls past the page-801 bottom margin and continues on PDF page 802, which is consistent with the corruption having been introduced by a per-page conversion step rather than by a global find-and-replace. Anyone repairing this must not assume a uniform pattern across the rule.

Impact, measured rather than asserted: `grep 'identifier-4'` returns 0 hits across lines 32460-32490 even though rule 11 references identifier-4 four times, so plain-name lookups miss the definition of the UNSTRING transfer algorithm entirely. Fix is a pure deletion of the '**' pairs; no wording changes.

### p804 · incorrect-figure · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** Format 2 (reporting):  USE [ GLOBAL ] BEFORE REPORTING identifier-1  — USE, GLOBAL, BEFORE and REPORTING are all underlined (required words); only identifier-1 is not.
- **Markdown:** Format 2 (reporting):

USE [ GLOBAL ] BEFORE REPORTING identifier-1   (ISO_COBOL.md line 32562 — bare prose, no code fence, and no 'Figure notes' paragraph, unlike Formats 1, 3 and 4 on the same page which each carry one)
- **Why:** Formats 1, 3 and 4 on this page each record which words are underlined in a Figure-notes paragraph; Format 2 records nothing, so the required-word marking on BEFORE and REPORTING is simply absent. A reader or generator applying the page's own convention would infer that no word in Format 2 is required, i.e. that 'USE REPORTING identifier-1' or 'USE BEFORE identifier-1' is legal.
- **Repair:** Put Format 2 in a fenced block like its siblings and add a Figure-notes line: 'USE, GLOBAL, BEFORE and REPORTING are underlined in the printed standard (required words); identifier-1 is not. [ GLOBAL ] is optional.'
- **Verifier:** Real, but the claim under-states the scope and over-states one inference. What is actually missing is ALL FOUR required-word markers, not just two: the PDF underlines USE, GLOBAL, BEFORE and REPORTING (four discrete rules, verified glyph-by-glyph on the 300 dpi crop), and ISO_COBOL.md line 32562 records none of them. The full statement text is present and correctly ordered, and the optionality of GLOBAL survives via the brackets; the only thing lost is the keyword/optional-word distinction, whose loss reads under clause 5.2 (ISO_COBOL.md lines 2628 and 2641) as making BEFORE and REPORTING omittable optional words. Two corrections to the claim's framing: (a) 'no code fence' is not itself a defect — 57 of the 147 general-format headers in the file are transcribed unfenced, which is the file's normal treatment of short one-line formats; the defect is solely the absent underline marking. (b) The remedy is not necessarily a Figure-notes paragraph: the file's established convention for unfenced one-liners is inline <u>...</u>, so the correct repair is 'USE [ <u>GLOBAL</u> ] <u>BEFORE</u> <u>REPORTING</u> identifier-1' with <u>USE</u>, matching e.g. line 14782. Finally, this is not isolated to page 804 — the same omission exists at least at lines 14821 and 14878 (SELECT clause Formats 3 (sequential) and 4 (sort-merge), transcribed as bare 'SELECT [ OPTIONAL ] file-name-1' while the sibling Format 2 at line 14782 carries the <u> markup), so it should be fixed as a pattern sweep over unfenced one-line general formats rather than as a one-page edit. Severity 'cosmetic' is defensible for reading but understated for machine consumption: a grammar generator driven off the markdown would emit BEFORE and REPORTING as optional noise words.

### p873 · ocr-error · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** (2 + 0.7182818284590452353602874713526).   ...   (2.718281828459045235360287471352662).
- **Markdown:** (2 + 0.71828182845904523536028747135\u200b26).   ...   (2.7182818284590452353602874713\u200b52662).
- **Why:** Both normative constants in 15.27.3 (E function returned value rules 1 and 3) carry an embedded ZERO WIDTH SPACE (U+200B) inside the digit string, at the point where the PDF line was re-flowed. The character is invisible when rendered, so a human reading the markdown sees the correct value, but any mechanical use of the transcription -- grep for the constant, copy/paste into a test golden, or a script that parses the literal -- gets a string that is not a number and will not match. These are the only two U+200B characters in the entire 8000+ page transcription, so this is a localized artifact and not a transcription convention. Digit counts were verified against the rendered page: rule 1 = 31 digits after the point, rule 3 = 33 digits after the point; both match the PDF exactly once the U+200B is removed.
- **Repair:** Delete the two U+200B characters (byte offsets ~2030029 and ~2030380 in specs/ISO_COBOL.md) so the lines read exactly '(2 + 0.7182818284590452353602874713526).' and '(2.718281828459045235360287471352662).'
- **Verifier:** Finding is real; one detail of the stated rationale is wrong. The PDF does NOT re-flow either line -- both constants are printed unbroken on a single centered line, so the U+200B does not mark a PDF line break. Its origin is the extraction pipeline (a text-run boundary inside the PDF content stream, invisible in the rendering), which makes it a pure transcription artifact with no counterpart in the printed page. Also stronger than claimed: these two U+200B are not merely the only two U+200B, they are the only invisible/format-category characters (Cf/Cc, zero-width, NBSP, soft hyphen) in the entire transcription. Corrected statement: specs/ISO_COBOL.md page 873, 15.27.3 E function returned value rules 1 and 3, each embed a spurious ZERO WIDTH SPACE (U+200B) inside a normative decimal literal -- '0.71828182845904523536028747135<U+200B>26' and '2.7182818284590452353602874713<U+200B>52662'. The PDF prints '(2 + 0.7182818284590452353602874713526).' (31 decimals) and '(2.718281828459045235360287471352662).' (33 decimals), unbroken. Fix: delete both U+200B; the resulting digit strings then match the PDF exactly.

### p903 · incorrect-figure · **cosmetic**

- **PDF:** FUNCTION  LENGTH  ( argument-1 [ PHYSICAL ] )   -- with FUNCTION, LENGTH and PHYSICAL each UNDERLINED in the printed general format (5.2.6.2: an underlined word is a required word).
- **Markdown:** ```\nFUNCTION LENGTH ( argument-1 [ PHYSICAL ] )\n```   -- plain fenced code, no underline markup. The same loss occurs in every general format on this batch except 15.58.2 and 15.59.2: 15.51.2 (p905, bare paragraph text), 15.52.2 (p906, inline backticks), 15.53.2 (p907, bare paragraph text), 15.54.2 (p908, fenced code), 15.55.2 (p909, inline backticks), 15.56.2 (p910, inline backticks), 15.57.2 (p911, bare paragraph text).
- **Why:** The transcription elsewhere DOES carry the standard's underlining as <u>…</u> (97 occurrences, including 15.58.2 `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>LOWEST-ALGEBRAIC</u> ( argument-1 )` on p912 and 15.59.2 `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>MAX</u> ( { argument-1 } … )` on p914 in this very batch). Dropping it on the neighbouring formats loses the required-word marking that 5.2.6.2 makes normative, and the four different renderings used within ten pages (fenced code / inline code / bare paragraph / <u>) make the general-format blocks non-uniform and unreliable to machine-extract. No alternative-selection information is lost on these particular pages, because every uppercase word in each of these formats is underlined in the PDF; hence cosmetic rather than normative.
- **Repair:** Normalize all eight formats to the document's existing convention, e.g. p903: `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>LENGTH</u> ( argument-1 [ <u>PHYSICAL</u> ] )`; p911: `<u>FUNCTION</u> <u>LOWER-CASE</u> ( argument-1 [ <u>LOCALE</u> locale-name-1 ] )` (the latter already exists verbatim for UPPER-CASE at 15.79.2).
- **Verifier:** REAL, but the rationale needs two fixes.

(1) WRONG CITATION. The claim twice attributes the normative force of underlining to '5.2.6.2'. Section 5.2.6.2 is 'Brackets' and says nothing about underlining -- it defines the meaning of '[ ]'. The correct citations are 5.2.2 (Keywords -- 'shown in uppercase and underlined in general formats. They are required...') and 5.2.3 (Optional words -- 'shown in uppercase and not underlined in general formats'), restated normatively at 8.3.2.4.2 (Required words) and 8.3.2.4.3 (Optional words). Any ledger entry or fix derived from this finding must cite 5.2.2 / 5.2.3 (and 8.3.2.4.2 / 8.3.2.4.3), not 5.2.6.2.

(2) MISCHARACTERISED CONVENTION, AND THE SCOPE IS WIDER THAN CLAIMED. The claim frames inline <u>...</u> as the transcription's established convention that p903 fails to follow. It is not the dominant device. The transcription's principal mechanism for preserving underline information is a prose 'Figure notes' block: 225 such blocks exist, 213 of which explicitly enumerate which words are underlined and which are not -- and they are careful enough to flag ISO's own typesetting defects (e.g. the LISTING directive note records that 'ON' is printed without an underline although the general rules treat it as specifiable). Inline <u> is the secondary device at 97 lines / 156 tags. Measured against 360 'General format' headings in the file, the majority of general formats carry NEITHER device, and only 6 Figure-notes blocks fall in the clause-15 intrinsic-function range at all. So there are five renderings in circulation, not four (fenced code / inline code / bare paragraph / <u> / figure-note prose), and the non-uniformity is a document-wide property of the transcription rather than an anomaly local to pages 903-914. The p903 entry should be treated as one instance of a systemic class, and any remediation should pick ONE canonical representation for general formats and apply it globally.

(3) SEVERITY CONFIRMED, WITH A CAVEAT ON THE CLASS. 'Cosmetic' is right for p903 and for this batch: I verified on p903 (FUNCTION, LENGTH, PHYSICAL) and p911 (FUNCTION, LOWER-CASE, LOCALE) that every uppercase word in these formats is underlined, so no required/optional distinction is destroyed here. But the defect class is NORMATIVE, not cosmetic, wherever a printed format mixes underlined and non-underlined uppercase words -- the transcription's own figure notes document exactly such cases (SOURCE FORMAT IS FIXED, where FORMAT and IS are not underlined; the FLAG-02, LISTING, PROPAGATE, TURN and figurative-constant formats). In a plain-text rendering of those, an omissible noise word is indistinguishable from a required keyword, which would directly mislead a compiler implementation. p903 simply happens not to be such a case.
